# Canonical facts for the 10/12–13 meeting (single source of truth for figures and text)

Rules for anyone building figures or text from this file:
- Use only numbers that appear here or in the raw files named next to them. If a number is not here, read it from the named raw file; never estimate or invent.
- Every figure states its data source in a caption/footer, the prompt set (12-prompt × 3-seed, or 43-prompt × 2-seed "bench-43", or jar/castle), and whether fps was measured "under load" (7 GPU jobs running concurrently, ≈ 10–20 % slower) or "quiet".
- Tables/figure text in English. Metric direction always marked (↑ / ↓).
- GPU for all timings: NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server Edition (96 GB), one GPU unless stated. NOT an H100.
- Paths are relative to /home/hk28965/specwm.

## 0. Setup
- Models: LingBot-World v2 causal-fast (Robbyant). Teacher = 14B, 4 denoising steps (official schedule: scheduler indices 0/250/500/750 = timesteps 999/937/833/624, σ 1.0/0.937/0.833/0.625; the model predicts x0 at every node). Student = official 1.3B causal-fast, same 4-step schedule.
- Officially supported sizes (wan/configs SUPPORTED_SIZES): 480×832 and 720×1280 only.
- Chunk = 3 latent frames = 12 video frames = 0.75 s at 16 fps; 161-frame clips = 13 chunks (≈10 s); StEvo camera tasks use 253 frames (21 chunks).
- KV cache: 6 sink latent frames + 18 local latent frames (ring buffer).
- Inference stack used by every LingBot arm (teacher, student, SDVG --fast, ours): FP8 W8A8 linears on the 14B only, SageAttention 2 (built from source), torch.compile per block, ring KV, dedup time embedding, no_kv_cat, RoPE cache/fp32 (lingbot-world-v2/specwm/fast_patch.py).
  Measured ladder (10/3–10/4, 5 prompts, 14B, e2e per clip): S0 original 82.5 s → S1 exact patches 70.9 s → S2 + compile 66.1 s → S3 + FP8 56.5 s (4-step up to here) → S4 2 steps 41.5 s → S5 T5/VAE pipeline 30.7 s → S7 + SageAttention 27.7 s. Forward 1030 → 875 ms with S1 patches; Sage kernel 8.05 → 5.54 ms (1.45×), per forward 0.550 → 0.467 s/chunk (1.18×). Blind test (3 raters, 24 vs 24 clips): S7 not worse than original (severe-clean 9/24 vs 3/24; quality 3.78 vs 3.65).
- One 14B forward on a full 832 chunk ≈ 0.467 s (cost ∝ tokens; attention a minor share). A KV commit = one full forward.

## 1. Our method ("ours") and its variants
- ours 704 (realtime mode): cloud 14B at max_area 270336 (≈704×384; exact size follows the start image's aspect, e.g. 688×384 for easy_lake), 2 steps (indices 0/500, σ 1.0 → 0.833), fused commit + partial commit of the last latent frame; device = VAE decode + bicubic upsample only (no 1.3B). Cloud 0.761 s/chunk → 15.8 fps (measured under load; earlier 704 fused alone 0.640 s/chunk = 18.7 fps, measured quiet on an exclusive GPU, README V3). The two timings were taken under different load, so their difference (0.12 s) is not the cost of partial commit; the quiet timing pass (§15, done 10/12) times both under one condition.
- ours 832 (quality mode): same at 832 (max_area 399360): 1.24 s/chunk → 9.7–9.8 fps.
- fused commit = keep the K/V written by the last denoising forward instead of a separate clean t=0 commit pass (saves one forward per chunk). Side effect found 10/12: chunk-boundary jumps (scene cuts at chunk boundaries in a few cases, e.g. easy_urban_rain 704 fused seed 1234 hard-cuts to another corridor at frame 128→129).
- partial commit = after the fused step, a clean t=0 pass over only the newest latent frame's tokens (≈1/3 forward; implemented via the token-subset path fast_patch.ACTIVE). Brings boundary jumps back to the separate-commit level (704: 19/432 = 4 % vs separate 15/432 = 3 %; 832 seed 1234: 0/144). "partial 2" = the same clean pass over the last 2 latent frames (17/432 = 4 %, no gain over partial 1).
- Boundary-jump metric (scripts/boundary_jump.py): consecutive-frame mean abs diff (grey, 208 px); at every chunk boundary (frame 8|9 then every 12 frames) r = d[b] / mean of the 4 neighbouring steps; report share of boundaries with r > 1.5. Control = the same r at the mid-chunk positions b+4 and b+8 (24 per clip): 0.7–3.7 % of control positions have r > 1.5 across the nine §5 arms (per-arm k/N in §5). 12 boundaries per 153-frame clip: 432 per 36-clip arm, 144 per 12-clip arm.

## 4. Low-resolution isolation (14B alone, no draft, native output; 12 prompts × 3 seeds, separate commit, 2-step)
832: 7.53 ± 0.14 / 50.8 ± 5.0 · 704: 7.22 ± 0.15 / 54.2 ± 5.0 · 576: 6.83 ± 0.14 / 56.9 ± 4.7 · 448 (448×256): 6.17 ± 0.18 / 58.2 ± 4.8 · 384 (384×224): 5.06 ± 0.25 / 66.9 ± 4.2 (quality / drift, mean ± SE over 36 clips; labels dg704_cloud … dg384_cloud in paper/1012/vlm_scores_diag.json; videos lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/fused/*/dg<res>_s*/cloud/spec.mp4). Quality falls slowly down to 576 and steeply below it; drift jumps at 384 — consistent with Guandao's 10/5 remark that < 400 px is outside the comfortable range.
Earlier partial evidence: 448 + SR flagged 73 % of chunks; people injected in 1/4 clips. More steps at 576 do not reduce drift (2/3/4 steps: 65–77 % chunks flagged vs 40–55 % at 832). Post-training at 576 failed (resolution distillation: drift 68–86 / collapse; RFT/DPO: no effect).

## 6. Decoder (README W2)
At 704: full Wan2.1 VAE 426 ms/chunk vs TAEHV (taew2_1) 12.8 ms/chunk. TAEHV costs quality −0.56 ± 0.13 (judge 448) / −1.06 ± 0.14 (judge 832), drift +7.4 / +11.1 (paired, 36 clips). At 832 the full VAE costs 631 ms/chunk (TAEHV 19 ms). With a separate device GPU the full VAE (426 ms) is faster than the cloud chunk (761 ms), so it does not lower the pipelined fps; on one shared GPU: cloud + VAE 1.19 s → 10.1 fps; cloud + TAEHV 0.774 s → 15.5 fps.

## 7. SDVG (Hu & Zhang, arXiv 2604.17397) ported to action-conditioned LingBot
Drafter 1.3B causal-fast (4 steps), target 14B causal-fast (4 steps), verifier ImageReward worst-frame score on the VAE-decoded chunk (τ −0.7 = paper; −2.2), chunk 0 forced to the target, rejected chunks regenerated by the target from the same noise, both KV caches updated with every committed chunk.
10/5 slide (original code: bf16 eager, T5 on CPU; jar/castle × 3 seeds; paper/1005/meeting/05_sdvg_breakdown): prepare 11.3, draft 8.5, VAE→x^d 5.3, ImageReward 3.8, 14B regenerate 47.3, KV commit 15.0, final VAE 8.6 → total 99.9 s; 1/12 non-forced chunks accepted. SDVG generation alone 80.0 s. Target-only original, same accounting: 83.3 s total (range 82.3–84.3) = prepare 11.3 + generation 63.45 (14B denoising 50.6, KV commit 12.6) + final VAE 8.6 (breakdown.json "Target only"; corrected 10/12 from "≈ 73 s", which is not supported by the raw files).
Physics verifier attempt (10/6; exp/queue/phys_verify2_run.log): SDVG loop with a physics VLM judge (accept if 5 − #violations ≥ τ), jar/castle × 3 seeds: τ=5 accepted 2–10/13 chunks, gen 66–100 s; τ=4 accepted 11–12/13, gen 102–128 s; verify step (VAE decode of previous + candidate chunk + judge; judge-only time not logged) 32–37 s per clip at τ=5, 72–100 s at τ=4 (same 12 verify calls per clip at both τ, so the τ=4 times are suspect; not diagnosed). Original stack: no clip faster than target-only generation (62.5–64.4 s). Videos deleted 10/9 (disk); logs remain. Off-the-shelf physics scorers (Qwen2.5-VL-7B zero-shot, VideoPhy-2 autorater) saturated and could not separate arms. StEvo physics plausibility (85 tasks, 1 seed; README §G): 10/7 14B arms 38.8–48.2 %; September runs: 14B official 4-step 32.9 %, 1.3B draft 1.2 % (corrected 10/12: the 1.3B 1.2 % and the 33 % lower bound are September runs, not 10/7).
Draft vs teacher gap (10/6 measurement): draft chunks 0.63 σ from the 14B output, 7 % of tokens within ε (14B's own step 1: 0.35 σ, 36 %). 10/8 6-scene noise calibration (README §E; the baseline of the LoRA pilot, README §I2): 1.3B draft 0.607 σ / 7 % within ε, 14B's own step 1 0.308 σ / 44 %, 14B with a different noise 0.405 σ / 34 %. Per-scene LoRA pilot (jar + castle, 1,152 steps): 0.483 σ / 18 % (unseen scenes 0.476 σ / 12 %).

## 8. Other closed directions (one line each)
- Draft-then-Finish (1.3B drafts at 832, 14B one forward from σ 0.27–0.63): quality 6.2 = draft level (teacher 7.6), fused commit matched separate commit there.
- Verify + redraw with the 14B drift head (AUROC 0.88 on unseen scenes): redrawn chunks flagged again; no gain, time ×2.
- Adaptive 576→832 switch: degenerates into scene routing; gains proportional to time at 832.
- Sequence parallel 2 GPUs over PCIe: ≤1.1×. Commit offload to a second GPU: hallucinated scenes (6/6 clips).
- Learned 1.3B refiner: −0.50 quality at 704 (removed); Real-ESRGAN worse than bicubic in VLM quality.
- Resolution distillation (MSE) collapsed; RFT/DPO no effect.

## 9. Baselines facts
- Matrix-Game 2.0: Skywork, game-trained keyboard/mouse world model, 352×640, 3 denoising steps, our runs use a flash-attn→SDPA shim and no torch.compile (official repo compiles; official claim 25 fps on H100); we measured 19.6–20.5 fps. Camera paths converted to mouse 0.067×deg/frame clipped ±0.1 + WASD. Recipe on MG2 (10/10, 10 prompts, seed 1234; README §M/§N; VLM from paper/1012/vlm_scores.json with the duplicated warm-up clip *_warm excluded, corrected 10/12 from 57 / 7.2 and 72 / 5.8): 3-step 640×352 20.5 fps, drift 55.0, quality 7.1; 2-step 448×256 34.3 fps, drift 71.5, quality 5.7.
- Waypoint-1.5-1B: Overworld, 1B, 1024×512, keyboard/mouse (mouse ≈118 units per degree per call, 4 frames per call), stretches the start image to 2:1, run with torch.compile max-autotune; 25–31 fps; under-rotates on long camera sweeps. No text input.
- Matrix-Game 3.5 (5B, image + camera path + text): 0.9 fps end-to-end at 1280×704 — cloud-class, excluded from the efficient set.
- StEvo-Bench: arXiv 2603.13215 (Caltech), 225 tasks / 6 categories; our 38 camera-WM tasks + 5 easy scenes = the 43 prompts. Default judge Gemini; ours = local Qwen3-VL-32B (not comparable to the paper's leaderboard numbers).

## 11. Rollout-path distance to the teacher (6 scenes; figures f10_paths_<scene>, f10_paths_summary)
Raw: means paper/1012/fig_paths_all_<scene>.json (key mean_rel_dist); per-seed values, s.e., paired differences, SDVG draft counts paper/meeting_1012/data/f10_paths_<scene>.json and f10_paths_summary.json (recomputed on CPU from the cached reenc_*.pt latents next to each video under lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/; means match the paper/1012 JSONs to < 1e-6); script paper/meeting_1012/scripts/f10_paths_all.py.
Metric: every arm's decoded video re-encoded with the Wan2.1 VAE on one half-res grid; per latent frame |z − z_T| / |z_T| against the teacher clip with the same seed index; ratio = time-mean distance ÷ time-mean teacher-vs-teacher (different seed) distance; first 39 latent frames. 1.0 = as far as another teacher sample; > 1 = farther than the teacher's own seed-to-seed spread. It is distance from the teacher's samples, NOT quality: values below 1 come from shared noise (ours 832) or from outputs that vary less than the teacher, and do not mean better (Waypoint 0.97 on jar vs bench-43 quality 4.03 vs teacher 7.51).
Seeds: 10 per arm (1234, 4321, 9999, 11–77); jar and sandcastle have only 3 teacher videos (1234/4321/9999; the other 7 teacher seeds exist only as lat.pt, not decoded), so arms there are compared on n = 3. Pairing by seed index is real only for ours 832 (res_split_infer.py and spec_early.py seed each chunk with seed*1009 + c*7 on the same 832 latent shape); the student (gen_fast.py) and SDVG (spec_ar_loop.py) use a generator seeded with plain seed, MG2/Waypoint are other models, so for them seed k is effectively an independent teacher clip.

- Ours 704 − student (paired by seed index, ratio units, mean ± s.e.): jar +0.04 ± 0.05 (n=3), sandcastle −0.05 ± 0.03 (n=3), lake −0.08 ± 0.03, Stonehenge +0.07 ± 0.04, urban rain +0.07 ± 0.04, pouring −0.19 ± 0.02 (−0.1946); over 6 scenes −0.02 ± 0.04 → ours 704 is as far from the teacher as the student, within seed noise. Only lake and pouring reach 2 s.e. (both ours closer); none of the three scenes where ours 704 is farther (jar, Stonehenge, urban rain) reaches 2 s.e.
- Matrix-Game 2.0 − ours 704 over scenes +0.30 ± 0.07, − student +0.28 ± 0.08 → clearly farther. Waypoint − student +0.07 ± 0.07 → varies by scene, not clearly farther.
- Ours 832: even with the teacher's own noise it ends as far from the teacher as an independent teacher sample (1.00).

- Reference uncertainty: leave-one-teacher-seed-out jackknife s.e. of the teacher-vs-teacher time mean = 15 % (jar), 3 % (sandcastle), 5 % (lake), 8 % (Stonehenge), 2 % (urban rain), 2 % (pouring); it shifts all arms of a scene together. PCA (left panels) keeps only 26–36 % of the variance in 2 PCs (PC1 17–27 %, PC2 6–13 %).

## 12. Direction 1 — chunk-wise adaptive steps (learned 14B head) + commit policy, bench-43 (86 clips per arm; fps under load like section 2) — DONE 10/12
Raw: paper/1012/bench43_summary.json (keys cw07_sep, cw07_pc1, cw07_safe, cw05_pc1), per-clip paper/1012/vlm_scores_bench43.json, videos lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/bench43/*/<arm>_s*/video.mp4, loop.json mean_steps / chunk_start. Method: 14B 4-step schedule; after each step a learned head (head14b_v3) predicts per-token convergence; the whole chunk stops once ≥ 70 % (cw07) or ≥ 50 % (cw05) of tokens are predicted converged. Commit policies: separate (clean full pass), partial (fused + clean pass on the newest latent frame), safe (partial if the chunk stopped at step ≥ 3, full clean pass if at step 1–2). No reference pass (wall time = method time).
| arm | mean steps | steady s/chunk (under load) | fps ↑ | speed vs teacher (under load 3.9 fps) | VLM drift ↓ | VLM quality ↑ | Δ quality vs teacher (paired) | Δ drift vs teacher | boundary jumps |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| teacher 14B 4-step | 4.00 | 3.04 | 3.9 | 1.00× | 51.0 ± 3.0 | 7.51 ± 0.08 | – | – | 1 % |
| cw07_sep (chunk-wise 0.7, separate commit) | 2.91 | 2.045 | 5.9 | 1.51× | 50.3 ± 3.1 | 7.55 ± 0.08 | +0.03 ± 0.05 (12/65/9) | −0.6 ± 3.2 | 2 % |
| cw07_pc1 (chunk-wise 0.7, fused + partial) | 3.01 | 1.748 | 6.9 | 1.77× | 50.5 ± 3.2 | 7.42 ± 0.07 | −0.09 ± 0.06 (10/59/17) | −0.5 ± 3.1 | 1 % |
| cw07_safe (chunk-wise 0.7, safe commit) | 3.01 | 1.764 | 6.8 | 1.74× | 47.7 ± 3.2 | 7.44 ± 0.08 | −0.07 ± 0.06 (10/60/16) | −3.3 ± 3.1 | 1 % |
| cw05_pc1 (chunk-wise 0.5, fused + partial) | 2.61 | 1.722 | 7.0 | 1.79× | 53.2 ± 3.0 | 7.34 ± 0.09 | −0.17 ± 0.07 (9/57/20) | +2.2 ± 3.1 | 2 % |
| ours 832 (2-step fused + partial) | 2.00 | 1.225 (cloud-only) | 9.8 | 2.5× | 53.5 ± 3.0 | 7.21 ± 0.08 | −0.30 ± 0.08 | +2.5 ± 3.0 | 2 % |
| ours 704 (2-step fused + partial) | 2.00 | 0.761 (cloud-only) | 15.8 | 4.1× | 56.9 ± 2.7 | 6.90 ± 0.08 | −0.62 ± 0.09 | +5.9 ± 3.4 | 2 % |
(better/equal/worse counts in parentheses.) Reading: chunk-wise adaptive keeps teacher quality on 43 prompts (cw07_sep +0.03); adding fused/partial commit raises the speed-up from 1.5× to ≈1.75× with quality differences vs the teacher within ~1.5 s.e. (−0.07/−0.09), but paired against cw07_sep the commit policy costs −0.10 / −0.13 (borderline, < 2 s.e.; 10/12 additions below); the looser stop (cw05) saves steps (2.61) but almost no wall time (cause not diagnosed, see below; corrected 10/12 from "head + per-step overhead") and costs more quality → not worth it. Teacher-parity mode = cw07_safe (or cw07_pc1). Mean steps here are slightly higher than the 10/8 runs (2.9) because exits are counted per chunk on 43 different prompts.
Caveat: the speed-ups are ratios of under-load numbers; the quiet timing pass (same arms on jar/castle) is done: §15 (1.45× / 1.66× / 1.67× / 1.97× / 2.39× / 3.85×; prefer those in slides).
s/chunk basis of the ours rows (added 10/12): ours 832 / 704 are cloud-only (chunks ≥ 6, device decode excluded), so their ratios to the teacher's chunk-to-chunk basis are not like-for-like (the chunk-wise arms are). Ours 832 1.225 s/chunk is the bench-43 value; 1.24 (§1, §5) is the 12-prompt seed-1234 run.
Speed-column convention (added 10/12; no table value changed): "speed vs teacher" = listed (rounded) fps ÷ 3.9. Divided from the unrounded steady s/chunk instead (teacher 3.038 = per clip median of np.diff(chunk_start)[5:], median over 86 clips; same rule for the chunk-wise arms, cloud_chunk_s[6:] for ours; lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/bench43/*/{uniform4,cw07_sep,cw07_pc1,cw07_safe,cw05_pc1,pc1_832,pc1_704}_s*/loop.json): cw07_sep 1.49×, cw07_pc1 1.74×, cw07_safe 1.72×, cw05_pc1 1.76× (3.0382 / 1.7218), ours 832 2.48×, ours 704 3.99× (so text may write the realtime mode as ≈ 4×). All under load.
Added 10/12 (recomputed on CPU from the raw files named in this section):
- Paired arm vs arm (paper/1012/vlm_scores_bench43.json, 86 pairs, same task and seed; quality better / equal / worse in parentheses): cw07_pc1 − cw07_sep Δ quality −0.13 ± 0.07 (10/56/20), Δ drift +0.1 ± 3.0; cw07_safe − cw07_sep Δ quality −0.10 ± 0.06 (10/57/19), Δ drift −2.6 ± 2.8. |Δ| / SE = 1.9 and 1.7 (both < 2): a small, borderline quality cost of the commit policy on top of chunk-wise; vs the teacher both arms stay within noise (−0.09 ± 0.06 / −0.07 ± 0.06).
- Ours 832 / 704 fps are cloud-GPU only. res_split_infer.py runs the device step inside the same per-chunk loop on the same GPU (medians over 86 clips: 832 gen_s 16.06 ≈ cloud_s 14.57 + device_s 1.46; 704 gen_s 10.66 ≈ cloud_s 9.19 + device_s 1.45; device_chunk_s median 0.122 s/chunk at both), but the steady s/chunk uses cloud_chunk_s only. With the device step counted serially (per clip median of (cloud_chunk_s + device_chunk_s)[6:], median over clips; lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/bench43/*/pc1_{832,704}_s*/loop.json): 832 1.348 s/chunk → 8.9 fps (2.25× vs 3.038 s/chunk); 704 0.884 s/chunk → 13.6 fps (3.44×). The listed 9.8 / 15.8 fps (2.5× / 4.1×) therefore assume the device step runs in parallel on a separate device (§1/§6 convention). These loop.json files have no chunk_start, so the whole-loop interval is not logged directly. Full-VAE decode is not in this loop; on one shared GPU cloud + full VAE at 704 = 10.1 fps (§6).
- cw05_pc1 vs cw07_pc1 in the steady chunks 5–11 that the timing uses (per chunk stop step = 1 + argmax of per_chunk_commit_hist[:4] in loop.json): 2.62 vs 3.00 steps, yet 1.722 vs 1.748 s/chunk (0.026 s/chunk faster). Cause not diagnosed: head and per-step overhead scale with the number of steps, so they do not explain it.

## 13. Item 1 — latency with Chenfeng's method (sdv2_latency definitions; our port scripts/wm_latency.py) — profile DONE 10/12, ours realtime/quality rows DONE 10/12 (timing pass, table below; teacher-parity per-frame columns n/a)
Protocol: jar + sandcastle × seeds 1234/4321/9999 (6 clips, 161 frames = 153 output frames, empty text prompt as in the 10/5 slide), one RTX PRO 6000 GPU per run (original and optimized stacks ran concurrently on two separate GPUs, scripts/sdvg_profile.sh; no other generation jobs, but our vLLM Qwen3-VL-32B judge server for the StEvo-38 eval ran on another GPU through the whole profile, generation 10:32–11:19 UTC; per-seed timings are tight, e.g. original target jar 2.21–2.22 fps; corrected 10/12 from the f13 footer's "no other jobs of ours"), streaming decode: every committed chunk is VAE-decoded right away on a side CUDA stream of the SAME GPU (so decode competes with generation), per-frame ready time recorded. server_ms(frame) = ready(frame) − moment the frame's chunk started (its action consumed); output fps = (frames − 1) / (last − first ready), a whole-clip mean (corrected 10/12 from "frames / (last − first ready)"; scripts/wm_latency.py); first frame = first ready − start of generation (prepare excluded; prepare in this script = 23–27 s because it prepares both models with T5 on CPU; T5 on GPU takes ≈2.0 s, README section Q). Deadline for 'miss': 750 ms (one chunk of playback). Raw: lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/sdvg_profile/latency/<label>/{summary.json, frames.csv}; runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/sdvg_profile/fast0 (original stack: bf16 eager, as on the 10/5 slide) and fast1 (optimized stack).
| config | output fps ↑ | server_ms p50 ↓ | p90 | p99 ↓ | miss > 750 ms ↓ | first frame after gen start ↓ | gen s / 10 s clip ↓ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| original: draft only (1.3B) | 8.2 | 1750 | 2046 | 2076 | 99.3 % | 641 ms | 18.8 |
| original: target only (14B 4-step) | 2.2 | 5931 | 6424 | 6601 | 100 % | 3855 ms | 71.1 |
| original: SDVG τ −0.7 | 1.8 | 7568 | 7997 | 8064 | 100 % | 3974 ms | 86.8 |
| original: SDVG τ −2.2 | 2.1 | 7379 | 7922 | 8083 | 100 % | 3971 ms | 75.7 |
| optimized: draft only (= student) | 10.4 | 1434 | 1680 | 1700 | 96.7 % | 439 ms | 14.6 |
| optimized: target only (= teacher) | 4.2 | 3237 | 3728 | 3810 | 100 % | 1714 ms | 37.8 |
| optimized: SDVG τ −0.7 | 3.0 | 4463 | 4897 | 4946 | 100 % | 1794 ms | 51.2 |
| optimized: SDVG τ −2.2 | 3.7 | 4156 | 4851 | 4947 | 100 % | 1791 ms | 43.3 |
Reading: with the same stack and the same measurement, SDVG is slower than target-only on average (mean of 6 clips) at both thresholds on these two prompts. Per clip (same task and seed; added 10/12; per-clip fps from summary.json, per-clip p50 from frames.csv): τ −0.7 is slower on 6/6 clips in both stacks (fps and p50); τ −2.2 is faster in fps on 1/6 clips per stack, only where many chunks came from the draft: optimized jar s4321 (10 of 13: 4.65 vs 4.12 fps, p50 2645 vs 3240 ms) and original castle s1234 (7 of 13: 2.58 vs 2.28 fps, p50 3766 vs 5784 ms); optimized castle s4321 (7 of 13) has a lower p50 (2765 vs 3181 ms) but lower fps (4.01 vs 4.21); original castle s4321 ties (2.28 vs 2.28 fps). SDVG latency is bimodal: low mode = chunk 0 (always target) + draft chunks, high mode = rejected chunks (draft + verify + 14B regenerate); the modes do not overlap (loop.json plan). Acceptance: τ −2.2 accepted only 1–7 (original, mean 3.8) / 2–10 (optimized, mean 5.2) of 13 chunks here (summary.json per_clip[].accepted: 2,4,1,7,5,4 / 2,10,2,5,7,5; corrected 10/12 from "1–5"), fewer than on bench-43 (mean 10.1, §2), because jar/castle are hard. The original-code SDVG total on the 10/5 slide (99.9 s incl. prepare 11.3 and a non-streamed final decode 8.6) is consistent with 86.8 s generation here.
Steady output fps (added 10/12; recomputed on CPU by paper/meeting_1012/scripts/e_steady_fps.py → paper/meeting_1012/data/e_steady_fps.json): per clip, ready time of each chunk's last frame (loop.json frame_ready in sdvg_profile/fast{0,1}/*/<arm>_s{1234,4321,9999}), median of np.diff(...)[5:] (chunks ≥ 6), median over 6 clips, 12 / that; streamed decode is inside. Original: draft 7.60, target 2.05, SDVG τ −0.7 1.60, τ −2.2 1.61. Optimized: draft 9.86 (1.217 s/chunk), target 3.80 (3.155 s/chunk), SDVG τ −0.7 2.71, τ −2.2 3.53. The whole-clip output fps in the table is above steady by +5 % (optimized τ −2.2) to +33 % (original τ −2.2), so compare it with no steady fps. Timing-pass rows (added 10/12, same script, keys tp_<arm>; clips = the per_clip dirs of timing_pass/latency/<arm>/summary.json): ours704 9.97 (1.203 s/chunk, n = 5), ours832 6.39 (1.877, n = 5), c704_sep 8.57 (1.401, n = 5), c704_fused 11.29 (1.063), u2_832_sep 5.60 (2.142), c576_pc1 16.07 (0.747) (n = 6 each). The optimized target's 3.155 s/chunk (decode inside, quiet) and the bench-43 teacher's 3.038 s/chunk (chunk_start, decode excluded, under load, §2) are different quantities.
Ours rows (same protocol with streaming decode): realtime (ours704) and quality (ours832) come from the timing pass, lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/timing_pass/ (res_split_infer --stream-decode 1): DONE 10/12 (table below; timing_pass.sh does not call scripts/wm_latency.py, so it was run by hand into timing_pass/latency/<label>/ and scripts/f13_latency.py re-run). Caution (added 10/12): as run, the ours rows are NOT the same warm protocol. res_split_infer warms up only tasks[0] (ours704_warm / ours832_warm exist only under sand_filling_up_jar), so the first sandcastle clip runs a cold torch.compile on a new latent shape (46×88 vs jar 48×88): timing_pass ours704 sandcastle s1234 gen 38.8 s, first frame 6979 ms, vs gen 14.0–14.6 s for the other 5 clips. Warm up each task, or re-run sandcastle s1234, for every res_split_infer arm (ours704, ours832, c704_sep, u2_832_sep) before quoting (resolved 10/12 by excluding castle s1234 for ours704 / ours832 / c704_sep, n = 5; see the table below); f13_latency.py marks a row with such a clip with † and says so in its footer and table. Teacher-parity (cw07_safe) runs through spec_early in scripts/timing_pass.sh, which has no streaming decode (and, with --no-video, no decode at all), so it records only chunk_start: it gets steady fps (chunk_start, chunks ≥ 5, decode excluded) and gen s only; its first-frame, action→frame and miss columns stay n/a until a spec_early run with streaming decode exists.
Composed latency (not per frame; README paper/1012/README.md §Q table and §V3; corrected 10/12: not previously listed here): 576 cloud + refiner, full VAE: TTFF 3.31 / 3.36 s (RTT 0 / 100 ms), steady fps 12.1, action→frame p50 1568 / 1618 ms; 576 cloud + refiner, TAEHV: 2.70 / 2.75 s, 21.7, 956 / 1006 ms; 704 cloud + refiner, TAEHV: 2.94 / 2.99 s, 12.7, 1351 / 1401 ms; 14B 2-step 832 one GPU, TAEHV / full VAE: TTFF ≈ 3.4 s, fps 8.3 / 5.8, action→frame ≈ 1.45 / 2.05 s; 704 fused (V3): first chunk 0.46 s, TTFF ≈ 2.5 s composed (2.0 s prepare + 0.46 s + TAEHV). Bases: §Q's 21.7 is composed with TAEHV from the latency_split per-chunk times (4 prompts); §3's 21.5 for the same arm is README §U's 12 / max(cloud s/chunk, device + TAEHV) (cloud 0.56 s/chunk, seed-1234 run); different measurements. The 832 2-step 8.3 is whole-clip generation only (README §N: 153 frames / 18.5 s), not steady; 5.8 matches 12 / (18.5 / 13 + 0.631) (README §Q gives no derivation).
Ours rows (timing pass, same protocol: jar + sandcastle, one GPU, streaming full-VAE decode on the same GPU, prepare excluded; castle s1234 EXCLUDED because the warm-up only covered jar's latent shape and that clip paid a recompile (gen 38.8 s vs 14.0–14.6 s); n = 5 clips: jar ×3 + castle ×2; raw lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/timing_pass/latency/<arm>/summary.json, clips linked in timing_pass/clean/<arm>/):
| config | output fps ↑ | server_ms p50 ↓ | p90 | p99 ↓ | miss > 750 ms ↓ | first frame after gen start ↓ | gen s / 10 s clip ↓ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ours realtime mode (704, 2-step, fused + partial) | 10.8 | 1296 | 1492 | 1495 | 96.7 % | 574 ms | 14.3 |
| ours quality mode (832, 2-step, fused + partial) | 6.9 | 1995 | 2298 | 2310 | 100 % | 852 ms | 22.4 |
Reading: on ONE GPU with the decode competing, ours 704 gives 10.8 fps output and the lowest action→frame latency of all baselines and operating modes (p50 1.30 s vs teacher 3.24 s, student 1.43 s; corrected 10/12 from "of all arms": the unused variants 704 fused 1180 ms and 576 fused + partial 818 ms, §15, are lower). Its generation-only rate is 15.8 fps (bench-43); the gap is the 704 full-VAE decode (426 ms/chunk) sharing the GPU. With the decode on a separate device the output rate would be bounded by the cloud (0.761 s/chunk → 15.8 fps) — not measured end to end. No configuration meets the 750 ms per-frame budget.

## 14. StEvo-Bench subset — DONE 10/12 (38 camera-WM StEvo tasks, seed 1234, 249-frame videos covering the 253-frame look-away-and-back camera path; local Qwen3-VL-32B judge, 3-vote majority, 32 frames; NOT comparable to the paper's Gemini leaderboard)
Raw: lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/stevo38/eval_runs/lingbot_b38_<arm>/summary.json (+ per_task/<task>/*_report.json); videos lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/stevo38/<task>/<arm dir>; eval wrapper scripts/stevo_eval.sh (criteria control / physics / state); output folders are named lingbot_b38_<arm> so the code applies its camera-controlled protocol (requested occlusion = camera pan) to every arm, including Matrix-Game 2.0 and Waypoint. SDVG τ −2.2 not run (disk); MG2 run at 249 frames (needs latent frames divisible by 3).
Metric definitions (stevo_bench_code/eval/summarize_results.py): occlusion_done = the look-away happened; trigger_applied = the prompted action happened; physical_inaccuracy = a physical inaccuracy was detected (↓); control_success = occlusion_done AND trigger_applied; state_evol_success = the expected state change is visible after looking back; task_success = state_evol AND NOT physical_inaccuracy.

| teacher 14B 4-step | 0.34 | 0.45 | 0.68 | 0.13 | 0.08 | 0.08 |
| student 1.3B 4-step | 0.42 | 0.39 | 1.00 | 0.13 | 0.00 | 0.00 |
| SDVG + actions τ −0.7 | 0.18 | 0.61 | 0.71 | 0.11 | 0.00 | 0.00 |
| Matrix-Game 2.0 | 0.97 | 0.24 | 0.79 | 0.24 | 0.00 | 0.00 |
| Waypoint-1.5-1B | 0.05 | 0.26 | 0.92 | 0.00 | 0.03 | 0.00 |
| ours quality mode (832) | 0.32 | 0.39 | 0.55 | 0.08 | 0.05 | 0.05 |
| ours realtime mode (704) | 0.24 | 0.37 | 0.68 | 0.08 | 0.05 | 0.00 |
(fractions of 38 tasks; binomial s.e. ≈ 0.05–0.08, so differences < ~0.15 are not significant.) Reading: state evolution / task success are at the floor (0–0.08) for every arm, as in the 10/7 run (85 tasks: 1–5 %) — this judge+protocol does not separate the arms there. Physical inaccuracy is the informative column: the 14B-based arms (teacher 0.68, ours 0.55–0.68, SDVG 0.71) are better than Matrix-Game 2.0 (0.79), Waypoint (0.92) and the student (1.00). Matrix-Game 2.0's high 'occlusion done' (0.97) reflects that it turns away visibly (game-style mouse look) while rarely showing the prompted action (0.24). Teacher-parity mode (chunk-wise) not run on StEvo (time).
Paired physical inaccuracy (added 10/12; exact two-sided McNemar, uncorrected, same 38 tasks; a-only / b-only flagged tasks; paper/meeting_1012/data/f12_stevo.json, scripts/f12_stevo.py; also in the f12_stevo footer): vs student — teacher 0/12 p < 0.001, SDVG 0/11 p < 0.001, ours 832 0/17 p < 0.001, ours 704 0/12 p < 0.001; vs Waypoint — teacher 3/12 p = 0.035, SDVG 3/11 p = 0.057 (borderline), ours 832 3/17 p = 0.003, ours 704 3/12 p = 0.035; vs MG2 — teacher 4/8 p = 0.39, SDVG 5/8 p = 0.58, ours 832 3/12 p = 0.035, ours 704 5/9 p = 0.42. So the "better than Waypoint" reading above holds for the teacher and both our modes but not clearly for SDVG; vs MG2 only ours 832 separates.
Frame counts (added 10/12, ffprobe on lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/stevo38/eval_in/lingbot_b38_<arm>/*.mp4): LingBot arms and MG2 249 frames; Waypoint 256 (4 frames per call); all cover the 253-frame camera path.

## 16. Action item 2, original test — physics-verifier SDVG, final videos scored on StEvo-38 (DONE 10/12)
SDVG + actions with a physics VLM verifier (spec_ar_loop --verifiers physapi:TAU): per chunk, previous + candidate chunk decoded, 12 frames, Qwen3-VL-32B answers the 5 WorldModelBench-style violation questions of specwm/phys_judge.py (scene change, appear/disappear, impossible deformation, impossible fluid/granular motion, gravity); score = 5 − #yes; accept the draft chunk if score ≥ τ. Same 38 tasks / seed / 253-frame path / judge as section 14. NOTE: verifier and StEvo judge are the same base model (different prompts) — this favours the verifier. Raw: lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/stevo38/<task>/physapi_<τ>_s1234/loop.json, eval_runs/lingbot_b38_phys{5,4}/summary.json.
| arm | draft chunks accepted (of 21) | gen s / clip ↓ | physical inaccuracy ↓ | control success ↑ | state evolution ↑ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| teacher 14B 4-step | – | 60.0 | **0.68** | 0.13 | 0.08 |
| SDVG + ImageReward τ −0.7 | (≈1–2 of 13 on bench-43) | 74.9 | 0.71 | 0.11 | 0.00 |
| SDVG + physics verifier τ=5 | 13.5 | 137.8 (verify ≈104) | 0.84 | 0.21 | 0.00 |
| SDVG + physics verifier τ=4 | 17.1 | 131.4 (verify ≈104) | 0.97 | 0.24 | 0.05 |
| student 1.3B | – | – | 1.00 | 0.13 | 0.00 |
Paired McNemar (38 tasks, flagged only-x vs only-y): phys τ5 vs teacher 8 vs 2 (p = 0.11), vs ImageReward SDVG 6 vs 1 (p = 0.13), vs student 0 vs 6 (p = 0.03); phys τ4 vs teacher 11 vs 0 (p = 0.001), vs student 0 vs 1 (p = 1.0).
Verdict: NO-GO. The physics verifier does not make the final videos more physical than target-only (worse: 0.84 / 0.97 vs 0.68), it is > 2× slower than the teacher, and the more draft chunks it accepts the closer the result gets to the student — even with the judge's own model as verifier.

## 17. Teacher-parity mode with a head trained WITHOUT any bench-43 scene (DONE 10/12)
Concern: the original head14b_v3 was trained on 79 of the 90 camera-task scenes, incl. 40 of the 43 bench-43 scenes (seed 7; bench-43 uses seeds 1234/4321). New head: gap_dump on the 47 scenes NOT in bench-43 (seed 7, 611 chunks; specwm_out/gap_dump_nob43), same recipe (lr 1e-4, 30 epochs; channel-norm stats chan_norm_14b.pt reused) → specwm_out/head14b_nob43/head.pt. Held-out 7 scenes: AUROC@ε0.24 0.928 / 0.960 / 0.977 (k = 1/2/3), predicted depth 2.22 vs oracle 2.21 — same as the old head.
Bench-43 rerun (cw07_safe_nob43, 86 clips, safe commit, ε 0.24, chunk-frac 0.7): mean steps 2.92 (old head 3.01).
| head | Δ quality vs teacher (paired, 86) | Δ drift vs teacher | VLM quality | VLM drift |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| old (trained incl. 40 bench-43 scenes) | −0.07 ± 0.06 | −3.3 ± 3.1 | 7.44 | 47.7 |
| new (no bench-43 scene) | +0.03 ± 0.07 | +3.4 ± 2.7 | 7.55 | 54.4 |
Both within ~1.3 s.e. of the teacher → the teacher-parity claim does not depend on the scene overlap. (new − old: quality +0.10 ± 0.06, drift +6.6 ± 2.7 — run-to-run variation of the stopping decisions; the old head's lower drift should not be quoted as an advantage.)

## 18. Action item A (10/12 meeting) — does the 14B fit on a device GPU? (DONE 10/13; specwm/fit24.py, scripts/fit24_report.py, data/fit24.json, data/fit24_report.md)
Setup: 14B alone (no draft, no cloud), the optimized stack of the quality / realtime modes (2-step σ 1.0→0.833, fused + partial commit 1, SageAttention, compile, ring KV window 18 latent frames incl. 6 sink), 161 frames, easy_lake seed 1234 for the footprint, one RTX PRO 6000. "Device emulation" = CUDA caching allocator capped at 24 / 32 GB with torch.cuda.set_per_process_memory_fraction AFTER the weight swap (a device would load pre-quantized weights); the CUDA context (~0.5–1 GB) and cuBLAS / compile workspaces are outside the cap, so a real card needs ≈1 GiB more than "peak reserved". KV FP8 = the ring buffer allocated as float8_e4m3fn (per-tensor, no scale; cast on write, bf16 on read). INT4 = packed 4-bit round-to-nearest with scale search (quantize.PackedInt4Linear) or AWQ (awq.py, 3 calibration chunks); both dequantize on the fly (no INT4 kernel), so their fps is a lower bound. Decode = the device's per-latent-frame streamed causal VAE decode (as res_split_infer), or a spatial 2x2 tiled chunk decode (vae_tiled; approximate because of the VAE's global attention block).
Footprint (GiB; weights incl. the CLIP image encoder; "resident" = after prepare, before generation):
| config | weights ↓ | KV window ↓ | resident ↓ | peak gen ↓ | peak streamed decode ↓ | peak reserved ↓ | fits 24 GB | fits 32 GB | steady fps (PRO 6000) ↑ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 704x384, FP8 w, bf16 KV (realtime mode as run) | 17.6 | 14.2 | 32.7 | 33.5 | 37.6 | 38.9 | no | no | 16.0 |
| 704x384, FP8 w, FP8 KV | 17.6 | 7.1 | 25.6 | 26.8 | 30.5 | 31.8 (27.9 tiled) | no | yes (cap-32 run OK) | 15.1 |
| 704x384, INT4 w, bf16 KV | 8.9 | 14.2 | 24.1 | 25.6 | 28.9 | 30.7 | no (cap-24 OOM at prepare) | yes | 10.7 |
| 704x384, INT4 w, FP8 KV | 8.9 | 7.1 | 17.0 | 18.5 | 21.8 | 23.3 (19.4 tiled) | yes, marginal streamed; yes tiled (cap-24 runs OK) | yes | 10.3 |
| 704x384, AWQ-INT4 w, FP8 KV | 9.1 | 7.1 | 17.0 | 18.6 | 21.8 | 23.2 | same as INT4 | yes | 10.2 |
| 832x480, FP8 w, bf16 KV (quality mode as run) | 17.6 | 20.7 | 39.3 | 40.4 | 46.4 | 48.4 | no | no | 10.0 |
| 832x480, FP8 w, FP8 KV | 17.6 | 10.3 | 29.0 | 30.6 | 36.0 | 38.7 (32.0 tiled) | no | no (tiled: at the edge) | 9.5 |
| 832x480, INT4 w, FP8 KV | 8.9 | 10.3 | 20.3 | 21.9 | 27.4 | 29.7 (23.9 tiled) | no streamed (cap-24 OOM at decode); tiled at the edge | yes | 6.9 |
Reading: the KV window, not the weights, is the big item (bf16 window = 14.2 GiB at 704, 20.7 at 832 — the 14B FP8 weights are 17.6). The per-frame streamed VAE decode adds 3.3 GiB (704) / 5.4 GiB (832) over the generation peak; the tiled decode adds 0.4–1.1 GiB.
Quality (VLM clip judge, seed 1234, all 43 bench-43 prompts, paired with the bench-43 clip of the same mode without quantization = pc1_704 / pc1_832 s1234):
| config | VLM quality ↑ | VLM drift ↓ | Δ quality vs unquantized (paired, 43) ↑ | Δ drift ↓ | better / equal / worse |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 704, FP8 w, FP8 KV | 6.77 ± 0.09 (ref 6.88) | 57.6 ± 4.0 (ref 57.3) | **−0.12 ± 0.11** | +0.2 ± 3.5 | 8 / 22 / 13 |
| 704, INT4 w, FP8 KV | 6.19 ± 0.14 | 60.6 ± 3.5 | −0.70 ± 0.14 | +3.3 ± 3.5 | 4 / 13 / 26 |
| 704, AWQ-INT4 w, FP8 KV | 6.28 ± 0.15 | 60.9 ± 3.8 | −0.60 ± 0.13 | +3.6 ± 4.0 | 2 / 19 / 22 |
| 832, FP8 w, FP8 KV | 7.12 ± 0.10 (ref 7.30) | 49.4 ± 4.2 (ref 50.6) | −0.19 ± 0.08 | −1.2 ± 2.9 | 2 / 32 / 9 |
(8-prompt pilot in data/fit24_report.md agrees. No pixel comparison to a reference clip: a rerun of the SAME config and seed already diverges — easy_lake 704: 34 dB at frame 0, 21 dB at frame 12, 11.5 dB at frame 80 vs the bench-43 clip — so PSNR measures AR chaos, not quantization.)
Verdict: (i) 32 GB device (RTX 5090 class): the realtime mode fits as is with an FP8 KV window — 704x384, FP8 weights, 15.1 fps on the PRO 6000, quality −0.12 ± 0.11 (within noise), no cloud needed. (ii) 24 GB device (4090 / A5000): fits only with INT4 weights + FP8 KV + tiled decode (19.4 GiB reserved); the naive INT4 costs −0.6 to −0.7 quality (6.2–6.3, still above the 1.3B student 5.83 but below MG2 6.91) and runs at 10.3 fps because the weights are dequantized on the fly — a real INT4 kernel (Marlin / torchao) and a calibrated method (GPTQ-4, better AWQ) are the obvious next steps, not tested. (iii) 832x480 does not fit 24 GB and only marginally 32 GB. All fps are PRO 6000 numbers; a 4090 (1 TB/s vs 1.8 TB/s, fewer SMs) will be slower — not measured, no such card reachable. Other levers not tried: shorter KV window (quality unknown), INT4 KV (kvstore2 exists for the old stack), VAE removal (Guandao's suggestion).
Other: a704_awq_fp8kv (8-prompt run) hung after 2 clips in a futex wait (killed); the 43-prompt AWQ run completed normally. Figure figs/f14_fit24_frame100_704.png (frame 100 of easy_lake and domino_falling for the four 704 variants; contents differ between runs by AR chaos, compare sharpness not content).

## 19. Device fit, follow-ups (DONE 10/13): INT4 kernels, GPTQ-4, KV window 12, and the "device-only 14B" table row (data/fit24_report.md, data/fit24.json, data/devfit_row.json, data/int4_bench*.json)
**INT4 kernels (specwm/int4_bench.py, int4_bench2.py, int4_kernels.py; one PRO 6000).** At our token counts (M = 3168 tokens per forward at 704x384, 4680 at 832, 1056 for the partial commit) weight-only INT4 GEMMs are slower than bf16: in-tree tinygemm torch._weight_int4pack_mm 13-14x slower at M = 3168 (decode-oriented kernel), a naive fused-dequant Triton W4A16 GEMM 6x slower; FP8 W8A8 _scaled_mm is 1.1-1.5x faster than bf16 at N = 13824 and slightly slower at K = 13824. So the best INT4 path is dequantize-then-GEMM; a fused Triton dequant brings a linear from 2.0x to 1.07x of the bf16 time in the microbench (exact), but end to end it changed nothing (10.28 vs 10.3 fps at 704) — under torch.compile the Python-launched Triton kernel breaks the block graph, and the remaining gap to the FP8 stack (1.46x) is the bf16 GEMM + lost fusion. Conclusion: INT4 weights cap the speed at about the bf16-stack level; FP8 weights stay the fastest. (A W4A8 kernel such as QServe/Marlin-W4A8 could change this; not available here.)
**GPTQ-4 (fit24 --wq gptq4: Hessians from 3 calibration chunks of the bf16 model, 4-bit group 128, packed; 780-790 s to quantize 568 linears).** 704x384: quality −0.23 ± 0.09 vs the unquantized run (RTN −0.70, AWQ −0.60), drift +0.9 ± 3.2. 832x480: −0.58 ± 0.12, drift +1.6 ± 4.2 (worse than at 704; not understood). At 832 the first attempt produced all-black videos: blocks.1.self_attn.o got non-finite GPTQ scales (Cholesky at damp 0.01); fit24 now re-quantizes such a layer at damp 0.1 (finite), which is the number above.
**KV window 12 latent frames (6 sink + 6 local = 2 chunks, instead of 18 = 4 chunks).** Quality unchanged within noise: 704 −0.07 ± 0.12 (drift −4.8 ± 4.3), 832 −0.05 ± 0.10 (drift +2.3 ± 2.5), 43 prompts each. Speed up: 704 17.5 fps (from 15.1), 832 11.2 (from 9.5). Memory: KV 4.7 / 6.9 GiB (from 7.1 / 10.3, FP8).
**Combined 24 GB candidate: 704x384, GPTQ-4 weights, FP8 KV, window 12, tiled decode** — peak reserved 17.0 GiB under a 24 GB cap (20.9 streamed), 11.3-11.4 fps, quality −0.14 ± 0.09 (43 prompts; drift −1.4 ± 3.8; better/equal/worse 3/30/10). **32 GB:** 704 FP8/FP8-KV window 12: 24.2 GiB gen peak, 29.4 streamed / ~25 tiled, 17.5 fps, −0.07 ± 0.12; 832 FP8/FP8-KV window 12 with tiled decode runs under a 32 GB cap (28.8 GiB, 11.3 fps, −0.05 ± 0.10) — i.e. the quality-mode resolution fits a 32 GB card with the window change only.
| device budget | config | peak reserved (cap run) ↓ | fps (PRO 6000) ↑ | Δ quality vs same mode unquantized (43 prompts) ↑ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 24 GB | 704, GPTQ-4 w, FP8 KV, window 12, tiled decode | 17.0 GiB | 11.3 | −0.14 ± 0.09 |
| 24 GB | 704, RTN-INT4 w, FP8 KV, window 18, tiled | 19.4 | 10.3 | −0.70 ± 0.14 |
| 32 GB | 704, FP8 w, FP8 KV, window 18, streamed | 31.9 | 15.1 | −0.12 ± 0.11 |
| 32 GB | 704, FP8 w, FP8 KV, window 12, streamed | 29.4 | 17.5 | −0.07 ± 0.12 |
| 32 GB | 832, FP8 w, FP8 KV, window 12, tiled | 28.8 | 11.3 | −0.05 ± 0.10 |
**"Device-only 14B" row for the final table (scripts/devfit_row.py; 704x384, FP8 w, FP8 KV, window 18, 2-step fused + partial; bench-43 seeds 1234 + 4321 = 86 clips, paired with the teacher; StEvo-38 at 253 frames):** gen 16.6 s/clip incl. decode + mp4 (generation alone ≈ 11 s), steady 15.0 fps quiet, VLM quality 6.77 ± 0.08, drift 57.6 ± 2.8, Δ vs teacher −0.74 ± 0.09 / +6.6 ± 3.3 (better/equal/worse 0/39/47), boundary jumps 3 %, StEvo-38 physical inaccuracy 0.66 (teacher 0.68, realtime mode 0.68). Compared with the realtime cloud+device mode (−0.62 ± 0.09, 15.7 fps): the same quality tier, no cloud, fits a 32 GB card. StEvo aggregation note: the eval wrote per-task majority votes but left "overall" empty (a later aggregation step did not run); the 0.66 is the mean of the 38 per-task votes, validated against ours704 / teacher (0.684 both ways).

## 20. Draft (1.3B) speed ladder (DONE 10/13; specwm/fit24.py --model 1p3b, scripts/draft_ladder_report.py, data/draft_ladder.json, lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/draft_ladder/)
Question (research_draft_acceleration.md §5 step 1): how far above real time can the 1.3B drafter run in latent space, and what does each speed lever cost in quality? 12 configs = {4-step, 2-step (nodes 0,500)} x {832x480, 704x384, 576x320} x {KV window 18, 12}; FP8 weights, bf16 KV, fused + partial commit, seed 1234, all 43 bench-43 prompts (161 frames), one PRO 6000 per run, quiet (4 chains on 4 GPUs, nothing else on them). Steady s/chunk = median of chunks 6-13 generation only (no decode). Quality = VLM clip judge, paired with the bench-43 student arm (uniform4_1p3b_s1234 = 832, 4-step, separate commit, bf16 KV; 5.88 / 75.7) and the teacher (uniform4_s1234: 7.58 / 50.0).
| draft 1.3B config | steady s/chunk ↓ | fps ↑ | look-ahead (0.75 s / chunk_s) ↑ | peak gen GiB ↓ | streamed decode s/chunk ↓ | VLM quality ↑ | VLM drift ↓ | Δ quality vs student arm (paired, 43) ↑ | Δ quality vs teacher ↑ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4-step, 832, window 18 | 0.437 | 27.5 | 1.7x | 7.4 | 0.63 | 5.84 ± 0.24 | 73.8 ± 3.4 | −0.05 ± 0.21 | −1.74 ± 0.23 |
| 4-step, 832, window 12 | 0.352 | 34.1 | 2.1x | 5.8 | 0.63 | 5.74 ± 0.21 | 74.9 ± 3.5 | −0.14 ± 0.22 | −1.84 ± 0.22 |
| 4-step, 704, window 18 | 0.257 | 46.7 | 2.9x | 5.8 | 0.43 | 5.63 ± 0.22 | 75.7 ± 3.7 | −0.26 ± 0.20 | −1.95 ± 0.23 |
| 4-step, 704, window 12 | 0.220 | 54.5 | 3.4x | 4.7 | 0.43 | **5.86 ± 0.20** | 77.3 ± 3.6 | **−0.02 ± 0.17** | **−1.72 ± 0.22** |
| 4-step, 576, window 18 | 0.210 | 57.1 | 3.6x | 4.6 | 0.29 | 5.51 ± 0.22 | 74.1 ± 3.3 | −0.37 ± 0.20 | −2.07 ± 0.23 |
| 4-step, 576, window 12 | 0.201 | 59.7 | 3.7x | 3.9 | 0.29 | 5.51 ± 0.22 | 74.1 ± 3.2 | −0.37 ± 0.20 | −2.07 ± 0.23 |
| 2-step, 832, window 18 | 0.241 | 49.8 | 3.1x | 7.4 | 0.63 | 4.00 ± 0.24 | 78.5 ± 3.5 | −1.88 ± 0.25 | −3.58 ± 0.22 |
| 2-step, 832, window 12 | 0.194 | 61.9 | 3.9x | 5.8 | 0.63 | 4.12 ± 0.26 | 77.0 ± 3.3 | −1.77 ± 0.25 | −3.47 ± 0.26 |
| 2-step, 704, window 18 | 0.144 | 83.3 | 5.2x | 5.8 | 0.43 | 4.07 ± 0.24 | 79.2 ± 3.0 | −1.81 ± 0.25 | −3.51 ± 0.25 |
| 2-step, 704, window 12 | 0.126 | 95.2 | 6.0x | 4.7 | 0.43 | 4.40 ± 0.25 | 80.6 ± 2.3 | −1.49 ± 0.26 | −3.19 ± 0.25 |
| 2-step, 576, window 18 | 0.120 | 100.0 | 6.2x | 4.6 | 0.30 | 4.40 ± 0.24 | 77.6 ± 2.8 | −1.49 ± 0.25 | −3.19 ± 0.24 |
| 2-step, 576, window 12 | **0.118** | **101.7** | **6.4x** | **3.9** | 0.30 | 4.35 ± 0.25 | 79.5 ± 2.7 | −1.53 ± 0.26 | −3.23 ± 0.26 |
(576 window 18 vs 12 show the same means by coincidence: the clips differ — pixel mean |diff| 26-43 after frame 80 — and 23 of 42 per-clip scores differ; the integer judge scores happen to sum equally.)
Reading: (i) the 1.3B draft in latent space is already above real time everywhere: 1.7x at the student's own config, 3.4x at 704/window 12 at the same quality as the student arm (−0.02 ± 0.17), 6.4x at 2-step 576 — in ≤ 7.4 GiB (≤ 4.7 GiB at 704/w12), so the drafter fits any 8 GB device. (ii) 2 steps cost −1.5 to −1.9 quality (4.0-4.4, judge-level "smeared"), unusable as a shown frame and even further from the teacher; the 4-step draft at 704/576 is the useful ladder. (iii) The KV window 12 is free for the 1.3B too (paired w12 − w18, 43 clips: 4-step 832 −0.09 ± 0.15, 704 +0.23 ± 0.15, 576 +0.00 ± 0.12; drift within ±2). (iv) Drift is 74-81 for every draft config vs 50 for the teacher: speed levers do not change the draft's drift, which is the acceptance-rate problem (facts §4, §16) — the ladder buys look-ahead budget, not a better proposal. (v) The displayed path is decode-bound: streamed per-frame VAE decode costs 0.63 s/chunk at 832 (> the 0.437 s generation), 0.43 at 704, 0.30 at 576; gen + decode = 1.07 / 0.69 / 0.51 s per 0.75 s chunk, so only 704 and below are real time end to end on one GPU, and a latent-space design (verify before decode) keeps the full look-ahead.
Budget for design D1 (latent look-ahead + 14B one-step departure verify, research_draft_acceleration.md §4): with the 704/w12 draft (0.22 s/chunk) the drafter gains 0.53 s of slack per real-time chunk; the 14B one-step verify costs ≈ 0.35-0.55 s per chunk at 832 (§15), so a second GPU can verify at most 1-2 draft chunks per real-time chunk; the loop only pays if ≥ ~50 % of draft chunks are accepted (today 7 % of tokens within ε, §4) — the D3 anchor (teacher-conditioned draft) is the prerequisite, not more draft speed.

## 21. Anchored vs own-history draft gap per chunk + departure-vs-quality (DONE 10/13; meeting items B and D3; specwm/anchor_gap.py, scripts/anchor_gap_report.py, data/anchor_gap.json, figs/f15_anchor_gap.png, clips + stats lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/anchor_gap/<task>_s<seed>/)
Setup: 43 bench-43 prompts x seeds 7 / 1234 / 4321 = 129 runs, 832x480, 161 frames = 13 chunks, one PRO 6000 per run (4 shards, quiet). One 14B 4-step rollout per run (FP8 / Sage / compile / ring KV, window 18). At every chunk the same noise z0 is denoised by: the 14B (steps 1-3 kept = self-speculation reference), the 14B from another noise (sample-diversity reference), the 1.3B ANCHORED on the teacher's history (its KV written from the 14B's committed latents = what gap_dump_cross measured on 10/6; the 1.3B is bf16, 4 steps) and the 1.3B on its OWN history (KV written from its own outputs = the student rollout; its clip is decoded). Gap = ||x0 - x0_T^(4)|| / sigma_T per 1x2x2 token (sigma_T = median token norm of the 14B output), eps = 0.24; stored per latent frame. Note: the 10/6 "7 % within eps" was ALREADY the anchored condition (gap_dump_cross committed the teacher's latents into the 1.3B cache), so "anchor the draft on the teacher state" is not a new lever; this run separates capacity from history and adds the chunk-index / KV-fill axis Guandao asked for.
| chunk index | 14B step 1 (self-spec.) | 14B step 2 | 14B step 3 | 14B, other noise | 1.3B anchored, 4 steps | 1.3B own history, 4 steps | own − anchored (sigma_T) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 (empty KV; frame 0 = conditioning image) | 0.318 / 49 % | 0.235 / 64 % | 0.165 / 81 % | 0.362 / 49 % | 0.452 / 36 % | 0.452 / 36 % | 0 |
| 0, frames 1-2 only | 0.418 / 24 % | – | – | 0.511 / 23 % | 0.627 / 4 % | 0.627 / 4 % | 0 |
| 1 | 0.381 / 30 % | 0.271 / 52 % | 0.181 / 76 % | 0.437 / 29 % | 0.690 / 4 % | 0.765 / 1 % | +0.08 ± 0.01 |
| 2 | 0.403 / 28 % | 0.283 / 50 % | 0.187 / 74 % | 0.490 / 26 % | 0.707 / 4 % | 0.954 / 0 % | +0.25 ± 0.01 |
| 4 | 0.373 / 34 % | 0.263 / 55 % | 0.176 / 80 % | 0.487 / 29 % | 0.695 / 7 % | 1.058 / 0 % | +0.36 ± 0.01 |
| 8 | 0.366 / 35 % | 0.258 / 55 % | 0.171 / 81 % | 0.480 / 30 % | 0.696 / 6 % | 1.100 / 0 % | +0.40 ± 0.02 |
| 12 | 0.374 / 29 % | 0.257 / 54 % | 0.169 / 82 % | 0.454 / 25 % | 0.641 / 7 % | 1.105 / 0 % | +0.46 ± 0.02 |
| mean, chunks 1-12 | 0.366 / 34 % | 0.256 / 56 % | 0.170 / 81 % | 0.463 / 29 % | 0.680 / 6 % | 1.049 / 0 % | +0.37 |
(cells = mean gap / share of tokens within eps, mean over 129 runs; full per-chunk table and per-frame curves in data/anchor_gap.json; 10/6 numbers for comparison: anchored 0.63 / 7 %, 14B step 1 0.35 / 36 %, 14B other noise 0.405 / 34 % on 6 scenes.)
Reading: (i) KV-fill check (Guandao's caveat on the PCA figure): the first chunk's low gap (36 %) is the conditioning frame — frame 0 is nearly fixed for every model (gap 0.06-0.12). On frames 1-2 of chunk 0 the draft is 0.627 / 4 %, against 0.767 / 3 % on the same frame positions of later chunks: an empty KV makes the draft only slightly closer, not acceptable. So the early agreement in the trajectory figure is the shared conditioning image plus history that has not yet diverged, not a KV-fill artefact of the measure. (ii) Capacity vs history: anchored on the teacher's own state the 1.3B is 0.68 sigma_T away with 6 % of tokens within eps at EVERY chunk (flat in c) — that is the model's per-chunk error and it alone already exceeds the 14B's own sample-to-sample spread (0.46 / 29 %); running on its own history adds +0.08 at chunk 1 and saturates at about +0.45 by chunk 5-6 (1.05-1.1 sigma_T, 0 % within eps). The departure is therefore front-loaded (capacity) and accumulates for ~5 chunks, then plateaus; there is no "agree for a while, then depart" regime at chunk level. (iii) Within a chunk the gap saw-tooths: the first latent frame after a commit is closest, the third farthest (figure f15; e.g. anchored 0.5 → 0.85 within each chunk), i.e. the commit/refresh resets part of the error — the same mechanism the safe commit exploits for the 14B. (iv) Self-speculation reference on the same runs: the 14B's own step 1 is 0.37 / 34 %, step 2 0.26 / 56 %, step 3 0.17 / 81 %, i.e. its one-step estimate is already as close to its final output as another 14B sample is.
Departure vs quality (item B-iii; VLM clip judge on the decoded own-history student clip and the teacher clip of each run; student 5.84 / 75.9, teacher 7.51 / 49.7 — matches bench-43's student / teacher arms 5.88 / 75.7 and 7.58 / 50.0):
| per-clip departure measure | Spearman vs student quality ↑ | vs student drift ↓ | vs Δquality (student − teacher) | vs Δdrift |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| own-history gap, chunks 1-12 mean | −0.10 (p = 0.26) | +0.10 (p = 0.26) | −0.01 (p = 0.89) | −0.03 (p = 0.70) |
| own-history gap, chunks 1-4 | −0.09 (p = 0.30) | +0.20 (p = 0.02) | −0.08 (p = 0.37) | +0.03 (p = 0.75) |
| own-history gap, chunks 9-12 | −0.04 (p = 0.68) | −0.09 (p = 0.29) | +0.04 (p = 0.66) | −0.14 (p = 0.11) |
| anchored gap, chunks 1-12 (capacity) | −0.20 (p = 0.02) | +0.28 (p = 0.001) | −0.11 (p = 0.23) | +0.02 (p = 0.84) |
| gap at chunk 0 (first chunk, incl. conditioning frame) | **−0.38 (p < 0.001)** | **+0.33 (p < 0.001)** | −0.28 (p = 0.001) | +0.18 (p = 0.04) |
Reading: the distance of the student's rollout from the teacher's trajectory does NOT predict the student clip's judged quality or drift (|rho| ≤ 0.2, n = 129); only the first chunk's gap — how hard the scene is for the 1.3B from the start — has a modest correlation (−0.38). This is the same message as §11 across arms (ours 704 as far as the student at 1.15 but 1 point better): trajectory distance is a paired-verification criterion, not a quality signal, so a "departure-point" verifier would decide acceptance, not quality. Caveat: judge is an 8-frame integer VLM score; the student's quality range is narrow.
Consequences: (a) D3 "anchor the draft on the teacher state" is closed — anchoring was already in the 10/6 number and buys 0.37 sigma_T but leaves 6 % acceptable; a usable separate drafter needs distillation against the 14B (capacity), not a better handoff. (b) The departure-point verifier as a cheap quality verifier (item B/D) is not supported by the data; as an acceptance criterion it is what the gap head already predicts (AUROC 0.91 cross, 0.93-0.98 self). (c) The self-speculation numbers (34 / 56 / 81 % at steps 1-3) are the ones to carry into the paper's "why self-draft" argument.

## 22. Distilling the 1.3B draft against the 14B (DONE 10/13; separate-drafter line CLOSED; specwm/draft_distill4.py --loss-nodes, specwm/anchor_gap.py --d-lora, scripts/distill_eval_report.py, data/distill47_eval.json, figs/f16_distill47_*.jpg; runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/distill47/<run>/draft_lora_s<step>.pt, eval lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/distill47_eval/<run>_s<step>/<task>_s7/)
Question (follow-up of §21): the anchored 1.3B is 0.68 sigma_T / 6 % acceptable at every chunk = capacity. Can a LoRA distilled against the 14B's own 4-step sampler lift the anchored acceptance on UNSEEN scenes to a usable level (target >= 30 %, i.e. the 14B's own step-1 estimate, 32 %)? Data: 47 tasks_camera scenes that are not in bench-43 x seeds 1-4 (target_dump.py, t-steps 0/250/500/750, 188 clips) + the 36 jar/castle pilot clips of 10/8 = 224 clips, 13 chunks each, per chunk the 14B's x0 at all four nodes with its own paired re-noise sequence; the draft sees the 14B's committed latents (teacher forcing). Loss = sum over nodes ||x0_D - x0_T||^2 / ||x0_T||^2, LoRA on q,k,v,o,ffn0,ffn2 of all 30 blocks, lr 1e-4, AdamW, 3000 steps (~2 epochs over 212 clips, 173 min on one PRO 6000), 12 clips held out for the validation loss (other seeds of training scenes). Variants: A = all 4 nodes, rank 64; B = nodes 0 and 1 only (layout nodes; texture nodes untouched), rank 64; C = all nodes, rank 128.
Validation loss (node 0 / nodes 1-3): start 0.326 / 0.076 → A 0.167 / 0.052 (s2004), B 0.172 / 0.057 (s2004), C 0.173 / 0.055 (s2004); all three got worse again at s2508 (0.193-0.210), i.e. the LoRAs peak around 2000 steps on this data, as on 9/26 (~400 steps there with 2 scenes).
Held-out test: the 12 bench-43 scenes of §21 that are NOT in the training set (easy_lake, domino_falling, pouring_water_into_cup, newtons_cradle, billiard_break, tire_inflate, traffic_light_green, easy_urban_rain, shower_knob_turn, balloon_block_cut, tea_dilution, seesaw_balance), seed 7, exact §21 protocol (same 14B rollout, same z0; anchored = draft on the teacher's KV; own history = the student rollout, decoded and judged by the VLM clip judge). "stock" = the untouched 1.3B from the §21 runs.
| draft variant | anchored gap sigma_T ↓ | anchored within eps ↑ | anchored 1-step within eps ↑ | own-history gap ↓ | student clip quality ↑ | student drift ↓ | Δ quality vs stock (paired, n = 12) ↑ | Δ drift vs stock ↓ | teacher clip quality (control) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| stock 1.3B | 0.684 | 5.3 % | 4.7 % | 1.027 | **6.00** | **72.1** | – | – | 7.42 |
| A all nodes r64, s504 | 0.584 | 8.1 % | 9.9 % | 1.005 | 3.67 | 86.7 | −2.33 ± 0.47 | +14.6 ± 6.1 | 7.33 |
| A all nodes r64, s1500 | 0.569 | 9.9 % | 11.2 % | 0.936 | <u>4.33</u> | 78.8 | <u>−1.67 ± 0.54</u> | +6.7 ± 5.0 | 7.67 |
| A all nodes r64, s2004 | 0.567 | <u>10.7 %</u> | 11.5 % | 0.985 | 3.67 | 82.5 | −2.33 ± 0.53 | +10.4 ± 8.7 | 7.42 |
| B nodes 0-1 r64, s2004 | **0.558** | **10.8 %** | **11.7 %** | 0.988 | 3.33 | 83.3 | −2.67 ± 0.43 | +11.2 ± 6.2 | 7.67 |
| B nodes 0-1 r64, s3000 | 0.569 | 8.1 % | 9.6 % | **0.910** | 3.17 | 86.7 | −2.83 ± 0.51 | +14.6 ± 6.9 | 7.67 |
| C all nodes r128, s1008 | 0.578 | 9.3 % | 10.0 % | <u>0.934</u> | 3.83 | 77.9 | −2.17 ± 0.53 | +5.8 ± 8.5 | 7.25 |
| C all nodes r128, s2004 | <u>0.564</u> | 10.3 % | 11.5 % | 1.015 | 4.00 | <u>75.8</u> | −2.00 ± 0.51 | <u>+3.8 ± 7.4</u> | 7.50 |
| reference: 14B own step 1 | 0.37 | 32.4 % | – | – | – | – | – | – | – |
(gap cells = mean over chunks 1-12 and 12 scenes; eps = 0.24; VLM = Qwen3-VL-32B clip judge, quality 1-10, drift 0-100, 8 frames; teacher control confirms the judge is stable across runs.)
Per chunk (anchored 4-step within eps, chunks 1..12, mean over scenes): stock 5 5 3 5 4 5 6 6 7 5 7 8; A s1500 14 8 6 10 6 11 9 11 9 13 11 12; B s2004 13 11 9 11 7 11 11 14 11 14 7 11 — the gain is uniform over the chunk index (still capacity-flat), largest at chunk 1 (13-15 %).
Reading: (i) distillation moves the anchored gap from 0.68 to 0.56-0.58 sigma_T and the acceptable share from 5 % to 8-11 % on unseen scenes — a 2x relative gain that is still 3x short of the 14B's own one-step estimate (32 %) and far from the >= 30 % bar set before the run. The validation loss on training scenes halves, so the LoRA fits the training scenes; it does not transfer as acceptance. (ii) The decoded student clip (own history) collapses: quality 6.0 → 3.2-4.3, drift 72 → 76-87, on 10-11 of 12 scenes (figs f16: smeared / ghosted textures on domino, pouring, cradle; subject lost on easy_lake). This is the 9/26 failure again — x0-MSE regression against a single teacher sample averages the teacher's distribution and removes texture — and it happens even in variant B, which never puts a loss on the texture nodes (the LoRA weights are shared by all nodes, so the layout-node fit changes the texture nodes too). (iii) More steps or rank do not help: B s3000 is worse than s2004 on every column, C r128 ≈ A r64.
Consequence: the separate-drafter line is CLOSED. Three levers were tried on the 1.3B as a proposal for the 14B: speed (§20: 3.4x real time at student quality), handoff/anchoring (§21: 6 %, flat = capacity) and distillation (§22: 11 % and a blurred draft). A usable separate drafter would need distribution-matching distillation (DMD / adversarial, as the Fast models themselves were trained) with the 14B as teacher — a training project, not a lever to test in a day — and even then it starts from 1.05 sigma_T on its own history. The 14B's self-speculation (step-1 estimate 32-34 % acceptable, 56 % at step 2, 81 % at step 3) stays the proposal mechanism in the paper; the 1.3B appears only as the "student / edge-only" baseline.
Costs for the record: data dump 224 clips (6.7 GB), training 3 x 173 min on one GPU each, evaluation 25 min per checkpoint (12 scenes) + VLM 3 min.

## 23. Sub-chunk feasibility: 1- and 2-latent-frame chunks on the stock Fast 14B (DONE 10/13; lever 4 of the self-speculation improvement list; specwm/chunk1_probe.py, data/chunk1_vlm.json, figs/f17_chunk1_*.jpg, clips lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/chunk1_probe/<task>_s7/cs{1,2,3}/spec.mp4)
Question: the action-to-frame latency floor is the chunk length (3 latent frames = 0.75 s; realtime mode p50 1.30 s, no config meets 750 ms). Does the Fast 14B, trained with 3-frame blocks, still work when asked to denoise 1 or 2 latent frames per chunk (0.25 / 0.5 s)? Setup: the 12 held-out scenes of §22, seed 7, stock 14B, 4 steps, deployed stack (FP8 / Sage / compile / ring KV, sink 6 frames, window 18 frames), prepare(chunk_size = 1 / 2 / 3); one PRO 6000 (GPU 2, other GPUs busy with training). Timing per chunk is the median over scenes of the steady-state mean (a few scenes hit a recompile fallback — max column).
| chunk size (latent frames / video s) | chunks per 161-frame clip | s per chunk (4 steps) median [max] ↓ | s per video-second ↓ | first-chunk s ↓ | VLM quality ↑ | VLM drift ↓ | Δ quality vs 3-frame (paired, n = 12) ↑ | Δ drift ↓ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 / 0.25 s | 41 | **0.99** [2.43] | 3.96 | **0.56** | 6.83 | 64.6 | −0.33 ± 0.14 | +8.8 ± 9.4 |
| 2 / 0.50 s | 20 | <u>1.76</u> [3.10] | <u>3.53</u> | <u>1.12</u> | **7.42** | <u>62.9</u> | +0.25 ± 0.13 | +7.1 ± 7.3 |
| 3 / 0.75 s (trained) | 13 | 2.46 [3.50] | **3.28** | 1.68 | <u>7.17</u> | **55.8** | – | – |
Reading: (i) the model does run with 1- and 2-frame chunks and the clips are sharp (figs f17); the judge sees −0.33 quality / +9 drift (n.s.) at 1 frame and no quality loss at 2 frames. By eye the 1-frame rollouts lose scene content sooner (domino table gone by mid-clip, lake camera dives into the water), which the 8-frame judge under-weights — a human/blind check is needed before using cs1 for quality claims. (ii) Compute per video-second rises only 8 % (2 frames) / 21 % (1 frame), so smaller chunks are not a throughput problem. (iii) Latency: with 1-frame chunks the first chunk is ready in 0.56 s (4 steps) and each later chunk in 0.99 s for 0.25 s of video; with the adaptive depth (mean 2.9 steps) or the 2-step realtime mode this is the first configuration whose action-to-frame latency can fall under 750 ms (0.25 s chunk + ~0.5 s compute at 2 steps; to be measured with the latency protocol of §15). Caveat: GPU 2 was shared with nothing but the box had four training jobs on other GPUs; absolute timings are ~15 % above the quiet §4 numbers.
Consequence: lever 4 is feasible without retraining; the next step is a 2-step / adaptive-depth run at chunk size 1-2 through the latency harness (TTFF, action-to-frame p50) and a blind check of the content drift at 1 frame.

## 24. Self-distilling the 14B's first step (lever 1 of the self-speculation improvement list; DONE 10/13, CLOSED for regression losses; specwm/self_distill14.py, specwm/self_gap.py, scripts/selfd_eval_report.py, data/selfd14_eval.json, figs/f18_selfd14_pouring.jpg; runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/selfd14/<run>/lora_s<step>.pt, eval specwm_out/selfd14_eval/<run>_s<step>/<task>_s7/{stats.json, teacher|stock1|lora1/spec.mp4})
Idea: the adaptive depth is bounded by how close the 14B's step-1 x0 estimate is to its 4-step output (34 % of tokens within eps, §21); a LoRA that is active ONLY at node 0 (gated by a scalar; nodes 1-3 and the commit pass untouched, so the §22 failure mode "node-shared LoRA changes the texture nodes" cannot occur) is trained to move the step-1 estimate toward the 14B's own later output. Data: the 224 target_dump clips of §22 (14B x0 at all four nodes, paired noise, teacher-forced context); LoRA r64 on q,k,v,o,ffn0,ffn2 of all 40 blocks (202M params), relative-MSE on the step-1 x0 against node 3 (final; T3), node 2 (T2) or node 1 (T1); 12 clips held out for the validation loss; ~6 s / step, 92 GiB peak (T5 offloaded between clips), one PRO 6000 per run.
Training: lr 1e-4 diverges on the 14B (all three targets: val within-eps of final 44.5 → 37-42 % at step 252, train loss above the start) — stopped; lr 3e-5 fits (T3: val loss 0.135 → 0.107, within-eps 44.5 → 48.9 % @252 → 50.7 % @504; T2 50.2 %, T1 50.4 % @504).
Held-out test (12 scenes of §22, seed 7, stock 14B rollout as the teacher-forced reference; "stock step-k" = the 14B's own estimates on the same runs; 1-step clip = a rollout that commits the step-1 estimate at EVERY chunk on its own history, the worst case for blur; VLM clip judge, quality 1-10, drift 0-100):
| variant (node-0 LoRA, lr 3e-5) | stock step-1 within eps (ref) | stock step-2 | stock step-3 | LoRA step-1 gap sigma_T ↓ | LoRA step-1 within eps ↑ | LoRA-first full 4-step within eps of stock final | LoRA step-1 vs stock step-1 (sigma_T) | 1-step clip quality: stock / LoRA ↑ | Δ quality (paired) ↑ | 1-step clip drift: stock / LoRA ↓ | Δ drift ↓ | teacher clip (control) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| T3 (target = final), step 252 | 38.3 % | 58.1 % | 82.1 % | 0.321 | 39.5 % | 59.7 % | 0.248 | 6.00 / 3.50 | −2.50 ± 0.36 | 64.2 / 77.1 | +12.9 ± 4.5 | 7.50 / 58.3 |
| T3, step 504 | 38.3 % | 58.1 % | 82.1 % | 0.325 | 40.1 % | 57.5 % | 0.258 | 6.00 / 3.33 | −2.67 ± 0.45 | 65.0 / 79.2 | +14.2 ± 4.4 | 7.50 / 58.3 |
| T2 (target = 3-step output), step 252 | 38.3 % | 58.1 % | 82.1 % | 0.320 | 40.4 % | 60.6 % | 0.243 | 6.00 / <u>3.75</u> | <u>−2.25 ± 0.64</u> | 65.0 / 80.4 | +15.4 ± 4.0 | 7.50 / 58.3 |
| T1 (target = 2-step output), step 252 | 38.3 % | 58.1 % | 82.1 % | **0.318** | **40.4 %** | **62.1 %** | 0.233 | 6.00 / 3.33 | −2.67 ± 0.38 | 65.0 / 79.6 | +14.6 ± 4.3 | 7.50 / 58.3 |
(eps = 0.24; gap columns = mean over chunks 1-12 and 12 scenes. The stock 1-step-everywhere clip scores 6.00 / 65 — the same level as the 1.3B student, i.e. the 14B's first step alone is a "student".)
Reading: (i) on unseen scenes the step-1 acceptance moves 38.3 → 39.5-40.4 % (+1-2 points) whatever the target node, although the LoRA moves 45 % of the step-1 tokens (0.23-0.26 sigma_T from the stock step 1) and the in-distribution validation gains 5-6 points: the fit is scene-specific. (ii) The decoded 1-step rollouts collapse (quality 6.0 → 3.3-3.8, drift +13-15, 11 of 12 scenes; fig f18: smeared, texture-free frames) — the 9/26 and §22 failure again, now on the 14B itself and with the LoRA confined to the first step. The mechanism is the loss, not the model: the step-1 estimate cannot know the noise drawn at steps 2-4, so the MSE-optimal first step is the conditional MEAN of the later outputs, which has no texture; a less stochastic target (T1 = the 2-step output) does not escape it. (iii) The LoRA-first full 4-step path lands 57-62 % within eps of the stock final, i.e. the changed first step also moves the final sample — the LoRA is not "free" for the rejected chunks either.
Consequence: lever 1 cannot be done with a regression loss. Raising the step-1 acceptance needs a distribution-matching objective (DMD / adversarial, the way the Fast models were made) or a different schedule, i.e. a training project with its own evaluation; not pursued now. The adaptive-depth numbers of §21 (34 / 56 / 81 % at steps 1-3) remain the proposal statistics in the paper. Levers 2 (quality-aware acceptance) and 4 (§23, sub-chunk latency) stay open.
Cost: 4 + 3 training runs (lr 1e-4 ones stopped at ~400 steps, lr 3e-5 ones at 500-750 steps), 4 held-out evaluations (30-35 min each), 11 GB of checkpoints in specwm_out/selfd14 (not deleted).

## 25. Lever 2 — acceptance threshold of the chunk-wise rule (12 held-out scenes DONE 10/13; bench-43 confirmation running; specwm/spec_early.py diag:learned_dense --chunk-frac F --commit safe, runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/lever2/<task>/cw<F>_safe_s7/, VLM lever2/lever2_vlm.json, data/lever2_threshold.json)
Question: §21 showed that the distance to the 4-step output does not predict judged quality, so the teacher-parity rule (commit the chunk once >= 70 % of its tokens are predicted within eps) may be more conservative than the judge needs. Same gap head, same eps = 0.24, same safe commit; only the chunk fraction F is swept. 12 held-out scenes of §22, seed 7, 832x480, 161 frames; teacher reference = the stock 4-step clip of the same seed (self_gap teacher clip, identical noise path), 1-step extreme = the self_gap stock1 clip. Quality/drift = VLM clip judge (8 frames).
| arm | mean steps ↓ | stop-step share 1/2/3/4 (%) | VLM quality ↑ | VLM drift ↓ | Δ quality vs teacher (paired, n = 12) ↑ | Δ drift ↓ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| teacher 4-step | 4.00 | 0/0/0/100 | <u>7.50</u> | 58.3 | – | – |
| chunk-frac 0.7 (teacher-parity mode) | 3.03 | 2/12/67/19 | **7.58** | 61.2 | **+0.08 ± 0.23** | +2.9 ± 6.8 |
| chunk-frac 0.5 | 2.44 | 8/45/44/4 | 7.42 | <u>59.6</u> | <u>−0.08 ± 0.15</u> | +1.2 ± 5.9 |
| chunk-frac 0.3 | 2.03 | 23/51/26/0 | 7.25 | **50.0** | −0.25 ± 0.22 | **−8.3 ± 5.7** |
| chunk-frac 0.1 | 1.38 | 64/34/2/0 | 6.42 | 55.4 | −1.08 ± 0.42 | −2.9 ± 6.3 |
| 1 step every chunk | 1.00 | 100/0/0/0 | 6.00 | 64.2 | −1.50 ± 0.45 | +5.8 ± 7.8 |
Reading: (i) F = 0.5 is at teacher quality (−0.08 ± 0.15) with 2.44 steps instead of 3.03 — on the §15 timing this is the 1.97× row instead of 1.67×, i.e. the parity mode can be ~18 % faster at no measured cost. (ii) F = 0.3 (2.03 steps, 1/4 of chunks at step 1) costs −0.25 ± 0.22, the same as the fixed 2-step quality mode (−0.30, §2) but with LOWER drift (50 vs 58), because the hard chunks still get 3 steps. (iii) Below that the first-step share dominates and quality falls to the 1-step floor (6.0 = the 1.3B student level). (iv) The rule degrades gracefully: quality is monotone in mean steps, so F is a single knob between parity (0.5–0.7) and the 2-step quality mode (0.3). Caveats: n = 12, one seed, judge noise ±0.2; bench-43 seed-7 runs of F = 0.7 / 0.5 / 0.3 and the teacher are running (specwm_out/lever2_b43/) to confirm before the paper number is changed. Not done: a drift-head gate on top of the gap rule — the 10/10 result (redrawing head-flagged chunks with 4 steps does not reduce drift) says the drift head cannot buy steps either way, so the sweep above is the whole lever.

### 23b. Sub-chunk latency pass (DONE 10/13; §13 protocol: jar + sandcastle x seeds 1234/4321/9999, per-task warm-up, one PRO 6000 per run with GPUs 0/1/5/6 as a pool, streaming full-VAE decode on the same GPU, prepare excluded; specwm/spec_early.py --chunk-size/--area (added 10/13; the uniform path now feeds the stream decoder), raw lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/timing_cs/<task>/<arm>_s<seed>/loop.json, summaries timing_cs/latency/<arm>/summary.json = data/timing_cs/<arm>.json)

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | 832 | chunk-wise 0.7, safe (teacher-parity) | 3.12 | 2637 | 3116 | 3510 | 100 % | 5.3 | 2401 | 1124 | 2.368 | 3.16 |
| 3 | 832 | 2-step | 2 | 2416 | 2809 | 2935 | 100 % | 5.8 | 2194 | 1085 | 2.264 | 3.02 |

| 2 | 832 | chunk-wise 0.7, safe | 2.59 | 1985 | 2300 | 2676 | 99.4 % | 5.2 | 1590 | 623 | 1.637 | 3.27 |
| 2 | 832 | 2-step | 2 | 1862 | 2151 | 2155 | 99.7 % | 5.4 | 1510 | 745 | 1.591 | 3.18 |

| 1 | 832 | 2-step | 2 | 1076 | 1163 | 1165 | 95.7 % | 4.9 | 816 | 419 | 0.885 | 3.54 |
| 1 | 704 | 2-step | 2 | **655** | <u>744</u> | **745** | **0.4 %** | 7.8 | **514** | **288** | 0.547 | 2.19 |
(3-frame 704 2-step reproduces §13's c704_sep: 9.2 vs 9.3 fps, p50 1547 vs 1484 ms, so the pass is comparable with §13. 1-frame chunks are less efficient per video-second (+16-21 %) because each forward is ~0.2 s at 1560 tokens and the stream decode competes for the GPU.)
Reading: (i) action-to-frame latency is roughly linear in the chunk length at fixed mode, as the theory memo predicts (704 2-step: 1547 → 1174 → 655 ms for 3 → 2 → 1 frames). (ii) 1-frame chunks at 704x384 with 2 steps is the FIRST configuration under the 750 ms target: p50 655 ms, p99 745 ms, 0.4 % misses, first frame 288 ms. (iii) Its output rate is 7.8 fps, below real-time playback (16 fps): the frames arrive 0.65 s after their action but only half as fast as they are consumed, so this mode is low-latency, not real-time, on one PRO 6000; the throughput-best sub-chunk option is 2-frame 704 (8.8 fps, p50 1174 ms). (iv) Quality of the 1-/2-frame 704 2-step modes is being measured on the 12 held-out scenes (specwm_out/lever4q/); expect roughly the sum of the 1-frame penalty (§23, −0.33) and the realtime-mode penalty (−0.62).
Quality of the sub-chunk 704 modes (added 10/13; 12 held-out scenes of §22, seed 7, with video; paired vs the stock 4-step 832 teacher clip of the same seed; data/lever4_quality.json, clips lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/lever4q/<task>/<arm>_s7/video.mp4):
| arm | mean steps | VLM quality ↑ | VLM drift ↓ | Δ quality vs teacher 832 4-step (paired, n = 12) ↑ | Δ drift ↓ | latency p50 (table above) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3-frame, 704, 2-step (= realtime mode, separate commit) | 2 | <u>6.83</u> | **53.8** | <u>−0.67 ± 0.19</u> | **−4.6 ± 6.7** | 1547 ms |
| 2-frame, 704, 2-step | 2 | **7.08** | **53.8** | **−0.42 ± 0.29** | **−4.6 ± 9.1** | 1174 ms |
| 1-frame, 704, 2-step | 2 | 6.67 | 65.0 | −0.83 ± 0.24 | +6.7 ± 5.8 | **655 ms** |
| 1-frame, 704, chunk-wise 0.5 safe | 1.81 | 6.67 | 55.8 | −0.83 ± 0.21 | −2.5 ± 5.1 | – (not timed) |
Reading: the 2-frame chunk costs nothing against the 3-frame realtime mode (−0.42 vs −0.67, same drift) and cuts the latency by 24 %; the 1-frame chunk costs a further −0.4 and +11 drift for the 750 ms target. So the paper can carry a 4th row "low-latency mode (1-frame, 704, 2-step): p50 655 ms, −0.83 ± 0.24", and the realtime mode itself should move to 2-frame chunks (p50 1174 ms, −0.42). Caveat: n = 12, one seed, judge noise ±0.2–0.3; bench-43 confirmation not yet run for the sub-chunk arms.
