|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| teacher 4-step | 4.00 | 0/0/0/100 | **7.51** | **45.0** | – | – | – |
| chunk-frac 0.7 (teacher-parity mode) | 3.02 | 3/11/67/19 | <u>7.44</u> | <u>52.3</u> | **−0.07 ± 0.11** | **+7.3 ± 4.0** | 10 / 25 / 8 |
| chunk-frac 0.5 | 2.53 | 7/38/50/5 | 7.30 | 54.3 | <u>−0.21 ± 0.09</u> | +9.3 ± 4.0 | 12 / 28 / 3 |
| chunk-frac 0.3 | 2.09 | 20/53/25/2 | 7.16 | 53.3 | −0.35 ± 0.10 | <u>+8.3 ± 4.2</u> | 15 / 26 / 2 |
Reading (supersedes the 12-scene reading above): on bench-43 the threshold is NOT free. 0.7 reproduces the 10/12 teacher-parity result (−0.07 ± 0.11; bench-43 10/12: −0.07 under load, +0.03 ± 0.07 with the bench-free head); 0.5 costs −0.21 ± 0.09 at 2.53 steps and 0.3 costs −0.35 ± 0.10 at 2.09 steps, i.e. the same cost as the fixed 2-step quality mode (−0.30 at 2.00 steps, §2) — the chunk-wise rule interpolates smoothly between the parity and quality modes but does not beat the fixed 2-step line on quality per step. The 12-scene "0.5 for free" was sample noise (n = 12, ±0.15). Drift: all three arms sit +7–9 above the teacher's 45 (the §2 bench-43 teacher-parity drift was +3.4); the per-scene judge noise is ±4. Decision: the paper keeps F = 0.7 as the parity mode; F is reported as the knob of a speed/quality curve (figure: mean steps vs Δquality with the fixed 2-step point on it). Lever 2 is closed with no change to the modes.

## 26. Temporal warm-start draft (proposal C, 10/13) — CLOSED offline
Question: can the previous latent frame (or the previous chunk) serve as a free step-1 estimate, so the 14B starts at node 1 and saves one forward per chunk? Offline on the 224 target_dump clips of §22 (2,688 chunks), within-eps (0.24 sigma_T) of the 4-step final chunk:
| candidate draft for chunk c | within eps (all 3 frames) | frame 0 of the chunk | frame 2 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 14B own step-1 estimate | **42.1 %** | **64.8 %** | **23.5 %** |
| previous latent frame repeated x3 | 3.1 % | 4.8 % | 1.8 % |
| previous chunk copied | 1.7 % | – | – |
| tokens within 0.1 sigma_T of the previous frame ("static") | 0.2 % | – | – |
Reading: in the causal VAE latent, consecutive frames are far apart in token space even for slow camera motion (0.2 % of tokens are static), so the temporal prior is useless as a draft and the 14B's own first step remains the only cheap estimate. Side result: the step-1 estimate is much better on the first latent frame of a chunk (65 %) than on the third (24 %) — the within-chunk saw-tooth of §21 seen from the proposal side; a per-frame acceptance threshold could exploit it (not tried).
Also measured (proposal A, offline): the 90 tasks_camera tasks use only 8 distinct pose files (81 tasks share one trajectory), so StEvo cannot measure action predictability of a human stream; on those 8 trajectories constant-velocity extrapolation over one 0.75 s chunk has median translation error 45 % of the chunk motion (40 % of chunks within 10 %, 53 % within 50 %), rotation error median 2.3 deg, p90 57 deg (the scripted paths have sharp turns). The model-side "action tolerance" is measured in §27.

## 27. Action tolerance of the Fast 14B (proposal A, measurement 2; DONE 10/13; specwm/action_tol.py, scripts/action_tol_report.py, data/action_tol.json, runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/action_tol/<task>_s7/stats.json)
Question: if the next action is speculated (branch computed before the user's action arrives), how close must the guess be for the pre-computed chunk to pass as the true-action chunk? Setup: 12 held-out scenes, seed 7, stock 14B 4-step, teacher-forced history from the true-action rollout; at chunks 2/4/6/8/10 the chunk is regenerated from the same z0 and noise with the chunk's framewise relative camera poses perturbed (normalized pose space, so no renormalization confound; Plücker embedding rebuilt for the 3 latent frames only, reproduction of prepare() checked to 1e-3). Gap = token distance to the true-action chunk; floor = the same action with another noise (facts §21's sample-to-sample spread).
| perturbation of the chunk's action | gap σ_T ↓ | within ε ↑ | chunks at/above the other-noise floor ↑ |
|---|---|---|---|
| same action, other noise (floor) | 0.462 | 29.5 % | 100 % |
| **constant-velocity extrapolation** from the previous chunk (the speculative predictor) | 0.291 | **59.2 %** | **80 %** |
| stop (identity motion) | 0.969 | 4.5 % | 5 % |
| translation × 0.5 | 0.028 | 96.4 % | 97 % |
| translation × 2 | 0.038 | 94.6 % | 95 % |
| extra yaw 10° over the chunk | 0.644 | 9.6 % | 17 % |
| extra yaw 30° over the chunk | 0.836 | 3.1 % | 5 % |
(60 chunks; per-scene detail in the stats files: on the shared StEvo trajectory the extrapolation fails only at chunk 10, where the scripted path turns, 12–32 % there vs 55–97 % elsewhere; easy_lake / urban_rain have their own paths with turns at chunks 4–6.)
Reading: (i) The model is nearly blind to the translation magnitude of a chunk (×0.5 / ×2 leave 95 % of tokens within ε; most StEvo chunks are pure rotation, median translation 0) but sharply sensitive to rotation: 10° of extra yaw over 0.75 s already moves the chunk beyond the sample-to-sample spread (9.6 % vs 29.5 %), and stopping the camera is a completely different chunk (4.5 %). So the "action tolerance" for speculation is a rotation tolerance of a few degrees per chunk; translation guesses are free. (ii) The constant-velocity predictor produces a chunk that is as close to the true-action chunk as another noise sample on 80 % of the chunks (59 % of tokens within ε, above the 29.5 % floor) — on these smooth scripted paths an action-speculative branch would be accepted ~80 % of the time at zero added latency and teacher quality. (iii) The limiting factor is not the model but the action stream: StEvo/tasks_camera has only 8 distinct pose files for 90 tasks (§26), so the 80 % is the predictability of a scripted path, not of a human. A human-like stream (piecewise-constant velocity with change points, or recorded trajectories from a game/teleop dataset) is needed to state a hit rate; the model-side tolerance above is what makes the design feasible.
Implication for the paper (proposal A + B): action-speculative branching is feasible on the model side (K branches of the next chunk with rotation hypotheses within ±5°; hit → 0 added latency, teacher quality; miss → generate as now) and combines with the sub-chunk mode for the misses (§23b: 1-frame 704 2-step p50 655 ms after an unpredicted action change, 3-frame chunks while the extrapolation holds). Both need an action-stream benchmark with change points; not built yet.

### 27b. Finer calibration (added 10/13 evening; specwm/action_tol.py --fine 1, runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/action_tol2/<task>_s7/stats.json, data/action_tol2.json; same 12 scenes x 5 chunks, same protocol)
| perturbation of the chunk's action | gap σ_T ↓ | within ε ↑ | chunks at/above the other-noise floor ↑ |
|---|---|---|---|
| same action, other noise (floor) | 0.462 | 29.5 % | 100 % |
| extra yaw 2° over the chunk | 0.367 | 33.7 % | 70 % |
| extra yaw 5° | 0.517 | 17.6 % | 42 % |
| extra yaw 10° | 0.644 | 9.6 % | 17 % |
| translation removed, rotation kept (trans0) | 0.042 | 94.5 % | 95 % |
| forward motion ADDED at full speed, rotation kept (transfwd) | 0.615 | 12.0 % | 23 % |
CORRECTION to §27 (i): only 10 of the 60 chunks have any translation (the shared StEvo path is a pure rotation after chunk 1), so "translation x0.5 / x2 leaves 95 % within ε" was measured mostly on chunks whose translation is 0 (a no-op). On the 10 moving chunks: x0.5 78 %, x2 68 %, translation removed 67 % (n = 10, all at/above the 29.5 % floor), while adding forward motion to a static chunk gives 10–20 %. So the model tolerates the translation MAGNITUDE (x0.5..x2) but not switching motion on/off; speculation must get the motion state right and can be loose on speed. Rotation tolerance: 2° over a chunk (0.7°/latent frame) is still at the floor (70 % of chunks), 5° is below it (42 %) → the hit test of specwm/act_spec.py uses ≤ 3° summed over the chunk's frames and a RELATIVE translation error ≤ 0.5 (on/off = 1 fails, x0.5 / x2 = 0.5 passes).

## 28. Action-speculative generation (proposals A + B) on a synthetic action-stream benchmark (10/13 evening; specwm/act_stream.py, specwm/act_spec.py, specwm/act_spec2.py, scripts/act_spec_report.py, data/actspec*.json, figs/f19_actspec.png; runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/actspec/<task>/<stream>/K<k>_<arm>_s7/ and actspec2/...)
Benchmark (data/actstream/<family>_s<seed>/): one relative SE(3) per latent frame (0.25 s), key-press model: velocity (yaw rate, pitch rate, forward speed, strafe) piecewise constant; segment length 1 + Poisson(4τ − 1) frames with τ = 1 / 2 / 4 s (change-point density axis); at a change point one component changes with p = 0.75 (a held key is released with p = 0.5), else all four are redrawn; p(still) = 0.15; value sets: yaw {0, ±4, ±8, ±12.65}°/frame (StEvo's turn = 12.65°/frame), pitch {0, ±3}, speed {0, 0.5, 1} (1 = StEvo 'straight'), strafe {0, ±0.5}. Family tau2j03 = τ 2 s + Gaussian jitter 0.3°/frame on both rates (hand on a mouse: 'same action' hits become inexact). 3 seeds per family (s0–s2), 6 held-out scenes (easy_lake, domino_falling, pouring_water_into_cup, billiard_break, traffic_light_green, seesaw_balance), seed 7.
Method (act_spec.py): before the next chunk's action is known the generator computes the chunk under K hypotheses — 'same' = last relative pose repeated (constant velocity) and, for K ≥ 2, the K−1 most likely other key states under a first-order transition prior fitted on 300 fresh streams of the same family (never the test stream) — each branch = denoise + commit + snapshot of the chunk's K/V ring slot + speculative VAE decode (decoder cache snapshot/restore). When the true action is read ('request' time, as in §13), a branch whose action is within 3° (sum of per-frame rotation errors over the chunk; §27b calibration: 2° at the floor, 5° below) and relative translation error ≤ 0.5 (scale ×0.5..×2 passes, motion on/off fails) is emitted at once (K/V slot copied back; frames already decoded); otherwise the chunk is generated with the true action as usual (the branches were wasted). K = 0 = baseline on the same stream, same noise, same synchronous decode. Oracle: for every hit the true-action chunk is also generated from the same noise (excluded from the timing) → token gap / within-ε of the emitted branch (the §21/§27 measure). Timing: one PRO 6000, decode synchronous on the same GPU in all arms (so the K = 0 p50 ≈ §13's teacher 3.24 s at 832 and §23b's 1.55 s at 704 2-step), prepare excluded; output fps = (frames − 1) / (last − first ready).
B (act_spec2.py, --adaptive 1): frame-granular K/V ring (fast_patch FLAGS ring_frame; verified bitwise identical to the chunk ring on 7 fixed chunks, specwm/ring_frame_check.py) and the model called per frame range; after a miss on a 3-frame unit only ONE frame is regenerated with the true action (latency / 3) and the stream continues in 1-frame units (speculated too) until 3 consecutive hits, then 3-frame units again. Its baselines (K0 / K1 fixed) are re-run under the same code (dynamic-shape compile; numerically different from act_spec.py's static compile, which the AR loop amplifies to different content after a few chunks — all comparisons are paired within one code path).
Results (DONE 10/14 00:50 UTC; 6 scenes x 3 stream seeds = 18 clips per cell for A, 12 (seeds s0, s1) for B; VLM judge quality 1-10 / drift 0-100 on the full clip, paired with the K = 0 run of the same scene x stream; latency per output frame):
A, 832 4-step (teacher quality), fixed 3-frame chunks — data/actspec_832_u4.json:
| stream family | K | hit rate ↑ | action→frame mean ms ↓ | hit chunks ↓ | miss chunks ↓ | output fps ↑ | generations / chunk ↓ | oracle within ε on hits ↑ | quality Δ vs K0 | drift Δ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| τ 1 s | 0 | – | 3119 | – | – | **4.1** | **1.00** | – | – | – |
| τ 1 s | 1 | 61 % | 1252 | **6** | 2988 | 2.9 | 1.36 | 87 % | +0.06 ± 0.12 | +3.6 |
| τ 1 s | 2 | 69 % | 1012 | **6** | 3016 | 1.8 | 2.21 | 84 % | +0.11 ± 0.07 | +0.0 |
| τ 2 s | 1 | 78 % | 776 | **6** | 3084 | 3.4 | 1.21 | 94 % | −0.22 ± 0.17 | +4.7 |
| τ 2 s | 2 | 78 % | 776 | **6** | 3083 | 2.0 | 2.05 | 93 % | −0.22 ± 0.19 | +1.1 |
| τ 2 s | 4 (no spec. decode: OOM at 832) | 81 % | 1223 | 653 | 3103 | 1.5 | 3.26 | 91 % | −0.33 ± 0.19 | +3.6 |
| τ 4 s | 1 | 86 % | 539 | **6** | 3123 | 3.6 | 1.13 | 100 % | −0.11 ± 0.07 | +1.4 |
| τ 4 s | 2 | 89 % | 456 | **6** | 3106 | 2.0 | 2.03 | 98 % | −0.06 ± 0.10 | +1.4 |
| τ 2 s + jitter 0.3° | 1 | 72 % | 923 | **6** | 3033 | 3.2 | 1.26 | 55 % | −0.17 ± 0.09 | −1.9 |
| τ 2 s + jitter 0.3° | 2 | 72 % | 924 | **6** | 3036 | 1.9 | 2.10 | 53 % | −0.11 ± 0.13 | +1.9 |
(K = 0 rows of the other families: mean 2991 ms, 4.2 fps. K0 quality 7.61 / 7.72 / 8.28 / 7.56, drift 50.8 / 67.5 / 24.2 / 68.1.)
A, 704 2-step (realtime mode), fixed 3-frame chunks — data/actspec_704_u2.json: K0 mean 1326–1391 ms, 9.2–9.4 fps; K1: τ 1 s 61 % hits, mean 563 ms, hit 4 ms, miss 1332 ms, 6.6 fps, 1.36 gen/chunk, oracle 87 %, Δq +0.06 ± 0.10, Δdrift −3.6; τ 2 s 78 %, 348 ms, 7.6 fps, 1.21, 93 %, +0.17 ± 0.12, −1.1; τ 4 s 86 %, 243 ms, 8.1 fps, 1.13, 100 %, +0.00, −0.6; jitter 72 %, 414 ms, 7.3 fps, 1.26, 52 %, +0.06 ± 0.12, +7.5.
B, adaptive chunk length (act_spec2, paired within the dynamic-compile code path; 12 clips per cell; data/actspec2_*.json, paired quality from scratch script → quoted here):
| arm | family | hit rate ↑ | mean ms ↓ | hit ↓ | miss ↓ | fps ↑ | gen / chunk ↓ | 1-frame units (share of frames) | quality Δ vs K0 fixed | drift Δ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 832 K1 fixed | τ 1 s | 67 % | 1110 | 8 | 3105 | 3.0 | 1.31 | 0 % | −0.18 ± 0.12 | +4.1 ± 4.1 |
| 832 K1 adaptive | τ 1 s | 74 % | **341** | 6 | **1206** | 2.7 | 1.25 | 49 % | −0.18 ± 0.12 | +13.2 ± 6.7 |
| 832 K1 fixed | τ 2 s | 75 % | 896 | 8 | 3225 | 3.2 | 1.23 | 0 % | −0.25 ± 0.12 | −5.4 ± 3.6 |
| 832 K1 adaptive | τ 2 s | 77 % | **301** | 6 | **1259** | 2.9 | 1.22 | 36 % | −0.25 ± 0.17 | −1.7 ± 1.1 |
| 832 K1 fixed | τ 4 s | 88 % | 517 | 8 | 3223 | 3.6 | 1.12 | 0 % | −0.08 ± 0.08 | +0.0 |
| 832 K1 adaptive | τ 4 s | 88 % | **203** | 7 | **1250** | 3.4 | 1.11 | 18 % | +0.09 ± 0.09 | +1.4 ± 8.4 |
| 832 K1 fixed | jitter | 71 % | 1025 | 8 | 3234 | 3.1 | 1.27 | 0 % | −0.40 ± 0.21 | +9.0 ± 5.9 |
| 832 K1 adaptive | jitter | 70 % | **370** | 6 | **1275** | 2.7 | 1.29 | 42 % | −0.30 ± 0.28 | +13.5 ± 6.0 |
| 704 K1 fixed | τ 1 s | 67 % | 500 | 6 | 1385 | 6.8 | 1.31 | 0 % | −0.10 ± 0.09 | −3.5 ± 9.0 |
| 704 K1 adaptive | τ 1 s | 74 % | **153** | 4 | **520** | 6.1 | 1.25 | 49 % | −0.70 ± 0.20 | +14.5 ± 10.2 |
| 704 K1 fixed | τ 2 s | 75 % | 401 | 6 | 1425 | 7.2 | 1.23 | 0 % | +0.10 ± 0.09 | −4.5 ± 7.7 |
| 704 K1 adaptive | τ 2 s | 77 % | **136** | 5 | **537** | 6.5 | 1.22 | 36 % | +0.00 ± 0.14 | +9.0 ± 5.7 |
| 704 K1 fixed | τ 4 s | 88 % | 234 | 6 | 1426 | 8.0 | 1.12 | 0 % | +0.10 ± 0.09 | +0.0 |
| 704 K1 adaptive | τ 4 s | 88 % | **94** | 5 | **535** | 7.7 | 1.11 | 18 % | +0.00 ± 0.14 | +1.5 ± 5.4 |
| 704 K1 fixed | jitter | 71 % | 458 | 6 | 1428 | 6.9 | 1.27 | 0 % | +0.22 ± 0.14 | +8.9 ± 5.7 |
| 704 K1 adaptive | jitter | 70 % | **164** | 5 | **542** | 6.2 | 1.29 | 42 % | −0.22 ± 0.14 | +11.1 ± 9.0 |
(K0 fixed: 832 mean 3080 ms, 4.0 fps; 704 mean 1372 ms, 9.1 fps.)
Reading: (i) Input-side speculation works as designed: a hit costs the K/V copy (4–8 ms) and the pre-decoded frames go out at once; a miss costs exactly what the baseline costs; the mean action→frame latency therefore scales with the miss rate (832: 3.0 s → 1.25 / 0.78 / 0.54 s at τ = 1 / 2 / 4 s). Hit rate = how often the next 0.75 s of input is the constant-velocity continuation of the last pose (within 3° and a translation factor of 2): 61 / 78 / 86 %; misses are almost all chunks that contain a change point (832 K1: 100 misses on change chunks vs 5 on others). (ii) The price is throughput, not quality: branches that miss are wasted generations (1.13–1.36 generations per chunk → fps 4.1 → 2.9–3.6 at 832, 9.3 → 6.6–8.1 at 704). Quality / drift of the K1 clips are within noise of K0 (Δ −0.22…+0.17, drift ±5) because hits are the same chunk (exact hits: oracle within-ε 100 %) and inexact hits (speed change under the tolerance, jitter) still agree with the true-action chunk on ≈ 50 % of tokens, above the 29.5 % sample-to-sample floor (hit analysis: 199 exact / 29 translation-inexact at 49 % / 63 rotation-inexact at 55 %, 832 K1). (iii) K > 1 does not pay on this benchmark: the key-press prior is nearly uniform over ~12 single-key changes, so the second-most-likely hypothesis adds 0–8 pts of hits for a whole extra generation per chunk (K2 fps 1.8–2.0; K4 1.5, and K4 at 832 needs the speculative decode off: 4 branches x (3.8 GB K/V slot + decoder cache) OOM on 96 GB). K > 1 only pays with a peaked action prior (real user logs) or batched branches. (iv) Adaptive chunk length (B) attacks the misses: regenerating one frame instead of three after a change point cuts the miss latency 3.1 → 1.25 s (832) and 1.43 → 0.53 s (704) and the mean latency to 203–370 ms (832) / 94–164 ms (704) — the first mode whose MEAN action→frame latency is under 750 ms at 832 teacher quality (fixed-chunk hits alone leave the mean at 0.5–1.1 s). It also raises the hit rate at τ = 1 s (67 → 74 %) because 1-frame units catch change points at frame granularity. Cost: 18–49 % of the frames are generated as 1-frame units (§23: −0.33 quality at 4 steps), visible as drift +9…+14 on the dense families (±6…10, n = 12) and quality −0.70 ± 0.20 on 704 2-step τ = 1 s (the 2-step 1-frame combination of §23b); at 832 4-step the quality Δ of adaptive equals fixed (−0.18 / −0.25 / +0.09 / −0.30). (v) The jitter family is the honest 'hand on a mouse' case: hit rate stays ≈ 71 % but the hits are inexact (oracle 50–55 %); the quality cost is −0.17…−0.40 ± 0.1–0.2 at 832 (n = 12–18).
Caveats: synthetic streams (the key-press model and its value sets are ours; a recorded human/game stream is needed for a 'human input' claim); the throughput numbers include a synchronous full-VAE decode on the same GPU; speculation is only 'free' when the generator is faster than playback (704 fused / 576 modes) — at 832 4-step it trades fps for latency; 12–18 clips per cell → quality deltas below ≈ 0.3 are not resolved (§25 lesson).

### 28b. Follow-ups 1–3 (DONE 10/14 ~03:40 UTC; act_spec2 --sub 2 / --commit partial1 / --rt 1; data/actspec2_<arm>.json; 12 clips per cell unless noted)
(1) Real-time action arrival on the realtime mode (704×384 2-step, fused + partial commit, arms uniform2_a270336_p1_{fx,rt_fx,rt_ad3s2}): the rt protocol (action of latent frame i available at t0 + (i − 2)·0.25 s, generator waits for it; speculation runs during the wait) gives numbers IDENTICAL to flat-out (K0 1175 ms mean, 10.6 fps; K1 fixed 67/75/88/71 % hits, mean 428/343/200/391 ms, 7.9–9.3 fps; K1 adaptive-2 mean 300/170/171/170 ms, 7.3–8.6 fps): the chunk period of 704 fused with the full VAE decoded synchronously on the same GPU is 1.18 s > 0.75 s of playback, so the action has always arrived before the generator can use it and no idle time exists. Condition for "free" speculation (branch computed entirely in idle time): t_gen(chunk) + t_branch ≤ 0.75 s, i.e. a chunk generated in ≤ 0.375 s incl. decode (branch ≈ generation). Measured chunk times: 704 fused + sync full VAE 1.18 s, 704 fused generation only 0.56 s (§15), 576 fused + partial with streaming decode 0.747 s (§15). So on one PRO 6000 no current mode reaches the free regime; it needs the decode off the generator GPU plus ≈ 576 resolution, or a faster base model. Until then speculation trades fps for latency (fps × 1/(generations per chunk)).
(2) Adaptive chunk length with 2-frame sub-units (--sub 2, arms uniform4_ad3s2 / uniform2_a270336_ad3s2) removes the 1-frame quality cost. 832 4-step: hits 69/84/83/84 % (τ 1/2/4 s/jitter), mean 776/437/439/437 ms, miss 2.2–2.3 s, fps 2.8–3.3, 2-frame units cover 18–49 % of frames; paired quality Δ vs K0 fixed −0.08 ± 0.15 / −0.25 ± 0.18 / +0.00 ± 0.12 / −0.17 ± 0.24 (= the fixed-chunk K1 deltas), drift +7.5 ± 8.6 / −0.8 / −2.5 / +2.9 (vs +13 with 1-frame units). 704 2-step: mean 338/193/194/193 ms, miss 0.95–0.98 s, fps 6.5–7.5; quality Δ +0.00 ± 0.12 / +0.00 ± 0.12 / +0.08 ± 0.19 / +0.08 ± 0.08 (1-frame: −0.70 ± 0.20 at τ 1 s), drift −5.4 / +8.3 ± 5.2 / +0.8 / +6.7 ± 4.5. The jitter family now equals τ 2 s (0.3°/frame stays under the 2° tolerance of a 2-frame unit). → the recommended B setting is 2-frame sub-units: 832 teacher quality at mean 440–780 ms, 704 at 190–340 ms, no measurable quality cost.
(3) Recorded camera paths (data/actstream/real_*: rt2_00/01/02/05, t55_02/05 = framewise relative poses of data/rt2_action_* / traj55_action_* exactly as prepare() computes them, first 161 video frames, translation normalized by the clip max; 6 scenes × 6 paths = 36 clips per arm, K = 1 only, no key prior):
| recorded path | rot / latent frame, median | 832 fixed K1 hit rate ↑ | mean ms ↓ | oracle within ε on hits ↑ | 832 adaptive-2 hit ↑ | mean ms ↓ | 704 2-step adaptive-2 mean ms ↓ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| rt2_00 | 10.6° | 0 % | 3100 | – | 0 % | 2236 | 956 |
| rt2_01 | 9.4° | 0 % | 3107 | – | 0 % | 2234 | 957 |
| rt2_02 | 0.3° | 50 % | 1592 | 34 % | 47 % | 1169 | 507 |
| rt2_05 | 9.5° | 0 % | 3100 | – | 6 % | 2113 | 905 |
| t55_02 | 0.3° | 50 % | 1609 | 34 % | 47 % | 1173 | 507 |
| t55_05 | 9.5° | 0 % | 3073 | – | 6 % | 2104 | 905 |
Pooled: 17 % hits, 832 fixed mean 2597 ms (K0 3094), fps 2.2 (1.77 generations per chunk — the wasted branches of a low hit rate), quality Δ −0.03 ± 0.07; adaptive-2 mean 1838 ms (832) / 790 ms (704), 85 % of frames in 2-frame units, quality Δ −0.22 ± 0.09 (832) / +0.11 ± 0.15 (704). Reading: these hand-held/SfM paths rotate 9–11° per latent frame with jerky direction changes (constant-velocity error per chunk 24–34°, offline §27-style check), so the constant-velocity hypothesis never hits; the two smooth paths (0.3°/frame) hit 50 % but inexactly (oracle within-ε 34 %, at the 29.5 % floor). A hit rate on human input therefore needs (a) a predictor beyond constant velocity (e.g. linear extrapolation of angular velocity, or a learned action prior on user logs) and (b) ideally key/joystick-driven input (games, teleop), whose piecewise-constant structure is what the key-press benchmark models; hand-held camera video is the adversarial case. The 2-frame adaptive mode still halves the latency there (3.1 → 1.8 s at 832, 1.4 → 0.8 s at 704) because it is miss-driven, but at a 1.8× generation cost.

### 28c. Proposal A' — late branching to cut the miss cost (10/14; specwm/action_tol.py --late 1, scripts/action_late_report.py, data/action_late.json, runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/action_late/<task>_s7/stats.json; 12 held-out scenes x chunks 2/4/6/8/10, same protocol as §27)
Question: on a miss, can the branch's partial computation be reused — run the first j steps with the hypothesis action and only the last steps with the true action? If the high-noise step were action-agnostic, a miss would cost one step instead of a chunk. Measured: within-ε of the switched chunk vs the true-action chunk from the same noise.
| hypothesis action (branch) | 4-step branch as is ↑ | late-1 (steps 2–4 with the true action) ↑ | late-2 ↑ | late-3 ↑ | 2-step as is ↑ | 2-step late-1 ↑ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| constant velocity | 60.2 % | 62.8 % | 60.5 % | 60.3 % | 62.7 % | 65.2 % |
| stop | 4.9 % | 6.9 % | 5.1 % | 5.0 % | 4.8 % | 6.7 % |
| extra yaw 10° | 10.8 % | 13.0 % | 10.8 % | 10.9 % | 10.9 % | 12.7 % |
| extra yaw 30° | 3.5 % | 4.5 % | 3.5 % | 3.5 % | 3.5 % | 4.6 % |
| forward motion added | 12.0 % | 25.1 % | 13.8 % | 12.4 % | 11.7 % | 24.2 % |
(floor = other noise 29.8 % / 29.2 %; 60 chunks)
Reading: the camera action is decided at the FIRST denoising step (σ = 1, the layout step of the distilled sampler): redoing every later step with the true action recovers +2 pts for rotation / stop and +13 pts for translation on/off, all still far below the floor. So a branch generated under the wrong action cannot be repaired cheaply; the miss cost stays one full chunk, and the only reuse left is the reverse (step 1 per hypothesis, later steps only for the match), which gives up the zero-latency hit. Proposal A' CLOSED. Consequence for the demo/benchmark: nothing changes; for the theory memo: action enters at step 1, texture at steps 2–4 (consistent with §24's node-0 story).

### 28d. Proposal B — tolerance-aware ("hedged") hypothesis (10/14; act_spec2 --hedge 1, arms uniform4_fx_hg / uniform2_a270336_fx_hg, data/actspec2_*_hg.json; 6 scenes x 8 streams, K = 1 fixed 3-frame chunks)
Idea: the hit region is x0.5..x2 in translation magnitude, so set the hypothesis magnitude to 0.707 (covers 0.5 and 1 at once). Result (hit rate / oracle within-ε on hits, plain → hedged): τ 1 s 67 → 58 % / 81 → 59 %; τ 2 s 75 → 75 % / 91 → 61 %; τ 4 s 88 → 88 % / 100 → 64 %; jitter 71 → 71 % / 49 → 38 % (same at 704). Latency, fps and quality unchanged within noise (832: Δq −0.17/+0.00/+0.00/−0.08 vs K0; 704: −0.17/−0.17/−0.08/+0.00). Why no gain: the misses are motion on/off and rotation changes, which no magnitude hedge can cover, while rescaling the whole translation vector breaks hits on strafe changes (prev (1, 0) → hyp (0.707, 0) vs true (1, 0.5): relative error 0.52 > 0.5) and makes every hit inexact (oracle 60 % instead of 100 %). CLOSED. With A' (§28c) also closed and C protocol-equivalent (the demo already uses the freshest key state), the action-speculation design stays: constant-velocity hypothesis, 3° / x2 tolerance, 2-frame adaptive units; the remaining lever is a learned action prior on real input logs (D).

### 28e. Proposal 1 — warp reconciliation of a missed branch (10/14; specwm/action_tol.py --warp 1, scripts/action_warp_report.py, data/action_warp.json, runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/action_warp/<task>_s7/stats.json; 12 scenes x chunks 2/4/6/8/10)
Idea: a miss caused by a rotation difference is a pure-rotation homography, so warp the branch's latent frames by the per-frame rotation difference (bilinear grid_sample in latent space, holes keep the branch value, latent intrinsics = K/8), re-noise to the last node (σ 0.625; or the last two, σ 0.833) and finish with the true action. Both warp signs tested; '+' (ray in the true camera → hypothesis camera: R_h^T R_t) is the right one.
| hypothesis (branch) | branch as is ↑ | warp only ↑ | warp + last step ↑ | warp + last 2 steps ↑ | 2-step branch ↑ | 2-step warp + last step ↑ | visible area after warp |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| stop (true chunk rotates) | 4.5 % | 3.7 % | 5.9 % | 4.8 % | 4.4 % | 12.9 % | 68 % |
| extra yaw 10° | 9.6 % | 6.1 % | 11.0 % | 9.9 % | 9.8 % | 21.5 % | 87 % |
| extra yaw 30° | 3.1 % | 1.9 % | 2.7 % | 2.2 % | 3.2 % | 10.5 % | 69 % |
(within-ε vs the true-action chunk from the same noise; floors 29.5 % / 28.7 %.) Reading: warping the LATENT by the homography does not bring the branch closer in token space (warp-only is below the unwarped branch: the Wan latent is not equivariant to image warps at 8x downsampling), and one or two low-noise steps cannot repair it; the 2-step variant gains a little (re-noising to σ 0.94 is almost a regeneration) but stays far below the floor. Pixel-space warp + VAE re-encode would be the proper test but costs a decode + encode (≈ 1 s at 832), more than the regeneration it would save. CLOSED.

### 28f. Proposal 5 — perceptual verifier: go/no-go (10/14; specwm/percep_dump.py, scripts/percep_judge.py, scripts/percep_abs.py, specwm/percep_oracle.py; runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/percep/ and percep_oracle/; 12 held-out scenes, seed 7)
(i) Pairwise VLM judgement (step-k strip vs final strip, random order) is unusable: the judge calls the SECOND image worse whenever it sees a difference (candidate first: worse 0 % / better 66 %; candidate second: worse 78 % / better 0 %). Its 'same' rate tracks the distance measure (step 1/2/3: 28 / 49 / 92 % vs true within-ε 37 / 59 / 82 %).
(ii) Absolute per-strip quality (1–10, 624 strips): step 1 6.49, step 2 6.91, step 3 7.06, final 7.13 (Δ −0.64 / −0.22 / −0.07); 'not lower than the final' 49 / 75 / 88 %, monotone in the head's predicted within-ε (43 % at < 0.3 → 90 % at > 0.85); the distance rule (head ≥ 0.7) accepts 4 / 28 / 88 %, so 46 / 53 / 10 % of chunks are 'rejected but judged not worse' — the apparent room.
(iii) Ceiling test — chunk-wise early exit with the VLM score itself as the verifier (all 4 steps run, decode + score every step, commit the earliest step whose strip score ≥ final − margin, the rollout follows the accepted chunks; clips judged paired with the teacher policy of the same code):
| policy | mean steps ↓ | clip quality Δ vs teacher ↑ | drift Δ ↓ | accepted at step 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| teacher (always 4) | 4.00 | 0 (7.50) | 0 (53.3) | 0 / 0 / 0 / 100 % |
| perceptual oracle, margin 0 | 1.84 | **−0.42 ± 0.19** | −0.4 ± 4.3 | 42 / 36 / 17 / 4 % |
| perceptual oracle, margin 1 | 1.21 | −0.75 ± 0.18 | +7.1 ± 5.3 | 83 / 13 / 4 / 0 % |
| reference: distance rule f* = 0.5 (§25, bench-43) | 2.53 | −0.21 ± 0.09 | +7 | – |
| reference: fixed 2-step (§2) | 2.00 | −0.30 ± 0.08 | – | – |
Reading: even a PERFECT predictor of the chunk-level perceptual judgement loses more clip quality than the distance rule at the same or fewer steps (−0.42 at 1.84 steps vs −0.30 at 2.00 fixed, −0.21 at 2.53): a chunk that looks as good as its 4-step version in isolation still degrades the following chunks through the K/V history, which the paired ε-distance rule guards against (it keeps the committed history close to the 4-step trajectory). The 'room' in (ii) is therefore not usable. Perceptual verifier: NO-GO; the ε-distance criterion stays. (Caveat: the judge is an 8B-32B VLM on 4-frame strips with integer scores; a human study could differ, but the mechanism — history degradation — is the §21 own-history result again.)

### 28g. Item 3 — real keyboard/mouse input: OpenAI VPT Minecraft contractor logs (10/14; scripts/vpt_streams.py; data/vpt/{logs,prior.json}, data/actstream/vpt_<0-7>/)
56 logs (5 min each, 20 Hz, 52 players; 1.4 h of play after dropping GUI ticks), per latent frame (5 ticks): yaw / pitch rate from the absolute angles, speed = (#W − #S)/5, strafe = (#D − #A)/5 × 0.5. Statistics: per-frame rotation median 0.9°, p75 9.9°, p90 27° (mouse flicks; 21 % of frames exceed the StEvo turn rate 12.65°), 50 % of frames rotate < 1°, 26 % of frames idle. **Constant-velocity hit rate on the raw stream (harness tolerance, 3-frame chunks): 27.0 %** (rotation criterion alone 29.5 %); at the quantized key level 'same state for the next 3 frames' 26.4 %. First-order transition prior (fitted on 42 train players): top-1 mass 49.5 %, top-2 58 %, top-3 63 %; P(next = current) 41 % — the real prior is peaked on 'same', the alternatives are spread (top-2 adds +9 pts), so K = 2 is still unlikely to pay. 8 quantized 41-frame segments from the 10 test players written as streams (10–13 of 14 chunks contain a change point); harness runs (K0 / K1 / adaptive-2 / K2-3 with the VPT prior) queued after the figure arms.

### 28h. Figure f20 — rollout paths of all arms on one key-press stream (DONE 10/14 ~09:30 UTC; scripts/f20_paths2.py, figs/f20_paths2.png, data/f20_paths2.json + f20_paths2_stats.json; runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/paths2/<task>/tau2_s0/K<k>_<arm>_s<seed>/lat.pt and paths2/<task>/uniform4_1p3b_tau2_s0_s<seed>/lat.pt)
Redraw of the §21 path figure on the action benchmark: 5 prompts (sand jar, sandcastle, easy_lake, stonehenge, pouring water) x 10 seeds (1234/4321/9999/11/22/33/44/55/66/77), all arms on the SAME prepared key-press stream tau2_s0 (change points at chunks 1/3/5/8/11/13), 832x480, first 39 generated latent frames, PCA per prompt over all arms x seeds. Arms: target 14B 4-step; draft only 1.3B 4-step; 14B uniform 2-/3-step; ours teacher-parity (chunk-wise 0.7 + safe commit); ours + action speculation K = 1 (3-frame); ours + action speculation + adaptive 2-frame. All 7 x 50 runs present (n = 10 per arm per prompt). Reading: the 1.3B draft leaves the shared start in a different direction on every prompt (orange), every 14B arm — uniform 2/3-step, teacher-parity, and both action-speculative variants — stays inside the 14B seed cloud and tracks the target mean path. Numbers (relative latent distance of the arm's seed-mean path to the target's seed-mean path, mean over 39 frames and 5 prompts; lower = closer; and the within-arm seed spread in the same units):

| arm | mean-path dist to target ↓ | seed spread |
|---|---|---|
| target 14B 4-step | 0 | 0.82 |
| draft only 1.3B | 0.95 | 0.68 |
| 14B uniform 2-step | 0.40 | 0.77 |
| 14B uniform 3-step | **0.35** | 0.81 |
| ours teacher-parity | 0.48 | 0.93 |
| ours + action spec K1 | 0.49 | 0.93 |
| ours + action spec + adaptive 2-frame | 0.55 | 0.93 |

Caveats stated on purpose: (a) the seed spread of the target itself (0.82) is larger than any 14B-arm offset (0.35–0.55), i.e. every 14B arm is within one seed's worth of the target; the draft's offset (0.95) is larger than the seed spread, which is the §21/§22 cross-model gap again. (b) Among 14B arms the uniform 2-/3-step means sit slightly closer to the target mean than the adaptive arms, and the adaptive arms have a slightly larger seed spread (0.93 vs 0.82) — the chunk-wise rule commits some chunks after fewer steps and the safe commit re-noises, so trajectories fan out a little more; this is not a quality statement (§28/§28b clip quality of these arms is within noise of the target), and trajectory distance is not quality (§24, |ρ| ≤ 0.2). The figure is a sanity picture of 'same model family, same basin' vs 'other model, other basin', not a ranking of the 14B arms.

## 29. KV window at StEvo length (253 frames = 21 chunks): does a longer window help on long clips? (DONE 10/14 ~10:50 UTC; specwm/fit24.py --frames 253 --window W --video 1, runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/fit24/s253_a704w{12,18,36}_fp8_fp8kv/ and s253_a704w18_teacher/, VLM scores fit24/vlm_s253.json, table scripts/s253_report.py)
Question from 10/14 (asymmetric KV: a longer window on the cloud, a short one on the device): on 161-frame clips window 12 = window 18 within noise (§19). Here the same test at the StEvo clip length, 704x384, 2-step fused + partial commit 1, FP8 weights + FP8 KV, window 12 / 18 / 36 latent frames (incl. 6 sink), plus the 4-step teacher (bf16 KV, separate commit, window 18) as the paired reference; seed 1234; 39 of the 43 bench prompts (easy_dragon_night, easy_greatwall, easy_stonehenge_sunset, easy_urban_rain have 161-frame pose files and fail at chunk 14 — excluded from every arm). Quality / drift = Qwen3-VL-32B clip judge (8 frames); paired ± = SE of the per-prompt difference.

| arm (704x384, 253 frames, 39 prompts, seed 1234) | quality ↑ | drift ↓ | Δq vs teacher ↑ | Δdrift vs teacher ↓ | Δq vs window 18 ↑ | steady fps ↑ | KV GiB ↓ | peak reserved GiB ↓ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| teacher 4-step, bf16 KV, window 18 | **7.10** | 69.0 | — | — | +0.44 ± 0.14 | 7.6 | 14.2 | 39.1 |
| 2-step fused + pc1, FP8 KV, window 12 | <u>6.77</u> | **60.5** | **−0.33 ± 0.15** | **−8.5 ± 3.8** | +0.10 ± 0.09 | **17.4** | **4.7** | **29.5** |
| 2-step fused + pc1, FP8 KV, window 18 | 6.67 | 63.8 | <u>−0.44 ± 0.14</u> | −5.1 ± 3.9 | 0 | <u>15.1</u> | <u>7.1</u> | <u>32.1</u> |
| 2-step fused + pc1, FP8 KV, window 36 | 6.56 | <u>63.1</u> | −0.54 ± 0.17 | <u>−5.9 ± 3.6</u> | −0.10 ± 0.11 | 10.6 | 14.2 | 39.1 |

Reading: at 253 frames the window order is 12 ≥ 18 ≥ 36 on quality (neighbouring differences 0.10 ± 0.09–0.11, i.e. within one SE each, but monotone across three points and the opposite of 'longer helps'); drift is lowest for window 12 and the teacher drifts most by this judge (69 vs 60–64; the judge's drift score is scene-level, so this is 'not worse', not 'better' — see the §4 caveat on the judge). Memory and speed scale with the window as expected (4.7 → 14.2 GiB, 17.4 → 10.6 fps). Conclusion for the asymmetric-KV idea: no evidence that the 14B uses context beyond ~6 local frames even on 21-chunk clips, so a cloud-side long window buys nothing to send down; window 12 remains the device setting at StEvo length too. The quality gap of the realtime stack vs the teacher at 253 frames (−0.33 ± 0.15 at window 12) matches the 161-frame number (§19: −0.07 ± 0.12 at window 12 relative to window 18, −0.62 ± 0.09 vs the 832 teacher in §13) — the long clips do not open a new gap.

**§28g harness results (DONE 10/14 ~11:30 UTC; act_spec2 --mode uniform:2 --area 270336 on the 8 VPT streams x 6 scenes = 48 clips per cell, seed 7, 704x384 2-step, separate commit, speculative decode; arms uniform2_a270336_fx (K0/K1), _ad3s2 (K1 adaptive 2-frame), _fx_vp (K2/K3 with the VPT first-order prior from data/vpt/prior.json); VLM scores actspec2/vlm.json; paired Δ vs K0 of the same clip.)**

| arm (VPT real keyboard/mouse streams, 704x384 2-step) | K | hit rate ↑ | action→frame mean ms ↓ | p50 ms ↓ | hit chunks ms | miss chunks ms | output fps ↑ | generations / chunk ↓ | oracle within ε on hits ↑ | 2-frame units (share of frames) | quality ↑ (Δ vs K0, paired) | drift ↓ (Δ) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| no speculation | 0 | – | 1371 | 1426 | – | – | **9.1** | **1.00** | – | 0 % | 7.25 | 41.1 |
| constant-velocity, fixed 3-frame | 1 | 20 % | 1118 | 1425 | 6 | 1398 | <u>5.1</u> | <u>1.74</u> | 78 % | 0 % | **7.29** (+0.04 ± 0.06) | 42.7 (+1.6 ± 1.2) |
| constant-velocity + adaptive 2-frame | 1 | 21 % | **747** | **980** | 5 | 952 | 5.0 | <u>1.74</u> | **88 %** | 84 % | 7.23 (−0.02 ± 0.08) | **40.4** (−0.7 ± 3.1) |
| + VPT prior, K = 2 | 2 | **27 %** | <u>1023</u> | <u>1394</u> | 6 | 1399 | 3.3 | 2.60 | 73 % | 0 % | **7.29** (+0.04 ± 0.06) | **40.4** (−0.7 ± 1.7) |
| + VPT prior, K = 3 | 3 | **27 %** | 1024 | 1394 | 6 | 1400 | 2.4 | 3.52 | 73 % | 0 % | **7.29** (+0.04 ± 0.06) | 40.6 (−0.5 ± 1.7) |

Reading: on real game input the constant-velocity hit rate is 20 % (the raw-stream estimate was 27 %; the quantized 41-frame segments were chosen to contain change points), so speculation saves 20 % of the chunks (mean latency 1371 → 1118 ms) at a throughput cost of 9.1 → 5.1 fps. The VPT prior at K = 2 lifts hits to 27 % (+7 pts, matching the prior's top-2 mass gain) for 2.6 generations per chunk (3.3 fps); K = 3 adds nothing (the prior's third alternative never hits). Adaptive 2-frame units are the one lever that moves the mean (747 ms, p50 980 ms): with hits this rare the stream stays in 2-frame units 84 % of the time, i.e. it is effectively the 2-frame-chunk mode (§23b, 1174 ms chunk → 952 ms miss here) plus occasional hits; quality unchanged within noise in every arm (Δ ≤ 0.04 ± 0.06–0.08). Conclusion for item 3: real human input is far jerkier than the synthetic families (τ_eff ≈ 0.3 s vs 1–4 s), so input-side speculation alone covers 20–27 % of chunks; for a human-input latency claim the lever is the generation unit (2-frame, §23b/§28b) and the output-side speed (fps), not the hypothesis set. A smoother input device (gamepad stick with rate limit, or key-driven motion as in our demo) sits between the VPT and τ = 1 s families.

## 30. Item 2 — learned action-edit refiner (LoRA on the 14B; 10/14; specwm/edit_dump.py, specwm/edit_train.py, act_spec2 --edit, scripts/edit_report.py; data lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/edit_data (78 train scenes x tau1_s0/s1, 12 pairs per clip) / edit_data_val (12 held-out scenes x tau1_s2); runs specwm_out/edit14/E{1,2,3} = edit node 1/2/3, LoRA r64 on all attention + FFN linears, lr 3e-5, loss = relative MSE to the true-action chunk x_T; harness runs actspec2/<task>/<stream>/K1_uniform4_fx_edE3_s252_s7/)
Idea: a miss currently costs a full regeneration (4 forwards + commit at 832) because the action is decided at step 1 (§28c). Train a LoRA that, in ONE forward at node E, turns the wrong-action chunk x_H (re-noised to σ_E) into the true-action chunk x_T generated from the same noise and history. Pairs: at every chunk of a teacher-forced 4-step rollout, x_T vs x_H under the constant-velocity hypothesis when the stream changes there (the deployed miss case, 'cv'), else a random perturbation (stop / yaw ±8° / translation on-off).
Validation (within-ε of the edited chunk vs x_T; as-is = the unedited x_H; 12 held-out scenes, 144 pairs): step 0 17–18 % (as-is 20.6 %) → step 252: node 1 33.0 %, node 2 37.4 %, node 3 43.1 % → step 504: 40.4 / 40.7 / 43.0 % (cv kind 36 / 35 / 39 % vs as-is 20 %; yaw 35–39 % vs 5 %; stop 45–47 % vs 41 %). All above the 29.5 % regeneration floor; node 3 (least noise) best; plateau by step 500.
**Harness (step-252 node-3 LoRA, 832x480 4-step, K = 1 fixed 3-frame, 12 held-out scenes x tau1_s0/s1 + tau2_s0/s1, paired with the K1 regenerate arm (identical hits, identical noise) and K0):**

| family | hit rate | edited chunks: within-ε vs true-action chunk (as-is branch) ↑ | miss-chunk ms: edit / regenerate ↓ | mean ms: edit / regen / K0 ↓ | fps: edit / regen / K0 ↑ | quality: edit / regen / K0 ↑ | Δq edit − regen (paired) ↑ | Δq edit − K0 ↑ | drift: edit / regen / K0 ↓ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| tau1 (12 clips, 48 edits) | 67 % | 49 % (45 %) | **1732** / 3105 | **583** / 1040 / 3133 | 3.19 / 3.02 / **4.04** | 6.92 / 7.75 / **7.92** | −0.83 ± 0.17 | −1.00 ± 0.17 | 63.8 / 54.6 / **50.8** |
| tau2 (12 clips, 36 edits) | 75 % | 29 % (26 %) | **1780** / 3225 | **451** / 812 / 3133 | 3.30 / 3.20 / **4.04** | 6.67 / 7.42 / **7.67** | −0.75 ± 0.18 | −1.00 ± 0.12 | 72.1 / 63.8 / **69.2** |

Reading (preliminary, step-252 checkpoint): the edit halves the miss latency (3.1 → 1.7 s = one forward + commit) and the mean action→frame latency (1040 → 583 ms at 832 teacher steps), but (i) in deployment it barely moves the chunk towards x_T — 49 vs 45 % as-is on tau1, 29 vs 26 % on tau2 — because the deployed misses (constant-velocity hypothesis) are already far milder than the dump's perturbations (as-is 45 % here vs 20 % in validation), and (ii) it costs 0.75–0.83 ± 0.17 clip quality against the regenerate arm and +9 drift: the edited chunks enter the history and the rollout degrades (the §21/§28f mechanism), exactly like the step-1 self-distill LoRA of §22. Final checkpoints (step 3000) are re-run in the harness when training ends; unless they close the quality gap the refiner is CLOSED and the miss cost stays a full chunk (→ the 2-frame unit of §28b is the latency lever).

**§30 final checkpoints (DONE 10/14 ~19:00 UTC; step 3000, E1/E2/E3 = edit node 1/2/3, val within-ε 47.3 / 47.2 / 45.4 % vs as-is 20.6 %; harness arms actspec2/<task>/<stream>/K1_uniform4_fx_ed{E1,E2,E3}_s7, same 48 clips; scripts/edit_report.py now matches the arm name exactly):**

| checkpoint | family | edited chunks within-ε (as-is) ↑ | miss ms: edit / regen ↓ | mean ms: edit / regen ↓ | quality: edit / regen / K0 ↑ | Δq edit − regen (paired) ↑ | drift: edit / regen ↓ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E3 step 252 (prelim.) | tau1 / tau2 | 49 % (45) / 29 % (26) | 1732 / 3105 · 1780 / 3225 | 583 / 1040 · 451 / 812 | 6.92 / 7.75 / 7.92 · 6.67 / 7.42 / 7.67 | −0.83 ± 0.17 · −0.75 ± 0.18 | 63.8 / 54.6 · 72.1 / 63.8 |
| E1 node 1, final | tau1 / tau2 | 61 % (46) / 43 % (29) | 1742 / 3105 · 1791 / 3225 | 586 / 1040 · 454 / 812 | 6.50 / 7.75 / 7.92 · 6.75 / 7.42 / 7.67 | −1.25 ± 0.18 · −0.67 ± 0.19 | 71.2 / 54.6 · 68.3 / 63.8 |
| E2 node 2, final | tau1 / tau2 | 60 % (45) / 42 % (29) | 1727 / 3105 · 1776 / 3225 | 581 / 1040 · 450 / 812 | 6.58 / 7.75 / 7.92 · 6.83 / 7.42 / 7.67 | −1.17 ± 0.11 · −0.58 ± 0.19 | 54.2 / 54.6 · 71.2 / 63.8 |
| E3 node 3, final | tau1 / tau2 | 60 % (46) / 45 % (31) | 1729 / 3105 · 1777 / 3225 | 581 / 1040 · 450 / 812 | 6.58 / 7.75 / 7.92 · 6.75 / 7.42 / 7.67 | −1.17 ± 0.17 · −0.67 ± 0.14 | 58.3 / 54.6 · 70.4 / 63.8 |

Verdict: **CLOSED.** Longer training raises the within-ε of the edited chunk (49 → 60 % on tau1) and LOWERS clip quality further (−0.83 → −1.17…−1.25 vs regenerate): the LoRA learns to move the chunk towards the teacher sample in the MSE sense, which is the §22 blur/over-smoothing mechanism, and the edited chunks poison the history. The miss cost stays a full chunk; the Berkeley 'correction training' paper (sunovivid.github.io/correction-training, 10/14 read) gives the same reading — a corrector regressed onto a single sample is not what makes correction work there (their gains come with INCREASED denoising error and real-data targets). A refiner is only worth revisiting as a distribution-matching (DMD-style) component in the direction-1 post-training.

## 31. Test-time scaling of the 14B causal-fast (direction-1 de-risk; DONE 10/14; specwm/fit24.py --nodes, runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/tts/s{2,3,4,6,8}/, vlm_tts.json, scripts/tts_report.py)
Question: does the shipped 4-step model improve with more steps (ODE-like base) or degrade (consistency-like, as Self-Forcing in AnyFlow Fig. 1)? Uniform node sets on the shift-5 1000-step grid, 832x480, 161 frames, FP8 weights, bf16 KV window 18, clean commit; 43 bench prompts, seed 1234, paired VLM judge vs the 4-step run.

| steps | n | quality ↑ | drift ↓ | Δq vs 4-step (paired) | Δdrift vs 4-step | steady fps ↑ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 (0,500) | 43 | 7.44 | **46.6** | −0.16 ± 0.11 | **−10.5 ± 4.6** | **8.1** |
| 3 (0,333,667) | 43 | <u>7.53</u> | 49.5 | <u>−0.07 ± 0.08</u> | −7.6 ± 4.0 | <u>6.1</u> |
| 4 (0,250,500,750) = shipped | 43 | **7.60** | 57.1 | 0 | 0 | 4.8 |
| 6 | 43 | 7.51 | <u>49.0</u> | −0.09 ± 0.08 | <u>−8.1 ± 5.1</u> | 3.4 |
| 8 | 43 | 7.42 | 56.4 | −0.19 ± 0.08 | −0.7 ± 3.8 | 2.6 |

(8-step rerun completed 10/14 21:18 UTC on GPU 3, all 43 clips.) Reading: 8 steps are WORSE than 4 (−0.19 ± 0.08, significant) — no test-time scaling — 6 steps are not better than 4 (−0.09 ± 0.08), so the base behaves like a consistency-distilled model, not an ODE solver; and the few-step penalty in this clean-commit stack is small (2-step −0.16 ± 0.11, 3-step −0.07 ± 0.08; the −0.30 of the quality mode in §13 includes the fused/partial commit). Consequence for direction 1: the flow-map base cannot be expected to gain much by 'more steps'; its value is on the other side — (i) a deterministic 1-/2-step path whose jump-to-0 agrees with the N-step path up to discretization error only (the native sampler injects FRESH noise at each step, so its step-1 x0 and 4-step output are different samples by construction — part of the 34/56/81 % acceptance ceiling is noise, not model error), and (ii) a model trained at all t so that the exit head's decision is between valid endpoints.

## 32. Direction 1, stage-1 pilot on the 1.3B: the finite-difference MeanFlow target is noise on the distilled base (10/14; specwm/flowmap.py diag, lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/fm/diag/diag_base.json, logs/diag_base.log)
Pilot try4 (LoRA r256, --phase-a 1000 plain flow matching at random t, then the AnyFlow (t, r) mix with central-difference d u/dt, eps = 5/1000): phase A diff loss 0.125 → 0.07–0.09; at phase B entry (steps 1025–1100) cons loss 16 → 63, map 3–12, |corr|/|v| 2.4–3.2 and the diff loss rose 0.066 → 0.11 — the flow-map terms were pushing the LoRA with a target dominated by the correction term. Diagnostic on 3 held-out clips × 3 chunks, GT-interpolant points z_t, r = 0 ('r0') and r = t/2 ('rhalf'), untrained 1.3B causal-fast (bf16, no LoRA): ratio |(t−r)·(u(t+e)−u(t−e))/2e| / |v| and the cosine of that derivative estimate against the eps = 50 estimate.

| s (uniform) → σ (shift 5) | r | eps 1 | eps 2 | eps 5 (AnyFlow) | eps 10 | eps 20 | eps 50 | cos(eps 5, eps 50) ↑ | cos(eps 20, 50) | |u−v|/|v| |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.3 → 0.68 | 0 | 3.17 | 2.25 | 1.24 | 0.96 | 0.85 | 0.69 | 0.68 | 0.90 | 0.27 |
| 0.5 → 0.83 | 0 | 4.01 | 3.18 | 2.58 | 2.32 | 2.00 | 1.47 | 0.59 | 0.79 | 0.30 |
| 0.7 → 0.92 | 0 | 8.64 | 7.48 | 6.24 | 5.37 | 4.20 | 2.81 | 0.40 | 0.61 | 0.34 |
| 0.9 → 0.98 | 0 | 21.6 | 20.0 | 16.1 | 12.0 | 8.43 | 6.42 | 0.23 | 0.51 | 0.43 |
(rhalf rows = half of the r0 ratios, same cosines; ratio = |corr|/|v| ↓ is the size of the correction relative to the plain flow-matching target.)

Reading: a true derivative is eps-independent; here the estimate keeps shrinking with eps and never converges (cosine between eps 5 and eps 50 only 0.2–0.7, between 20 and 50 only 0.5–0.9), i.e. the 4-step distilled model's output is rough in t off its 4 training nodes (at σ ≈ 0.98 the output moves by ≈ 0.65 |v| over Δt = 0.1, more than its whole distance to v, 0.43 |v|). AnyFlow can use eps = 5 because it starts from the 50-step Wan2.1 base, which is smooth in t; LingBot only ships 4-step causal models (and a bidirectional 14B base). 1000 steps of plain flow matching at random t did not cure it (phase B losses above). Decision 10/14 23:50 UTC: pilot restarted from the phase-A LoRA (lora_s1000) with a derivative-free semigroup target (--fd-mode semi, δ = 0.1 in shifted σ): u(z_t, t, r) ← (δ·v + (t−δ−r)·sg[u(z_{t−δ}, t−δ, r)]) / (t−r) along the GT interpolant — the discrete form of the same identity (consistency-training style), whose target noise is the model's value roughness, not roughness / eps. Run: specwm_out/fm/p13_semi (log fm/logs/p13_semi.log); the FD run archived as fm/p13_r256_fd.
Same diagnostic on the phase-A LoRA (lora_s1000, logs/diag_s1000.log), r = 0: |corr|/|v| at eps 1/2/5/10/20/50 → σ 0.68: 2.99/2.10/0.92/0.89/0.82/0.73 (cos(5,50) 0.72); σ 0.83: 2.45/2.14/2.15/1.46/1.35/1.21 (0.73); σ 0.92: 2.77/2.21/2.28/2.19/1.92/1.80 (0.82); σ 0.98: 4.87/4.33/3.82/3.83/3.48/3.99 (0.64). So 1000 steps of random-t flow matching DO smooth the field: for eps ≥ 10 the estimate is nearly eps-independent (a real derivative), but it is large — (t−r)·du/dt is 1–4 × |v| — so the flow-map fixed point is far from the current model and the eps = 5 phase B was still on the noisy side (cos 0.64–0.82) and diverged. Options kept in reserve if the semigroup run stalls: FD with eps 10–20 after phase A; longer phase A.

**Semigroup run (p13_semi, δ = 0.1 fixed, from lora_s1000) — collapsed (10/15 01:40 UTC).** Losses looked healthy (diff 0.10, cons 0.035, map 0.057 at step 500; |tgt−v|/|v| 0.2) and the teacher-forced val within-ε jumped to 57 / 86 / 99 % (k = 1/2/3), but the free-running eval on the 6 held-out clips (fm/eval_s500, VLM judge):

| label (1.3B, 6 held-out clips, free-running) | N | quality ↑ | drift ↓ | within-ε k=1/2/3 ↑ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| base, native sampler | 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 | 3.83 / 6.00 / **6.67** / **7.00** | 71.7 / **55.0** / 56.7 / **52.5** | – / 37 / 34·48·64 / 30·34·42 % |
| semi δ 0.1 step 500, flow-map Euler | 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 | 1.00 / 2.33 / 3.67 / 3.33 | 100 / 95.8 / 90.8 / 85.8 | – / 89 / 78·94·99 / 68·81·90 % |

Reading: self-consistency went up because the model collapsed — with δ fixed at 0.1 the target for r < t is 89 % the model's own output at a nearby (t−δ, r) and only 11 % ground truth, a bootstrap chain of ≈ 9 hops with no anchor early in training (consistency training needs an annealed schedule), so u(·, ·, r < t) converged to a self-consistent but wrong field. Lesson for the paper's recipe: acceptance (within-ε) must always be reported with the N-step quality; it is trivially high for a degenerate model. Next (launched 01:45 UTC, both from lora_s1000): (a) GPU 6 p13_fd20 — AnyFlow central differences with eps = 20 (the smallest eps at which the phase-A field has a real derivative, cos(20,50) ≥ 0.84); (b) GPU 0 p13_semisched — semigroup with δ annealed from (t−r) (= plain conditional flow matching for every (t, r)) to 0.1 over 2000 steps. Sanity on GPU 1: s500 and the phase-A LoRA under the native sampler (is the LoRA damaging the model itself, or only the flow-map path?).

**Sanity evals (10/15 02:30 UTC, fm/eval_phaseA, eval_s500 native): the phase-A LoRA alone already degrades the model.** Native sampler (x0 + fresh noise, the shipped sampler), 6 held-out clips: phase-A LoRA (1000 steps plain flow matching at random t, diff loss 0.125 → 0.087) N = 1 / 2 / 4 quality 1.33 / 2.00 / 4.50, drift 100 / 96.7 / 75.8 (base 3.83 / 6.00 / 6.67, drift 71.7 / 55.0 / 56.7); on the flow-map Euler path 2.33 / 2.00 / 2.67. The semi-δ0.1 LoRA at step 500 under the native sampler: 1.00 / 3.00 / 5.67 (drift 100 / 95 / 75.8). Mechanism (same as §22): plain flow matching regresses the network to the conditional-mean velocity E[v | z_t]; the shipped causal-fast is a DMD/Self-Forcing-distilled model whose few-step outputs are deliberately sharper than that mean, so flow matching turns it back into a diffusion model, which is blurry and drifty at 4 steps. AnyFlow never faces this: its stage 1 starts from the 50-step Wan2.1 base (already the mean-velocity model) and its sharpness at few steps comes from the flow-map objective at scale (256K videos) plus stage-2 DMD. Consequence: on the LingBot checkpoints, stage 1 alone (any variant of a data-regression target) is a step backwards; the any-step property can only be obtained by re-distilling (DMD stage with the bid 14B as real score, flow-map student). Both running variants (fd20, semisched) start from the degraded phase-A LoRA; their step-500 evals (fm/chain_s500.sh) will show whether the flow-map terms recover quality at all before the line is decided.

**Stage-1 line closed; both variants died at step 500 on a full disk (10/15 04:10 UTC).** p13_fd20 (eps 20) diverged: diff loss 0.085 → 0.206, cons 20 → 32, val within-ε 0 / 0 / 0.1 % at step 500. p13_semisched (δ annealed from t−r) reached val within-ε 66 / 92 / 99 % at step 500 but its checkpoint could not be written (/home at 100 %, 3.3 GB free after removing my corrupted/duplicate LoRA files) — not evaluated, and it starts from the degraded phase-A LoRA anyway. Phase-A 500-step LoRA under the native sampler: N = 1 / 2 / 4 quality 1.00 / 1.67 / 4.33 (drift 97.5 / 95.0 / 75.0) — the damage is already there at 500 steps. Verdict for the paper: on a DMD-distilled few-step base, any flow-matching / flow-map regression stage (MeanFlow FD at eps 5 or 20, semigroup fixed δ, semigroup annealed δ) degrades the few-step sampler before it learns the (t, r) map; the any-step property needs the distillation stage itself (DMD with the bid 14B as real score) — go/no-go on that is the user's call (cost: 1–2 days of code, 1.3B pilot ≈ 5 h on one GPU, 14B ≈ 15 h on two).

## 33. How fast is the bidirectional 14B at the plan resolution? (item (b) of the 10/15 a→b→c plan; DONE 10/15 09:30 UTC; specwm/plan_gen.py --fp8 1 / --offload 1 + timing fields gen_s/fwd_s/n_fwd/swap_s in plan.json; runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/bidspeed/{l4_fp8,l4_bf16,off70_fp8,off70_bf16}/, chain bidspeed/run2.sh, logs bidspeed/logs/)
Setting: bid 14B (Wan2.2-style high/low-noise experts, `bid_as_v1_cam` weights via SPECWM_BASE_CKPT), easy_lake seed 1234, 161 frames at 832×480 → latents (16, 41, 58, 104) = 61.8 K tokens per forward (the whole clip at once), one RTX PRO 6000 (GPU 3). "l4" = the 4-step lightx2v LoRA sampler (no CFG, shift 5) we use for plans; "70" = the official sampler (70 steps, CFG guide 5 → 2 forwards per step, shift 10). bf16 with both experts resident OOMs at this token count (93 GB), so bf16 rows keep the experts on the CPU and swap the needed one in (`--offload 1`; 2 swaps per clip: initial load + one boundary crossing).

| sampler | weights | clip time ↓ (s) | forward time (s) / n_fwd | s per forward ↓ | swap s | fps-equivalent ↑ (161 frames) | peak weights |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4-step LoRA, no CFG | FP8 (both resident) | <u>98.6</u> | 80.0 / 4 | 20.0 | 0 | <u>1.63</u> | 35.6 GiB |
| 4-step LoRA, no CFG | bf16 (offload) | 139.9 | 75.1 / 4 | **18.8** | 42.4 | 1.15 | 70 GB on CPU, 1 expert on GPU |
| 70-step CFG (official) | FP8 (both resident) | 2819.7 | 2801.1 / 140 | 20.0 | 0 | 0.057 | 35.6 GiB |
| 70-step CFG (official) | bf16 (offload) | 2702.8 | 2640.0 / 140 | <u>18.9</u> | 44.8 | 0.060 | as above |
| *causal-fast 14B teacher (ref., §2: 4.08 fps)* | bf16 | **39.5** | 13 chunks × 4 steps | – | – | **4.08** | 28 GB |
| *teacher-parity mode (§2: 6.80 fps)* | | 23.7 | | | | 6.80 | |
| *realtime mode 704×384 2-step (§2: 15.73 fps)* | | 10.2 | | | | 15.73 | |

Readings. (1) One bidirectional forward over the 161-frame clip costs 19–20 s regardless of weight format: FP8 weight swapping (fast_patch.fp8_swap, weight-only) brings no speed-up at 62 K tokens — the forward is attention-bound (full 62 K × 62 K attention per layer), the FP8 cast overhead even makes it 6 % slower; its only benefit is memory (both experts resident in 35.6 GiB instead of 70 GB). (2) The cheapest bid sampler (4 steps, no CFG) needs 99 s per 10-s clip = 1.6 fps, 2.5× slower than the causal-fast teacher on the same clip (39.5 s) and 10× slower than our realtime mode; the official 70-step CFG sampler is 28× slower still (45–47 min per clip, 0.06 fps). (3) Step distillation is where the bid's 25–28× came from (140 → 4 forwards); the remaining structural gap to the causal model is full attention over the whole clip (≈ 1.8× the attention FLOPs per clip of the windowed causal model at sink 6 + local 18, §29) plus the fact that nothing can be shown before the whole clip is done (TTFF = clip time, no mid-clip actions). Generic levers left on the 4-step bid (sage attention, torch.compile, step caching, token pruning) are worth ≈ 1.5–2× at most and cannot change (3). Conclusion: the bid 14B is a teacher / data generator / cloud planner (the 10/5 plan→render pipeline in the README; DMD real score in §34), not a candidate for the interactive path.

## 34. Direction 1, stage 2 — DMD pilot on the 1.3B with the bidirectional 14B as the real score (item (a) of the 10/15 plan; specwm/dmd_any.py; runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/dmd/p13_a (DMD + flow-map co-train) and p13_b (DMD only); evals dmd/eval_a<step>, chain dmd/chain_eval*.sh, report scripts/fm_report.py fm/eval_base13 dmd/eval_a<step>)
Setup: student = v2 1.3B causal-fast + LoRA r256 (gen adapter with the (t, r) flow-map embedding, gate 0.25; a second 'fake' adapter = critic), real score = bid 14B (both experts bf16 on GPU 1, CFG guide 5, expert by boundary σ 0.947, plucker camera from the action stream), per step: free-running flow-map rollout of 7 chunks with N ∈ {1,2,3,4} → DMD gradient (x0_fake − x0_real) / mean|x − x0_real| at one σ ~ shift-5(U[0.02, 0.98]) → regression on the last step of 3 random chunks; critic = flow matching on a fresh rollout; AdamW lr 1e-5 / 2e-5, grad clip 1; p13_a adds the semigroup flow-map loss (weight 1, δ annealed) on teacher-forced data clips. ~61 s per step (bid forwards 18 s). First run OOMed GPU 1 at step ~95 (a per-(task, stream) plucker cache, ~0.1 GiB/step; fixed, resumed from lora_s50 with identical val).

| 1.3B, 6 held-out clips, free-running | N | quality ↑ | drift ↓ | teacher-forced within-ε k=1 / 2 / 3 ↑ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| base, native sampler | 1 / 2 / 4 | 3.83 / 6.00 / **6.67** | 71.7 / **55.0** / 56.7 | 34 / 48 / 64 % (N=4 native) |
| p13_a step 50, flow-map Euler | 1 / 2 / 4 | 3.17 / 3.50 / 3.50 | 83.3 / 79.2 / 61.7 | 5.7 / 12.9 / 84.7 % |
| p13_a step 50, native N=4 | 4 | <u>5.50</u> | **38.3** | |
| p13_a step 100, flow-map Euler | 1 / 2 / 4 | 2.83 / 3.00 / 3.50 | 86.7 / 95.0 / 83.3 | 9.9 / 35.0 / 91.3 % |
| p13_a step 100, native N=4 | 4 | 4.33 | 69.2 | |

Reading (10/15 10:30 UTC): the same pattern as stage 1 — teacher-forced self-consistency rises (k=2: 13 → 35 %, approaching the native 48 %) while free-running quality falls at every N (native N=4: 6.67 → 5.50 → 4.33), i.e. the model is getting smoother, not better. DMD losses were flat (0.26–0.40, |grad|/|x| 1.2–2.1, critic 0.09–0.34), so the DMD term is not visibly steering yet; the semigroup co-train term is the known collapsing force (§32). p13_a stopped at step 100 (checkpoints lora_s50 / s100 kept); **p13_b = DMD only (--fm-weight 0, critic ratio 2, 300 steps) launched 10:30 UTC** to isolate the DMD signal: if quality still falls, the real-score setup itself (bid teacher on a causal student's rollouts, CFG 5, batch 1) is the problem and stage 2 is closed on this hardware; if it holds, the flow-map term gets reweighted.

p13_b (DMD only, --fm-weight 0, critic ratio 2; GPUs 0+1; stopped at step 115, 12:50 UTC; checkpoints dmd/p13_b/lora_s50, lora_s100; eval dmd/eval_b100, VLM labels dmdB100 / dmdB100nat):

| 1.3B, 6 held-out clips, free-running | N | quality ↑ | drift ↓ | teacher-forced within-ε k=1 / 2 / 3 ↑ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| base, native sampler | 1 / 2 / 4 | 3.83 / 6.00 / **6.67** | 71.7 / **55.0** / 56.7 | 34 / 48 / 64 % (N=4 native) |
| p13_b step 50 (val only) | | | | 0.0 / 0.0 / 11.2 % |
| p13_b step 100, flow-map Euler | 1 / 2 / 4 | 1.00 / 1.33 / 2.33 | 100 / 100 / 99.2 | 0.0 / 0.0 / 27.5 % |
| p13_b step 100, native N=4 | 4 | 3.33 | 75.8 | |

Reading (10/15 13:10 UTC): removing the flow-map term makes it worse, not better — with DMD alone the student collapses in 100 steps at every N (flow N=4 2.33 vs base 6.67; native N=4 3.33 vs 6.67) and the teacher-forced within-ε drops to zero (k=3 27 % vs 64 % base; p13_a had 85–91 %), i.e. the DMD gradient from the bidirectional real score is pushing the causal student *away* from its own distribution rather than towards the teacher's. DMD losses never moved (0.14–0.39, |grad|/|x| 1.2–1.8, critic 0.10–0.43 over 115 steps, 61 s/step). Both go/no-go conditions fail in both variants. **Stage 2 (DMD with the bid 14B as real score) is CLOSED on this hardware**: the real-score setup — a bidirectional teacher scoring a causal student's free-running rollouts at batch 1, CFG 5, with a critic that has to track a moving 258M-parameter generator — does not produce a usable signal in the ~300-step budget one GPU pair allows, and the stage-1 result (§32) already showed that the student's own-rollout distribution is the thing every consistency/semigroup target destroys. Direction 1 on the 1.3B is therefore closed in both stages; the self-speculation line on the 14B (§35) is the one that carries forward. Kept: p13_a/p13_b checkpoints (lora_s50/s100), eval dirs dmd/eval_a50, eval_a100, eval_b100 (~small), code specwm/dmd_any.py.

## 35. Two-machine self-speculation = the 14B's 4-step denoising pipelined across a device and a cloud (item (c) of the 10/15 plan; specwm/two_machine.py, scripts/twomach_report.py; runs lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/twomach/<tag>/<arm>/{device,cloud}/<task>/{timing.json,lat.pt,spec.mp4}, VLM twomach/vlm_<tag>.json; 10/15 10:00–11:00 UTC timing at 704×384, 12 prompts seed 1234; 43-prompt quality run tag a704_43 in progress)
Protocol (two processes over TCP, device GPU 6 ↔ cloud GPU 3 on spi0, both the FP8 14B causal-fast with the realtime stack, sage attention, KV window 18 = sink 6 + 4 local chunks): the device drafts every chunk with nodes 1–2 of the teacher's 4-node schedule (σ 1.0 → 0.937; shown immediately = the "exit at step 2" stream), sends the x0 latent (405 KB at 704×384 in fp16) and goes on; the cloud re-noises it to node 3 (σ 0.833), runs nodes 3–4 in its own context (the corrected sequence) and returns the corrected x0; the device re-commits that chunk's KV slot (dense clean commit, 0.31 s) if it differs from the draft by more than --thr (relative L2; observed 0.23–0.25 on every chunk, so thr 0 = always). The cloud skips refinement (commits the draft as is) when more than one draft is queued, so it never falls behind. Network: measured round trip for one chunk latent 0.8 ms on loopback, 9.9 ms spi0 → lamarr → spi0 through an ssh tunnel (0.2 ms ping; lamarr cannot open ports towards spi0); --delay-ms injects a one-way WAN delay at both receivers (arm d50 = 50 ms).

| arm (704×384, 161 frames = 13 chunks of 0.75 s; medians over chunks 3–12 of 12 clips) | device s/chunk: draft / total incl. re-commit ↓ | device fps shown ↑ | corrections applied per clip (of 13) | correction lag from show ↓ (s) | cloud refined / skipped per clip | cloud s/chunk | cloud-stream latency from show ↓ (s) | peak GiB device / cloud |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| hyb: device exit-2 + cloud nodes 3–4, feedback (thr 0) | 0.763 / 1.072 | 11.2 | 10.9 | 1.07 | 13.0 / 0.0 | 0.763 | **0.77** | 48.1 / 48.1 |
| nofb: same, device ignores the corrections | 0.762 / **0.763** | **15.7** | 0 | – | 9.8 / 0.2 | 0.758 | **0.77** | 48.1 / 48.1 |
| d50: hyb + 50 ms one-way WAN delay | 0.762 / 1.072 | 11.2 | 10.8 | 1.08 | 13.0 / 0.0 | 0.763 | <u>0.81</u> | 48.1 / 48.1 |
| solo teacher: 4 steps on one GPU (FP8) | 1.405 / – | 8.5 | – | – | – | – | 1.41 (= its own chunk time) | 48.1 |
| solo teacher, bf16 weights | 1.935 / – | 6.2 | – | – | – | – | 1.94 | 65.1 |
| solo exit-2 (nodes 1–2 of 4, = the device stream without a cloud) | 0.765 / – | <u>15.7</u> | – | – | – | – | – | 48.1 |
| solo realtime 2-step (nodes 0,500; §2 realtime mode without SR) | 0.763 / – | <u>15.7</u> | – | – | – | – | – | 48.1 |

Readings. (1) The pipeline works as a pipeline: with two GPUs of the same kind the cloud refines every chunk (13 / 13) at the device's pace, so the corrected (teacher-schedule, 4-forward) stream runs at 15.7 fps throughput with 0.77 s of extra latency behind the shown draft — the single-GPU teacher gets 8.5 fps at a 1.4 s chunk latency. (2) Feeding the corrections back into the device's KV costs a dense clean commit per corrected chunk (0.31 s → 11.2 fps shown instead of 15.7); a partial (last-frame) re-commit or a learned "which chunks matter" rule would cut this, not measured. (3) 50 ms of one-way WAN delay adds 40 ms to the cloud-stream latency and nothing to throughput; the correction lag is quantised by the chunk period (corrections are applied at chunk boundaries). (4) Quality on 12 prompts (Qwen3-VL judge): hyb cloud stream 7.17, nofb cloud 7.33, hyb device 7.08, exit-2 6.92, realtime 2-step 7.17, teacher 7.17, teacher bf16 7.08 — every arm within ±0.33 of the teacher with paired SE 0.2–0.35, i.e. not resolvable at n = 12 (the §2 realtime gap of −0.62 was on 86 clips with the 1.3B draft pipeline); the 43-prompt run (same set as §31) is in progress for the quality claim.

43-prompt quality (10/15 13:15 UTC; TAG a704_43, the §31 TTS prompt set, 704×384, seed 1234, Qwen3-VL judge; timing identical to the 12-prompt table: hyb 0.763 / 1.073 s → 11.2 fps shown, nofb 0.763 → 15.7 fps, teacher 1.405 → 8.5 fps, exit2 0.766, rt2 0.764; cloud refined 10.0 / 13 chunks per clip, latency from show 0.77 s):

| stream (704×384, 43 clips) | fps shown ↑ | latency behind draft ↓ | quality ↑ | drift ↓ | Δq vs solo teacher (paired) | Δdrift |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| solo teacher (4 steps, FP8, one GPU) | 8.5 | 1.41 s (own chunk) | **7.16** | <u>55.3</u> | 0 | 0 |
| nofb cloud stream (device drafts nodes 1–2, cloud finishes 3–4, no feedback) | **15.7** | +0.77 s | <u>7.09</u> | 57.1 | −0.07 ± 0.13 | +1.7 ± 4.0 |
| hyb cloud stream (same + device re-commits the corrections) | 11.2 | +0.77 s | 7.02 | 54.9 | −0.14 ± 0.12 | −0.5 ± 3.7 |
| hyb device stream (shown draft with feedback) | 11.2 | 0 | 6.84 | **54.8** | −0.33 ± 0.12 | −0.6 ± 3.8 |
| solo rt2 (2-node schedule 0,500; realtime mode without SR) | **15.7** | 0 | 6.98 | 56.0 | −0.19 ± 0.13 | +0.7 ± 4.3 |
| solo exit2 (nodes 1–2 of the 4-node schedule = device stream without feedback) | **15.7** | 0 | 6.72 | 59.0 | −0.44 ± 0.15 | +3.6 ± 4.5 |

Paired contrasts (43 clips, wins / ties / losses): hyb device − exit2 +0.12 ± 0.11 (11/26/6, drift −4.2 ± 3.5); hyb cloud − nofb cloud −0.07 ± 0.08 (5/30/8); rt2 − exit2 +0.26 ± 0.12 (14/24/5); rt2 − hyb device +0.14 ± 0.09 (11/27/5); hyb cloud − hyb device +0.19 ± 0.10 (10/31/2).

Readings (43 prompts). (1) **The cloud-corrected stream is at teacher parity at the realtime rate**: nofb cloud −0.07 ± 0.13 vs the solo teacher at 15.7 fps throughput, 0.77 s behind the shown draft, vs 8.5 fps / 1.41 s for the teacher alone — this is the two-machine deliverable: same four forwards, split 2 + 2 across two GPUs, pipelined, 1.85× throughput at the teacher's quality. (2) Feedback (re-committing the corrected chunk into the device KV) buys +0.12 ± 0.11 on the shown stream and nothing on the cloud stream (−0.07 ± 0.08), and costs 4.5 fps shown (0.31 s dense re-commit per chunk) — not worth it as implemented; a partial re-commit would only shrink the cost of a marginal gain, so it is not pursued. (3) The device's draft schedule matters more than feedback: the solo 2-node realtime schedule (σ 1.0 → 0.833) beats the first two nodes of the 4-node schedule (σ 1.0 → 0.937 → 0.833) by +0.26 ± 0.12 and beats the fed-back device stream by +0.14 ± 0.09, with the same cost. So the device should draft with the 2-node schedule and the cloud should refine from that x0 (re-noise to σ 0.833, nodes 3–4) — arms rt2hyb / rt2nofb launched 13:18 UTC on the 43 prompts (run_43b.sh; labels a704_43_rt2hyb_dev / rt2hyb_cloud / rt2nofb_cloud). (4) The 832×480 12-prompt chain (TAG a832, hyb / nofb / d50 / teacher / exit2 / rt2 / teacher_bf16) is running on GPUs 0+1 for the resolution column.

## 36. Where is the redundancy a cheaper 14B forward could exploit? (10/15 16:50–17:10 UTC; specwm/probe_redund.py → lingbot-world-v2/specwm_out/probe/redund{,2,3}.json; specwm/skip_bench.py smoke → specwm_out/skip/smoke; 704×384, 4-node teacher rollout, 3 clips easy_lake / billiard_break / hydraulic_press_can_crush, chunks 3–12, all 40 blocks)
Motivation: the user's direction after a→b→c — make the forward itself cheaper as a contribution, with a verifier deciding acceptance (speculative framing) instead of the open-loop input-similarity gates of the 2026 cache papers (X-Cache 2604.20289 block residuals across chunks, C3ache 2606.08962, WorldCache 2603.22286 cross-step, WAM-Cache 2610.11401 per-token KV refresh by action attention, Sparse-WAM 2609.38984 action-attended tokens, FlowCache 2602.10825 cross-step for 50–64-step AR models). First question: does this model have the redundancy those methods reuse?

Latent / pixel space (43 teacher clips a704_43): per-token (2×2 latent patch) relative change between consecutive latent frames has median 0.76; only 6 % of tokens change < 30 % (static easy_lake 17 %, physics clips 0 %). In pixel space the same clips have 51–66 % (easy_lake) / 10–25 % (physics) of 16×16 patches changing < 20–30 % over 4 frames. The causal VAE latent is not token-wise temporally smooth, so "compute only the changed tokens" cannot be decided in latent space.

Hidden states (probe 1, per-token relative change of block outputs): across denoising steps 0.5–1.0 at every block except the last (0.3); across chunks (same token index or across the boundary) 0.5–1.2; every block's residual is 0.3–0.5 |h| (first block 2.5–3.3, last 0.8–1.5). Fraction of tokens changing < 30 %: 0 % everywhere except block 40 (6–43 %).

Residual reuse (probe 2, what X-Cache / C3ache / TeaCache-style methods reuse; median over chunks, 4 steps):

| metric, blocks 4–37 (first / 39 / 40 in brackets) | value |
|---|---|
| block-input cosine chunk c−1 vs c (X-Cache gate ≥ 0.95–0.97) ↑ | **0.25–0.72** (0.4–0.6 / 0.3–0.6 / 0.45–0.7) |
| residual cosine chunk c−1 vs c ↑ | **0.18–0.51** (0.85–0.93 / 0.5–0.76 / 0.97–0.99) |
| per-token output error if the previous chunk's residual is reused ↓ | **0.39–0.58** (0.36–0.49 / 0.26–0.42 / 0.08–0.14) |
| residual cosine step j−1 vs j, same chunk ↑ | 0.24–0.80 (0.86–0.92 / 0.78–0.89 / 0.99) |
| per-token output error if the previous step's residual is reused ↓ | **0.25–0.55** (0.36–0.47 / 0.17–0.31 / 0.06–0.14) |

End-to-end check (skip_bench, 2-node rt2 draft, blocks 6–39 replaced by K/V-projection-only + previous-chunk residual, teacher-forced vs the full draft on the same noise and state): draft 0.248 s vs 0.911 s per chunk (3.7× cheaper) but relative gap 0.84 (easy_lake) / 1.09 (billiard_break), tokens within 0.3: 2 % / 0 % — unusable, as the probe predicted.

Reading: **the causal-fast 14B has no cross-chunk or cross-step block-residual redundancy in its middle 34 blocks** (which are 85 % of the cost); residual cosines 0.2–0.5 vs the ≥ 0.95 input cosines X-Cache reports for its Wan-2.2 driving model. Structural cause: each chunk starts from fresh noise and every step mixes new noise, so the token hidden states are noise-dominated; the only redundant blocks are the first (embedding) and the last two (read-out), i.e. the cache papers' lever does not exist here, with or without a verifier. A "minimal-information draft" must therefore keep the 14B's noise path and cut tokens or steps instead: (1) lower-resolution draft by the same model (cost ∝ tokens), (2) token-subset draft (probe 3 = spatial predictability of residuals, pending), (3) 1-step draft (known: 34 % within ε). The deployment-relevant gap metric is the cloud's `rel` = |refined − draft| / |draft| (rt2 draft → nodes 3–4 refine: 0.19–0.49 per chunk, 43 clips). Run 10/15 17:15 UTC: twomach/run_43c.sh (12 prompts, no feedback): device drafts at 576×320 / 448×256 (cloud upsamples the latent bicubically before re-noising), device sends the previous chunk (zero compute) or pure noise → how much draft information the σ = 0.833 re-noised cloud refine actually uses.
Probe 3 (spatial predictability, same chunk and step): per-token residual cosine vs the mean of its 4 in-frame neighbours is 0.6–0.85 in the middle blocks (0.92–1.0 at blocks 1 / 39 / 40), but replacing the residual by the neighbour mean still leaves a per-token output error of 0.25–0.36 per block (the residual itself is ~0.4 |h|), i.e. interpolating a token-subset draft recovers only ~25 % of the residual energy per block — the token-subset family (2) is weak too. Family (1), the lower-resolution draft, is the one left; its run is twomach/run_43c.sh (cloud --max-lag 99 so that every chunk is refined: the 576×320 device drafts at 0.48 s/chunk, faster than the cloud's 0.71 s full-res refine, which models a device paced to real time with the cloud keeping up).

## 37. Minimal-draft ladder: how little draft information does the cloud refinement need? (10/15 17:25–18:15 UTC; twomach/run_43c.sh on GPUs 3 (cloud) + 6 (device); TAG a704 = 12 prompts seed 1234, 704×384; report scripts/twomach_report.py a704; VLM twomach/vlm_a704.json)
Step (a) of the verified-cheap-forward plan (§36 closed cache-style reuse). Device drafts of decreasing cost are sent to the cloud, which bicubically upsamples the latent to its own grid, re-noises to σ 0.833 and runs nodes 500, 750 on every chunk (`--max-lag 99`); no feedback (`--thr 1e9`). Draft families: the 2-node rt2 schedule at 704×384 (= rt2nofb / nofb), the same at 576×320 (`--draft-area 184320`, 68 % of the tokens) and 448×256 (`--draft-area 114688`, 42 %), the previous chunk's latent (`--draft prev`, zero compute) and pure noise (`--draft noise`). `rel` = ‖refined − (upsampled) draft‖ / ‖draft‖ per chunk, chunks 3–12.

| draft (device, 12 clips) | device s/chunk ↓ | fps shown ↑ | draft shown: quality ↑ / drift ↓ / Δq vs teacher | cloud `rel` median (q10 / q90) | cloud stream: quality ↑ / drift ↓ / Δq vs teacher | Δdrift |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| teacher (solo 4 steps) | 1.405 | 8.5 | 7.17 / **42.1** / 0 | – | – | – |
| rt2 704×384 (nofb cloud) | 0.762 | 15.7 | **7.17** / <u>48.3</u> / +0.00 ± 0.30 | 0.459 (0.348 / 0.558) | **7.33** / **48.3** / +0.17 ± 0.30 | +6.2 ± 9.7 |
| rt2 576×320 (lr576) | <u>0.479</u> | <u>25.0</u> | <u>6.75</u> / 53.8 / −0.42 ± 0.40 | 0.469 (0.316 / 0.548) | 7.08 / <u>52.9</u> / −0.08 ± 0.23 | +10.8 ± 5.7 |
| rt2 448×256 (lr448) | **0.292** | **41.0** | 6.08 / 68.3 / −1.08 ± 0.26 | 0.461 (0.351 / 0.559) | <u>7.17</u> / 58.3 / +0.00 ± 0.28 | +16.2 ± 9.6 |
| previous chunk (prev, 0 compute) | 0.000 | – | – | 1.030 (1.023 / 1.039) | 3.08 / 92.5 / −4.08 ± 0.48 | +50.4 ± 8.4 |
| pure noise (noise, 0 compute) | 0.001 | – | – | 1.029 (1.020 / 1.040) | 3.25 / 89.2 / −3.92 ± 0.51 | +47.1 ± 8.8 |

Cloud refine 0.764 s/chunk in every arm (its cost is fixed by its own grid), so the cloud stream is capped at 15.7 fps: with the 576 / 448 drafts the device runs ahead (25 / 41 fps) and the cloud's queue grows (cloud-stream latency from show 2.6 / 4.0 s at the end of a 13-chunk clip = backlog, not network); with `--max-lag 1` it would skip 40–60 % of the chunks instead. Device peak memory 38.2 / 30.8 GiB at 576 / 448 vs 48.1 at 704.
Readings. (1) The draft carries the information: with zero-information drafts (prev / noise) the cloud's two steps collapse (3.1–3.3, drift ~90) — the re-noised x0 at σ 0.833 keeps only 17 % of the draft amplitude in z, yet the node-500 x0-prediction reads the scene from it. The cloud stream's teacher parity in §35 is therefore a property of the pair, not of the cloud's two steps. (2) Down to 448×256 (2.6× cheaper draft, 42 % of the tokens) the cloud stream stays at teacher parity on 12 prompts (−0.08 ± 0.23, +0.00 ± 0.28); drift rises monotonically with cheaper drafts (+6 → +11 → +16, the 448 value 1.7 SE). (3) What the device *shows* degrades fast: −0.42 at 576, −1.08 at 448 — the cheap draft is a proposal, not a display. (4) `rel` does not see draft quality: 0.46 ± 0.01 for every model draft (448 included), 1.03 for prev / noise — the cloud's fresh σ 0.833 noise dominates the draft→refined distance, so a latent-distance "acceptance" signal cannot rank model drafts; a verifier has to read the draft's features (→ (b)). (5) The throughput lever is now the cloud step count, not the draft: next arms = cloud runs node 750 only from the device's x0 (the 3-step schedule 0,500,750 split 2+1, cloud 0.38 s = 31 fps), with solo 0,500,750 as its ceiling (run_43d.sh / run_43f.sh).

## 38. One-step cloud: the 3-step schedule 0,500,750 split 2+1 (10/15 18:20–18:46 UTC; twomach/run_43d.sh (GPUs 3+6) + run_43f.sh (GPU 2); TAG a704 = 12 prompts seed 1234, 704×384; report scripts/twomach_report.py a704)
§37 (5): the cloud's two steps (nodes 500, 750 from the re-noised draft; 0.764 s) capped the corrected stream at 15.7 fps. Here the cloud continues the device's 2-node path with node 750 only (`--use 3`: z = 0.375·x0_draft + 0.625·ε, σ 0.625), i.e. the device's nodes 0,500 + the cloud's 750 = the 3-step schedule 0,500,750 split 2+1; solo 0,500,750 is its ceiling.

Readings. (1) One cloud step is enough: c1 / lr576c1 are at teacher parity (+0.08 ± 0.34 / −0.08 ± 0.23) like the 2-step cloud, with the cloud at 0.443 s/chunk (27 fps) and the corrected stream 0.45 s behind the shown one (was 0.77). (2) With the 576×320 draft the pair is balanced: device 25 fps shown, cloud 27 fps, corrected stream 0.44 s behind — the first configuration where both streams exceed 20 fps at 704×384 teacher parity (12 prompts; 43-prompt confirmation running as a704_43/rt2c1, lr576c1). (3) The 2+1 split is a continuation of the same path, so `rel` drops from 0.46 to 0.27–0.29 (q10–q90 0.20–0.34), i.e. the same per-token gap the 10/6 head predicted (AUROC 0.93–0.98 for the nodes-500→750 continuation) — the verifier target of (b). (4) solo3 scores highest on 12 prompts (+0.33 ± 0.28, drift 44.6) at 11.1 fps: the 3-node schedule may simply be a better schedule than the 4-node one at this budget; 43-prompt run on GPU 2 will tell.
