Paired contrasts (43 clips): rt2c1 − rt2c2 −0.07 ± 0.07 (3/34/6), lr576c1 − lr576c2 −0.09 ± 0.06 (2/35/6); lr576c2 − rt2c2 +0.00 ± 0.08, lr576c1 − rt2c1 −0.02 ± 0.08 (drift +3.0…+3.6 ± 3); lr576c1 − lr576 shown +0.37 ± 0.10 (15/27/1), lr576c2 − lr576 shown +0.47 ± 0.10 (19/23/1); rt2c1 − solo rt2 −0.02 ± 0.08; rt2c1 − solo3 −0.26 ± 0.11 (4/26/13); solo3 − teacher +0.05 ± 0.12; rt2c2 − rt2nofb 0.00 (43 ties: the pipeline is deterministic). lr576 shown − solo rt2 −0.42 ± 0.11.
Readings (43). (5) The 12-prompt parity of the 1-step cloud does not hold up: it is 0.07–0.09 below the 2-step cloud (1–1.5 SE, 2–3 wins vs 6 losses) and, on the 704 rt2 draft, no better than the draft itself (−0.02 ± 0.08) — the 2+1 split costs 0.26 ± 0.11 against the same three nodes run in one context (solo3 = teacher at 11.1 fps): the cloud's node-750 step runs in its own (refined) KV context on an x0 the device produced in the draft context, and the σ 0.625 re-noise keeps that mismatch where the σ 0.833 re-noise of the 2-step cloud washes it out. (6) The cloud output does not depend on the draft's resolution (576 vs 704: 0.00 / −0.02 ± 0.08), so the 576 draft (25 fps shown, −0.60) is the device setting; the cloud step count trades 0.08 quality for 0.32 s latency and 1.7× cloud throughput: lr576c2 (−0.14, 0.76 s/chunk, cannot keep up with a 25-fps device alone) vs lr576c1 (−0.23, 0.44 s, 0.45 s behind). (7) solo3 (3 nodes 0,500,750, 11.1 fps) equals the 4-node teacher (+0.05 ± 0.12): the cheapest teacher-parity single-GPU setting at 704 so far (was 4 nodes 8.5 fps).

## 39. Draft-verifier head on the device's own features (step (b)/(c); 10/15 19:00–19:55 UTC; data = the 43-prompt two-machine runs with `two_machine.py --dump-feat 1` (twomach/a704_43/{rt2c2,lr576c2,rt2c1,lr576c1}/device/*/feat.pt), trainer scripts/draft_head.py → specwm_out/drafthead/<arm>/{head_fold*.pt,result.json,.log})
Setup: per chunk the device saves the last block's hidden after each of its two draft steps (per-channel standardized with chan_norm_14b.pt, random-projected 5120→1536 with gap_dump_v3/proj.pt, 2×2 pooled → [3 frames, 12×22 (704) or 10×18 (576), 1536], fp16; 63 MB/clip). Label = per-token gap between the (upsampled) draft and what the cloud returned, ‖refined − draft‖ / median token norm of the refined chunk, pooled to the feature grid; chunks 0–2 excluded. Head = the 10/6 Speculative-Forcing head (LN → Conv3d 1536→96→48→1, 4 M params, Huber, Adam 1e-4, 30 epochs, 15 s per fold), 5-fold cross-validation over the 43 tasks (every chunk scored by a head that never saw its task). Gates for (c): head chunk-mean score; X-Cache-style open-loop gates = 1 − cosine of the device feature (chunk c vs c−1) and 1 − cosine of the draft latent (c vs c−1); random; oracle = true chunk-mean gap.

| rt2 704 + 1-step cloud (rt2c1) | 0.264 | 0.890 / 0.886 / 0.896 | 0.801 | 0.847 / 0.854 | <u>0.927</u> |
| 576 + 1-step cloud (lr576c1) | 0.280 | **0.917 / 0.904 / 0.912** | **0.849** | 0.833 / <u>0.855</u> | 0.900 |

| 1 − latent cosine c vs c−1 | <u>0.298</u> | <u>0.273 / 0.26</u> | <u>0.263 / 0.52</u> | <u>0.244 / 0.78</u> |
| random | −0.012 | 0.277 / 0.25 | 0.278 / 0.50 | 0.280 / 0.75 |

Same picture in the other three arms (rt2c2: head ρ 0.81, cosines 0.12 / 0.21; lr576c2: 0.86 vs 0.26 / 0.33; rt2c1: 0.85 vs 0.09 / 0.17).
Readings. (1) The device's features know what the cloud will change: token AUROC 0.83–0.92, chunk-level ρ 0.81–0.86 on held-out scenes, with the step-1 features almost as good as step-2 (0.81–0.85) — the decision can be made before the second draft step. (2) The label of the 1-step cloud (same-path continuation, median 0.26–0.28) is more predictable than the 2-step cloud's resampling-dominated label (0.44–0.45): AUROC 0.89–0.92 vs 0.85–0.88. (3) Open-loop similarity gates — what the 2026 cache papers use — are near random here (ρ 0.1–0.3): under a 50 % budget the head leaves the unrefined chunks at 0.244 mean gap (oracle 0.240, 98 % of the oracle's reduction from random 0.278); the cosine gates reach 0.263–0.267 (40 %). (4) Cost of the head at inference: one 1536-channel projection + three small 3-D convs per chunk (< 5 ms); no pixels, no latent inspection. End-to-end test (run_43g/h.sh: 576 draft + 1-step cloud, the device sends only the chunks its gate flags at 50 % / 25 % budget; head vs random vs latent cosine; 43 prompts) running 19:55–21:00 UTC.

## 40. End-to-end gate test and feedback with the 576 draft (10/15 19:55–21:40 UTC; twomach/run_43g.sh (GPUs 3+6: g50head / g50rand / g50cos), run_43h.sh (GPUs 0+1: g25head / g25rand), run_43i.sh (GPU 2, both roles → timing invalid: lr576c1hyb, gdrift, gdrifthyb); 43 prompts seed 1234, 704×384 cloud, 576×320 device draft, 1-step cloud (node 750); `two_machine.py --gate head|random|cos --gate-budget B` — chunks 0–2 always sent, the cloud *accepts* (commits the upsampled draft as-is) every chunk the device does not flag)
Setup: the device's gate decides per chunk whether the cloud refines it (head = held-out-fold draft-verifier head of §39 with the global threshold that sends B of the pooled chunks; cos = 1 − latent cosine c vs c−1; random p = B). Accepted chunks are still committed by the cloud (full-res dense forward + VAE decode = 0.32 s) so the cloud stream stays continuous; refined chunks cost 0.44 s.

| arm (43 clips) | chunks refined / 13 (incl. 0–2) | cloud s per chunk ↓ (accept / refine / mean) | cloud-stream latency from show ↓ | fps shown ↑ | cloud quality ↑ | drift ↓ | Δq vs teacher | Δq vs lr576c1 (all refined, paired) | Δq vs 576 draft shown (paired) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| lr576c1: every chunk refined | 13.0 | – / 0.443 / 0.558 | 0.45 s | 25.1 | **6.93** | **57.2** | **−0.23 ± 0.10** | 0 | **+0.37 ± 0.10** |
| g50head (head gate, 50 %) | 8.0 | 0.320 / 0.443 / 0.474 | 0.42 s | 25.1 | <u>6.56</u> | <u>57.7</u> | <u>−0.60 ± 0.15</u> | −0.37 ± 0.10 (2/25/16) | +0.00 ± 0.07 (5/33/5) |
| g50cos (latent-cosine gate, 50 %) | 8.2 | 0.320 / 0.443 / 0.476 | 0.44 s | 25.2 | <u>6.56</u> | 61.5 | <u>−0.60 ± 0.17</u> | −0.37 | +0.00 ± 0.12 |
| g50rand (random, 50 %) | 8.3 | 0.320 / 0.443 / 0.485 | 0.44 s | 25.2 | 6.42 | 61.3 | −0.74 ± 0.13 | −0.51 | −0.14 |
| g25head (head gate, 25 %) | 5.5 | 0.320 / 0.442 / 0.435 | 0.32 s | 25.2 | 6.26 | 59.2 | −0.91 ± 0.19 | −0.68 | −0.30 ± 0.12 (3/27/13) |
| g25rand (random, 25 %) | 5.7 | 0.320 / 0.443 / – | 0.32 s | 25.3 | 6.33 | 61.6 | −0.84 ± 0.14 | −0.61 | −0.23 |
| 576 draft shown (device stream) | – | – | 0 | 25.1 | 6.56 | 58.4 | −0.60 ± 0.14 | −0.37 | 0 |

Paired gate-vs-gate: g50head − g50rand +0.14 ± 0.09 (10/30/3), drift −3.6 ± 3.0; g50head − g50cos +0.00 ± 0.12, drift −3.8 ± 1.9; g25head − g25rand −0.07 ± 0.10. Per clip (g50head): clips with 10–13 chunks refined still lose −0.26 vs lr576c1; clips with ≤ 4 refined lose −0.58.
Why gating fails here (frames, newtons_cradle chunks 2–7, scratchpad strip_gate_newtons.png): an accepted chunk is the bicubically upsampled 576 draft decoded at 704 — visibly softer than its refined neighbours (poster text, window frame) — so a 50 % gate produces a stream that alternates sharp / soft every 0.75 s; the judge scores it like the all-soft draft stream (+0.00 vs the shown draft) and a 25 % gate below it. With a low-resolution draft, what the 1-step cloud adds to *every* chunk is resolution, which no per-chunk gate can skip; the head ranks chunks by trajectory gap (ρ 0.86, §39) but the gap is not what the judge sees. The cloud does not even save much: an accepted chunk still costs the dense commit + decode (0.32 s vs 0.44 s), so 50 % gating cuts cloud time per chunk by 15 % (0.558 → 0.474) and latency by 0.03 s.
Feedback with the 576 draft (lr576c1hyb: cloud-corrected 704 chunk downsampled to the 576 grid and re-committed into the device KV; GPU 2, both roles): device stream −0.65 ± 0.12 vs teacher = −0.05 ± 0.13 vs the no-feedback draft (6/30/7), drift −3.7 ± 3.8; cloud stream −0.21 ± 0.11 = +0.02 ± 0.09 vs lr576c1, drift −4.4 ± 3.8; re-commit 0.41 s → 10.0 fps shown. Frames (strip_fb_*.png): pouring_water keeps the glass jug through 9 s with feedback (the no-feedback draft turns it into a metal pitcher at 3 s) but domino_falling hallucinates people at 3–5 s with feedback (5–9 s without) — feedback changes the sample, it does not keep the trajectory on the teacher's.
Readings. (1) **(c) closed for the low-res pipeline**: the learned gate beats random by +0.14 ± 0.09 and ties cosine, but any gate < 100 % loses the whole +0.37 the cloud adds, because the cloud's per-chunk contribution with a low-res draft is super-resolution, not correction. The verifier's ranking is real (§39) but the quantity it ranks (trajectory gap) is not the quantity the viewer loses. (2) A gate can only pay off where accept and refine look the same (same-resolution draft, rt2) — and there the 1-step cloud adds nothing (rt2c1 − rt2 = −0.02 ± 0.08, §38), so there is nothing to gate. (3) **(a) closed**: feedback with the 576 draft buys no quality (−0.05 / +0.02) and a non-significant drift −4, at 0.41 s/chunk (25 → 10 fps). (4) The head remains a usable departure-point *detector* (§39); its use is monitoring / deciding when to re-anchor, not skipping cloud work. Drift-head gate (gdrift / gdrifthyb, run_43i.sh) and rt2c1hyb (run_43j.sh) pending.

| arm | chunks refined / 13 | fps shown ↑ | cloud-stream lat. ↓ | cloud q ↑ / drift ↓ | Δq cloud vs teacher | device q ↑ | Δq device vs its no-feedback draft | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| gdrift (576 draft, drift head τ −3.25 gate, no fb) | 9.4 | (11.1, invalid) | – | 6.56 / 56 | −0.60 ± 0.18 | – | – | = draft stream (+0.00 ± 0.17), = g50head (+0.00 ± 0.17), −0.37 vs all-refined |
| gdrifthyb (drift gate + feedback) | 9.2 | (9.5) | – | 6.56 / 57 | −0.58 ± 0.14 | 6.67 | +0.12 ± 0.13 (11/25/7), drift −3.5 ± 2.6 | cloud +0.02 ± 0.12 vs gdrift |
| rt2c1hyb (704 rt2 draft + 1-step cloud + feedback) | 13 | **11.2** (re-commit 0.309 s) | 0.45 s | **7.09 / 53** | **−0.07 ± 0.10** (5/29/9) | 7.00 | +0.02 ± 0.10 vs solo rt2, drift −4.4 ± 3.4 | **+0.14 ± 0.10 (8/31/4) vs rt2c1 (no fb)**; = rt2hyb (2-step cloud + fb) exactly: +0.00 ± 0.09 cloud, +0.00 ± 0.09 device |

Readings. (5) The 10/9 drift head as a gate behaves exactly like the learned draft head and the random gate: with a low-res draft any partial refinement leaves the soft accepted chunks in the stream (−0.60 = draft). It flags 70 % of chunks at τ −3.25 (clean-chunk 95th pct), so it is also not selective on this bench. (b) closed. (6) **With the same-resolution rt2 draft, feedback makes the 1-step cloud as good as the 2-step cloud**: rt2c1hyb cloud −0.07 ± 0.10 vs teacher, +0.14 vs the no-feedback 1-step stream, and tied (+0.00) with rt2hyb on both streams — the device re-anchoring to the cloud's chunk removes the context mismatch that cost the 2+1 split −0.26 against solo3 (§38). Cost: shown stream 15.7 → 11.2 fps (0.31 s dense re-commit), cloud 0.44 s/chunk (vs 0.76 for the 2-step cloud), stream 0.45 s behind. So the cheapest teacher-parity two-machine config is now rt2c1hyb: 704 draft 2 steps + 1 cloud step + feedback, cloud work 1.7× lower than §35's recommendation at the same quality; the price is the shown-stream fps. (7) Low-res draft + feedback (lr576c1hyb / gdrifthyb) stays at −0.58…−0.65 on the device stream: the 576 draft's loss is resolution, which feedback cannot return.

## 42. Edge-only baseline: ABot-World-0-5B-LF on our prompts (10/15 23:10 – 10/16 00:00 UTC; tools/abot (clone + venv torch 2.8 cu128 / flash-attn 2.8.1 / SageAttention / lightx2v_kernel patched for torch 2.8), weights hf/ABot-World-0-5B-LF; driver tools/abot/bench_ours.py (per-block CUDA-synchronised DiT + VAE time, TTFF, steady fps), keys tools/abot/poses_to_keys.py (our camera path → W/A/S/D/I/J/K/L per 12-frame block); runs specwm_out/abot/{a1280,a704}/<task>; judge labels a704_43_abot1280 / abot704 in vlm_a704_43.json; GPU 2 = RTX PRO 6000)
What it is: Alibaba AMAP's causal student of a bidirectional Wan2.2-TI2V-5B teacher (teacher forcing → ODE distillation → LongForcing DMD on self-rollouts), the same 4-node schedule [1000, 750, 500, 250] shift 5 and 3-latent-frame (12-frame) blocks as LingBot, local KV window 21 latent frames, native 1280×704 at 12 fps, keyboard-only control (8 keys), reference-character memory. Official: 720p up to 16 fps, 1.2 s action-to-first-frame, ≤ 19.3 GiB on one RTX 5090 (FP8 + LightVAE + memory-aware scheduling; FP8 default 12.4–13.3 fps), "verified on RTX 4090". Our run: FP8 per-tensor, taew2_2 tiny VAE, T5 resident (no offload), 13 blocks, same 12 prompts (task image + caption + camera path converted to keys; physics tasks share one orbit path, so their keys are identical).

| ABot-World-0 5B | 704×384 (out of distribution) | 8 keys | 1.24 s | 0.499 / 0.516 | 23.3 | 1.94× | **22.5** | 5.00 | 76 | −2.33 ± 0.50 (0/1/11) |

Subsets (ABot 1280 − our teacher): easy_* roaming (4) −1.75 ± 0.25, physics (8) −0.50 ± 0.27; vs our rt2 −0.67 ± 0.40 (all), roaming −1.50 ± 0.65.
Frames (scratchpad strip_abot_*.png, looked at): easy_lake 1280 keeps the tree and lake under the orbit (a cloud-reflection blob at 6 s), 704×384 collapses into sky/water blur by 4 s; pouring_water 1280 stops pouring at once, the jug is gone by 2 s and the camera wanders over an empty table (a roaming model: it does not act out the caption's event; ours keeps the jug and pour), 704 collapses; easy_urban_rain 1280 walks the wet street (W) then our pitch-down keys (K) leave it staring at the asphalt from 4 s on — the key conversion, not only the model, costs it that prompt.
Readings. (1) **Speed/memory**: ABot's 5B at 720p runs 13.3 fps (1.11× its 12-fps real time) with 32 GiB here (19 GiB with their T5 offload) — the same 4-node/3-frame recipe as ours at 2.8× our pixel count; per pixel it is ~2.4× cheaper than our 14B rt2 (0.90 s for 3520 vs 0.76 s for 1056 tokens/frame), which is the 5B-vs-14B ratio, not a method difference. TTFF 1.5 s (first block runs with an unfilled cache, 1.23 s + decode) vs our 0.76 s. (2) **Quality** on our prompts is below our 704 teacher by 0.9 and below the 704 rt2 draft by 0.7 (12 prompts), with +20 drift; at 704×384 it is unusable (−2.3, scene collapse), so a matched-resolution comparison at our resolutions is not possible for ABot — the matched comparison has to be at 1280×704 (our a1280 rows, GPU 3, pending). (3) Caveats for the table: keyboard conversion is lossy (8 discrete keys; pitch keys over-rotate), physics prompts are outside ABot's roaming domain, its native fps is 12 (13-s clips) vs our 16 (10-s clips), and the judge is ours. The fair edge-only comparison per Guandao is: same GPU budget (24–32 GB), each model at its native resolution, roaming prompts — on that cut ABot is 13.3 fps / 32 GiB / 6.0 vs our rt2 15.7 fps / 48 GiB (17 GiB GPTQ-4 at 11.3 fps) / 7.5 on the 4 roaming prompts. (4) Portability: ABot's schedule and chunking are identical to ours, so the device/cloud step split, KV-window fit and feedback can be demonstrated on it unchanged (its FP8 + LightVAE stack is already the device side); this is the natural second model for the "scalable recipe" claim.

Readings. (5) At ABot's native resolution our 14B is 1.4–1.6 points better on the judge (10–11 of 12 clips, roaming −2.25) with 13–17 less drift, but 3.6× slower (3.7 vs 13.3 fps) and 2.6× the memory (84 vs 32 GiB; 24 GB fit impossible at 720p without the §18 reductions). The quality/compute trade is model size: 14B at 0.23× real time vs 5B at 1.1×. (6) This is the honest edge-only comparison table for Guandao's bar: ABot wins speed and memory at 720p, we win quality everywhere; neither runs our 704×384 realtime point competitively (ABot collapses off-resolution). Our device-side realtime claim (15.7 fps at 704, 25 fps with the cloud split) therefore has to be compared against ABot at 720p by *pixels per second*: ours 704×384×15.7 = 4.2 Mpix/s (14B), ABot 1280×704×13.3 = 12.0 Mpix/s (5B); or against a 5B model of ours — which is the portability experiment (§42 reading 4).

## 43. Bidirectional 14B quality: official 70-step CFG vs 4-step LoRA vs the causal-fast teacher (user Q3 of 10/15; DONE 10/16 01:45 UTC; specwm_out/bid/worker.sh work queue on GPUs 0,1,4,5,6, outputs bid/bidl4/<task>/video.mp4 and bid/bid70/<task>/video.mp4, judged into twomach/vlm_a832.json as a832_bidl4 / a832_bid70)
Setting: the 12 prompts of §41 at 832x480, 161 frames, seed 1234, bid 14B (`bid_as_v1_cam`) FP8 both experts resident; l4 = 4-step lightx2v LoRA no CFG (shift 5), 70 = official 70 steps CFG 5 (shift 10). Paired against the a832 rows (same prompts, judge = ours). No 70B checkpoint exists locally and the official 14B causal model is only released as the 4-step fast version, so the chain "70B bid → 14B 70-step AR → 14B 4-step AR" cannot be measured; this is the closest available comparison.

| model (832x480, 12 prompts) | s / clip ↓ | fps ↑ | streaming (actions mid-clip) | quality ↑ | drift ↓ | Δq vs causal teacher (paired) | Δdrift vs teacher |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| bid 14B, official 70-step CFG | 2850 (47 min) | 0.06 | no (whole clip) | **8.00** | **25.8** | **+0.42 ± 0.23** (7/3/2) | **−12.5 ± 9.7** |
| bid 14B, 4-step LoRA (plan sampler) | 99 | 1.6 | no | 7.42 | <u>29.2</u> | −0.17 ± 0.34 (4/2/6) | −9.2 ± 8.5 |
| causal-fast 14B teacher 4-step FP8 | 23.4 | 6.9 | yes (0.75 s chunks) | 7.58 | 38.3 | 0 | 0 |
| causal-fast teacher bf16 | — | — | yes | <u>7.75</u> | 44.6 | +0.17 | +6.3 |
| causal-fast solo3 (0,500,750) | 20.9 | 7.7 | yes | 7.67 | 43.8 | +0.08 ± 0.23 | +5.5 |
(bid70 − bidl4 = +0.58 ± 0.23 (7/4/1); bid70 − solo3 +0.33 ± 0.26; bid70 − teacher_bf16 +0.25 ± 0.18.)

Frames (scratchpad bid_cmp.mp4, pouring + domino at 2/5/8 s): both bid samplers keep the jug, glass and a visible water stream through 8 s, and bid70 adds a sharper background and a longer, straighter domino row; the causal teacher follows the orbiting camera far more (the kitchen swings through the frame, a second glass appears) and its drift is partly that camera motion. Caveat: the bid clips move the camera much less than the causal clips on the same poses (the bid plan conditioning is the same plucker stream but the whole-clip sampler damps it), so part of the drift advantage is "less motion", not better physics.
Reading: the 70-step bid is the best-looking model we have (+0.4 judge, −12 drift) but it is 120x slower than the causal teacher, 800x slower than realtime mode, and not interactive. The 4-step bid LoRA is not better than the causal teacher (−0.17, 4/2/6) — step distillation already cost the bid its advantage, so "use the 70-step model" means distilling again, which is the route LingBot took to make causal-fast. The bid70 clips are the natural "ground truth"/teacher for a correction-trained cloud model (§44 plan) and for DMD-style re-distillation; they are not a runtime candidate (confirms §33).

## 44. Pruning pilot: untrained depth removal on the causal-fast 14B (user Q "pruning해서 5B로 못 만드나?"; DONE 10/16 02:10 UTC; two_machine --skip-blocks (block forward = identity, K/V of the skipped blocks untouched), twomach/run_skip.sh, outputs twomach/a704_43/skip{4,10,20}, judged as a704_43_skip*)
Setting: teacher config (4 nodes, 704x384, FP8, window 18), 12 prompts seed 1234; blocks removed uniformly between 2 and 37 (blocks 0-1 and 38-39 kept): 4 / 10 / 20 of 40 = 36 / 30 / 20 active blocks ≈ 12.6 / 10.5 / 7 B active parameters. No training of any kind.

| 14B teacher config, 704x384, 12 prompts | active blocks | s / chunk ↓ | fps ↑ | quality ↑ | drift ↓ | Δq vs full teacher (paired) | Δdrift |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| full (reference) | 40 | 1.406 | 8.5 | **7.33** | **37.5** | 0 | 0 |
| rt2 2-node (reference, realtime mode) | 40 (2 steps) | 0.760 | 15.8 | <u>7.08</u> | <u>50.0</u> | −0.25 | +12.5 |
| skip 4 (10 %) | 36 | 1.266 | 9.5 | 5.92 | 63.8 | −1.42 ± 0.40 (1/0/11) | +26.2 ± 6.5 |
| skip 10 (25 %) | 30 | 1.054 | 11.4 | 3.25 | 90.4 | −4.08 ± 0.38 (0/0/12) | +52.9 ± 6.2 |
| skip 20 (50 %, "7B") | 20 | 0.711 | 16.9 | 3.00 | 91.7 | −4.33 ± 0.40 (0/0/12) | +54.2 ± 6.9 |
Reading: the distilled 14B has no slack in depth — removing 10 % of the blocks already costs 1.4 judge points (more than dropping half the denoising steps: rt2 −0.25 at 54 % of the time), and 25 % collapses the model. Time scales with the active fraction (36/40 → 0.90x), so the speed side of pruning is as expected. A "5–10 B LingBot" therefore needs a full knowledge-distillation recovery (TinyFusion-scale: learnable block masks + distillation against the 40-block teacher for a few percent of pre-training compute), not fine-tuning on top of a nearly intact model; the KD target is deterministic (same x_t, t, noise → teacher x0), so the regression objective is valid here, but the data/compute (tens of thousands of teacher-forced chunks, multi-day on 6 GPUs) puts it behind the device refiner (§45 plan) and the 2-step distillation in priority. Untrained pruning: closed.

## 45. Could the bid 14B 4-step LoRA serve as a cloud planner over a short action window? Window cost (user Q1 of 10/16; DONE 10/16 02:40 UTC; specwm_out/bidwin/run.sh, GPU 0, plan_gen --steps 4 --cfg-steps 0 --shift 5 --lora 1022 --fp8 1, easy_lake seed 1234)
fwd_s = the 4 bid forwards over the whole window; gen_s = whole call incl. VAE encode/decode, T5, LoRA merge overheads (fixed ≈ 12–18 s per call, would be amortised in a resident server). Window = chunks of 12 frames (0.75 s of video).

| bid 4-step window | 2 chunks (25 fr) | 4 (49) | 6 (73) | 8 (97) | 13 (161, whole clip) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 576x320: 4 forwards s ↓ / per chunk ↓ | 3.8 / **1.9** | 7.7 / 1.9 | 11.5 / 1.9 | 15.8 / 2.0 | 28.5 / 2.2 |
| 576x320: gen_s (with fixed overhead) | 16.4 | 22.4 | 25.2 | 29.5 | 43.1 |
| 704x384: 4 forwards s / per chunk | 6.0 / 3.0 | 11.6 / 2.9 | 17.9 / 3.0 | 24.9 / 3.1 | 47.2 / 3.6 |
| 704x384: gen_s | 21.8 | 25.9 | 34.2 | 41.3 | 65.8 |
| causal-fast reference (per chunk, 704, 4 steps / 2 steps) | 1.41 / 0.76 | | | | |
Reading: the bid cost is linear in the window at these sizes (attention is not yet the bottleneck below 100 frames), ≈ 1.9 s per chunk at 576 and 3.0 s at 704 for the denoising alone = 2.5–4x slower than real time (0.75 s/chunk) and 1.4–2.1x slower than the causal teacher's full 4 steps at 704; there is no window length at which a bid planner keeps up with the stream, even before the fixed overhead and before the action-horizon problem (the window's actions must be known in advance). As a re-anchoring corrector it would also be 4–7x the causal cloud's 1-step (0.44 s). Bid 4-step as a cloud planner/corrector: closed; the bid models remain data/teacher only (§33, §43).

## §46 Stage 1 — trained device refiner (10/16, bench-43, judge Qwen3-VL-32B)
Design: device draft 576x320 (nodes 0,500) → bicubic latent upsample to the 704 grid → small 3D U-Net (input xd_up + the cloud's node-750 eps draw, MSE to the cloud's one-step output) → shown. Data: 47 train tasks x 3 seeds (141 clips, specwm_out/refiner/pairs). Latent-level training works: val rel-MSE (1.0 = raw upsampled draft) r1 20M 0.712 (overfits after 2k steps), r2 no-eps 0.784, r3 5M 0.679 (stable). On the bench the refined stream is 32–38 % closer to the cloud latent than the raw upsample (easy_lake 0.059 → 0.041, pouring 0.092 → 0.070).
Judge (Δquality ↑ / Δdrift ↓, paired, n=43):

| stream | vs raw 576 draft (lr576_dev) | vs cloud 1-step stream (lr576c1_cloud) | vs teacher |
|---|---|---|---|
| raw 576 draft, native decode (lr576_dev) | 0 | −0.37 | −0.60 |
| raw draft bicubic-upsampled to 704, decoded (lr576_raw_up, n=2) | −0.50 | | |
| + refiner r1 (lr576ref) | −2.60 ± 0.18 (0/2/41), drift +14.0 | −2.98 | −3.21 |
| + refiner r3 (lr576ref3) | −2.47 ± 0.20 (0/4/39), drift +10.6 | −2.84 | −3.07 |
| cloud 704 stream bicubic-DOWNsampled to the 576 grid, decoded (lr576_cloud_down) | −0.21 ± 0.09 (3/28/12) | −0.58 ± 0.10 (0/20/23) | −0.81 |
| lr576ref_c1hyb cloud stream (refiner on both sides, feedback) | | −0.07 ± 0.11 (7/28/8) vs lr576c1_cloud | −0.30 ± 0.13 |

Frames (easy_lake 1/5/9 s): the refined rows carry a mosaic/grid texture over the whole image; the trajectory is the raw draft's. Cause: bicubic resampling of Wan latents is not a valid picture operation — the VAE decodes the interpolated latent with a tiled high-frequency pattern (upsample) or a 0.58-point loss (downsample, even from the good cloud clip). The cloud's one-step at σ 0.625 re-noises and re-synthesises the high frequencies, so the two-machine results of §38–§41 were never exposed to it; a device-side net trained with MSE removes the low-frequency part of the gap only. Closed: ANY device-side use of a resampled latent (either direction). The refiner line continues only on a shared grid: stage 1b = 704 rt2 draft → cloud one-step on the same grid (pairs704) → refiner → shown (a704_43/rt2ref). Bug fixed on the way: RefinerNet.refine ran without no_grad on the cloud's accept/skip path → 94 GiB OOM on the first clip of lr576ref_g50hyb (gate arm not measured).

### §46b Stage 1b — same-grid refiner (no resampling): closed
Pairs: device 704x384 rt2 draft (nodes 0,500) → cloud one-step at node 750 on the same grid (141 clips, specwm_out/refiner/pairs704); r3 config (5M, lr 1e-4, 8k steps, 4 min): val rel-MSE 0.699 (1.0 = raw draft). Solo rt2 + refiner (a704_43/rt2ref, 0.757 s/chunk = 15.9 fps shown, refiner cost ≈ 0):

| rt2ref vs | Δquality ↑ | win/tie/loss | Δdrift ↓ |
|---|---|---|---|
| rt2 (raw draft, −0.62 vs teacher) | −0.81 ± 0.10 | 0/13/30 | +7.1 |
| rt2c1hyb cloud stream | −0.93 ± 0.16 | 0/17/26 | +10.2 |
| teacher | −1.00 ± 0.17 | 1/13/29 | +7.8 |
| lr576_dev (raw 576 draft) | −0.40 ± 0.13 | 3/25/15 | +4.8 |

Reading: with the resampling artefact removed, a 30 % latent-MSE reduction toward the cloud's one-step output still LOWERS judged quality by 0.8 (never a win on 43 clips): the MSE-optimal map is the eps-conditional mean of the cloud step, i.e. a blur of the draft, and the judge penalises blur more than the draft's own softness (same mechanism as §24 step-1 self-distillation and the 10/11 1.3B refiner). Deterministic target was not enough — the target itself (one 14B step from a shared eps) is still a 14B function a 5–20M net cannot represent, and what it can represent is the mean. Device refiner line closed for regression losses; a perceptual/adversarial refiner would be a distillation project (stage 4), not a cheap head. Device-side sharpness therefore stays with the draft itself (rt2 / lr576 as measured); the cloud's value remains trajectory feedback (§41).

## §47 Step-1 verifier: chunk-wise adaptive 2/3-step (A) and early send of the step-1 estimate (B) (10/16, bench-43, 704x384, judge Qwen3-VL-32B)
Head: the rt2c1 draft-verifier head (specwm_out/drafthead/rt2c1, one conv head trained jointly on step-1 and step-2 features, scored per step; step-1 chunk Spearman 0.847 / top-50 AUROC 0.927, as accurate as step-2). Fold = the task's held-out fold. two_machine.py: `--adapt-budget B --adapt-node 2 --gate-dir DIR` (after draft step 1 the head scores the chunk; above the budget-B step-1 threshold the chunk gets a third node at 750; chunks 0–2 always extended), `--send-step 1` (device sends its step-1 x0 estimate to the cloud at once, finishes step 2 locally; the cloud's correction is fed back as before).

**A. adaptive steps, solo (nodes 0,500 + 750 on flagged chunks)** — Δquality ↑ (paired, n=43), steady s/chunk ↓, fps ↑ (median / mean over clips):

| arm | vs rt2 | vs solo3 | vs teacher | s/chunk | fps | extended chunks |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| rt2 (2-step, −0.62 vs teacher, §35) | 0 | | −0.62 | 0.765 | **15.7 / 15.7** | 0 / 430 |
| solo3 (3-step, every chunk) | | 0 | +0.05 (8/27/8) | 1.085 | 11.1 / 11.1 | 430 / 430 |
| adapt25 (budget 0.25) | +0.14 ± 0.11 (12/24/7) | −0.09 ± 0.09 (5/29/9) | −0.05 ± 0.10 (6/28/9) | <u>0.770</u> | <u>15.6 / 15.3</u> | 92 / 430 (21 %) |
| adapt50 (budget 0.50) | **+0.16 ± 0.10** (11/27/5) | **−0.07 ± 0.08** (4/32/7) | **−0.02 ± 0.10** (4/32/7) | 0.952 | 12.6 / 13.2 | 201 / 430 (47 %) |

adapt50 − adapt25 = +0.02 ± 0.06 (4/36/3): the gain sits in the ~20 % of chunks the step-1 head ranks highest; extending more buys nothing. Flips adapt25 vs rt2: wins lightbulb_and_switch 4→6, easy_greatwall 7→8, seesaw_balance, newtons_cradle, upipe_equilibrium, string_pull_lamp, hydraulic_press_can_crush_10 (+1 each, 12 wins); losses domino_falling, syringe_hydraulic, traffic_light_green, plastic_bottle_hand_crush (−1 each, 7 losses). Reading: the 3rd step is paid only where the step-1 verifier flags the chunk → the realtime mode (15.3 fps mean) moves from −0.62 to −0.05 vs teacher at the cost of a +1 % median chunk time (the flagged chunks take 1.09 s, so frame pacing is uneven: mean fps 15.3 vs median 15.6). Caveat: 1-point judge granularity, ± 0.10.

**B. early send (device sends the step-1 x0 to the cloud, keeps showing its own 2-step draft; feedback as in rt2c1hyb)** — Δquality ↑:

| arm | cloud stream vs rt2c1hyb_cloud | cloud vs teacher | device vs rt2c1hyb_dev | device vs rt2 | device fps shown ↑ | lag median ↓ | applied |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| rt2c1hyb (§40, step-2 draft sent) | 0 | −0.07 | 0 | +0.02 | **11.1** | 1.06 s | 473 / 559 |
| rt2e1c1hyb (step-1 sent, cloud 1 step) | −0.26 ± 0.08 (1/31/11) | −0.33 ± 0.11 (3/23/17) | −0.21 ± 0.09 (2/31/10) | −0.19 ± 0.10 (5/25/13) | 10.0 | **0.32 s** | **510 / 559** |
| rt2e1c2hyb (step-1 sent, cloud 2 steps) | −0.40 ± 0.12 (1/28/14); −0.33 ± 0.10 vs rt2c2_cloud | −0.47 ± 0.15 (3/19/21) | −0.12 ± 0.09 (2/34/7) | −0.09 ± 0.10 (6/26/11) | **11.1** | 1.06 s | 473 / 559 |

Lag does drop below the chunk time (0.32 s: corrections alternate age 0 / age 1, 12 of 13 applied per clip) but the corrected stream is WORSE than with the finished draft (−0.26, 1 win / 11 losses) and the device stream follows it down (−0.21). Frames (hydraulic_press_can_crush, lightbulb_and_switch, newtons_cradle; teacher / rt2c1hyb cloud+dev / rt2e1c1hyb cloud+dev): all arms leave the initial scene on these camera-motion tasks (teacher too); the early-send arms follow a different trajectory with more smeared frames (hydraulic: blurred pipes at 7–9 s; lightbulb: blurred hand at 4–6 s), while the cloud and device rows of the same arm match (feedback works). Reading: the cloud's one-step at σ 0.625 re-noises its input and regenerates; a step-1 x0 (σ-1 → 0 estimate, 0.35 σ from the final) is a cruder input than the step-2 draft and the one-step correction cannot recover it; two cloud steps (c2) do not help either (−0.33 vs rt2c2 with the finished draft) and lose the lag gain (cloud time > chunk time → lag back to 1.06 s). Closed: send the finished draft; lag < chunk time needs a faster cloud step, not an earlier send.

Conclusion §47: (A) step-1 verifier + chunk-wise 3rd step = new realtime recommendation at 704 (adapt25: −0.05 vs teacher, 15.3 fps mean); (B) early send closed. Combination not run (A on device + cloud feedback on flagged chunks only) — open.

## §48 Stage 3 — depth-pruned 14B student with KD LoRA (10/16, bench-43, 704x384 rt2 role, judge Qwen3-VL-32B)
Setup: blocks bypassed (identity) + rank-128 LoRA (q,k,v,o,ffn) on the remaining blocks, trained on the 2-node teacher dumps (kd_data/n2_704, 47 train tasks, nodes 0/500 with the teacher's exact inputs, teacher-forced history), 3240 steps (2 epochs, ~4.6 h on one GPU, 81 GiB). Runtime: solo rt2 with `--student kd_data/studentN/lora.pt` (student commits its own chunks).

| student | blocks skipped | val rel-MSE node0 / node1 (untrained → trained) | Δq vs rt2 ↑ | Δq vs teacher ↑ | Δdrift ↓ vs rt2 | steady s/chunk ↓ | fps ↑ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| rt2 (full 40 blocks) | 0 | – | 0 | −0.62 | 0 | 0.765 | 15.7 |
| student16 | 16 (20,22–27,29–37) | 0.945 → 0.167 / 0.637 → 0.086 | −4.00 ± 0.14 (0/0/43) | −4.19 ± 0.15 | +28.3 | **0.518** | **23.2** |
| student8 | 8 (29–32,34–37) | 0.342 → 0.115 / 0.240 → 0.047 | <u>−3.33 ± 0.15</u> (0/0/43) | <u>−3.51 ± 0.16</u> | <u>+22.7</u> | <u>0.685</u> | <u>17.5</u> |

student16 frames (easy_lake, hydraulic_press_can_crush vs rt2): chunk 0 is fine (teacher-committed first chunk), then every chunk loses texture (water, machine surfaces turn to smeared blobs) and the loss compounds through its own KV; quality 1–5 on all clips (easy_lake best at 5). The 82 % latent-MSE reduction under teacher forcing does not transfer to free running: a 0.17 relative error per step on the x0 estimate is far above what the autoregressive loop tolerates (the untrained skip-16 was −4.33, §44 — KD moved it by 0.3). student8 (frames easy_lake, seesaw_balance vs rt2): the scene layout survives (lake, playground, sandbag) but every surface turns painterly/smeared from chunk 1 and degrades further along the clip; best clip easy_lake 7, most clips 3; 0 wins on 43. Untrained skip-4/10 were −1.42/−4.08 (§44), so KD on 8 blocks lands where an untrained 8-skip would (≈ −3): 3240 steps of teacher-forced x0-MSE reduce the per-step error 3× but the free-running loop amplifies what remains. Conclusion §48: depth pruning + KD LoRA closed at this budget; the student is 1.1–1.5× faster than rt2 (17.5 / 23.2 fps) at −3.3 / −4.0, far below adapt25 (§47) which is 15.3 fps at −0.05 vs teacher. A usable pruned student would need on-policy (own-context) training with a distribution loss, i.e. the stage-4 recipe, not regression.

## §49 Stage 2b — cloud corrector LoRA at node 750 (10/16, bench-43, judge Qwen3-VL-32B): closed
Setup: rank-128 LoRA on the full 14B, active ONLY during the node-750 (σ 0.625) step of the cloud; input = (1−σ)·xd_up + σ·eps with xd_up = the 576 draft bicubic-upsampled to the 704 grid (training drafts built the Correction-Training way from the teacher chunk: downsample → re-noise σ 0.833 → one 576 step → upsample, kd_data/corr_576), target = the teacher's final chunk, rel-MSE; context = teacher chunks committed with the stock weights. 3240 steps, val rel-MSE 0.095 (stock one-step) → 0.064. Runtime: lr576_corr1hyb = plain 576 device draft (shown natively) + cloud stock pipeline with the LoRA gated on at node 750 + feedback (same as lr576c1hyb except the LoRA).

| stream | Δq vs lr576c1hyb (stock cloud) ↑ | Δq vs lr576c1_cloud | Δq vs teacher | Δq vs lr576_dev | Δdrift ↓ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| lr576_corr1hyb cloud (corrected) | −2.63 ± 0.23 (0/5/38) | −2.60 ± 0.24 (0/6/37) | −2.84 ± 0.25 (1/4/38) | | +19.4 |
| lr576_corr1hyb device (own draft, corrected KV) | +0.05 ± 0.11 (8/27/8) | | | +0.00 ± 0.12 (7/28/8) | +4.3 |

Frames (easy_lake, seesaw_balance; rows teacher / lr576c1hyb cloud / corr1 cloud / corr1 device): the corrected stream carries the §46 mosaic/grid texture from chunk 1 on (lake water and mountains become a dotted lattice, the sandbag a mesh) and loses detail; the stock one-step from the same input (row 2) is clean. The device row is clean (native 576 decode) and unchanged vs the stock-cloud feedback (+0.05). Reading: a 33 % latent-MSE gain at the lowest-noise node is the conditional-mean solution: the LoRA learns to stay close to its input (the target's layout = the input's layout), so the bicubic-resampled latent structure leaks through where the stock step regenerates it, and the rest blurs. Same failure class as §46/§46b and the 9/26 low-noise MSE LoRAs: regression at σ ≤ 0.63 cannot be the cloud corrector; a corrector needs a distribution loss (full-schedule diffusion loss as in Correction Training, or adversarial) = stage-4 scale. Closed.

### §47b Adaptive 2/3-step at 832x480 (exp 2, 10/16; a832 12-prompt bench, same 704-trained step-1 head and thresholds)
| arm (832, n=12) | Δq vs rt2 ↑ | Δq vs solo3 ↑ | Δq vs teacher ↑ | steady s/chunk ↓ | fps median / mean ↑ | extended chunks |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| rt2 (2-step) | 0 | | +0.17 ± 0.24 (5/4/3) | **1.228** | **9.8 / 9.8** | 0 / 120 |
| solo3 (3-step) | | 0 | +0.08 ± 0.23 (4/5/3) | 1.740 | 6.9 / 6.9 | 120 / 120 |
| adapt25 | −0.17 ± 0.11 (0/10/2) | −0.08 ± 0.08 (0/11/1) | +0.00 ± 0.21 (3/6/3) | <u>1.235</u> | <u>9.7 / 9.9</u> | 19 / 120 (16 %) |
| adapt50 | −0.17 ± 0.11 (0/10/2) | −0.08 ± 0.08 (0/11/1) | +0.00 ± 0.21 (3/6/3) | 1.358 | 8.9 / 8.6 | 41 / 120 (34 %) |

Per clip adapt25 = adapt50 on all 12 (judge granularity). The two losses (billiard_break 8→7, domino_falling 8→7) are clips with 1 and 0 head-flagged extensions: only the forced 3-step chunks 0–2 differ, i.e. a different trajectory, not a head decision. Reading: at 832 on this prompt set the 2-step draft is already at teacher level (+0.17 ± 0.24), so a 3rd step has nothing to add; the thresholds transfer in count (16 % / 34 % extended) but the gain of §47 is specific to 704 where the 2-step is −0.62. Exp 2 closed: adaptive steps stay a 704 (realtime-mode) tool.

### §47c Adaptive draft + cloud feedback (exp 1, 10/16, bench-43, 704x384)
Device = adapt25 draft (step-1 head, 3rd node on flagged chunks); cloud = one step at node 750 + KV feedback (as rt2c1hyb). adapt25c1hyb: every chunk sent. adapt25g1hyb: `--gate adapt` = only the chunks the head extended are sent (chunks 0–2 always), the cloud commits the rest as drafted.

| arm | cloud stream Δq vs teacher ↑ | cloud vs rt2c1hyb_cloud | device Δq vs teacher ↑ | device vs adapt25 | device fps shown ↑ | lag median ↓ | applied | cloud refined |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| rt2c1hyb (§40) | −0.07 | 0 | +0.02 vs rt2… (−0.60) | | **11.1** | 1.06 s | 473/559 | 559/559 |
| adapt25 solo (§47) | | | −0.05 ± 0.10 | 0 | <u>15.3</u> (mean) | – | – | – |
| adapt25c1hyb | **+0.07 ± 0.11** (9/28/6), drift −8.7 ± 3.9 | **+0.14 ± 0.10** (12/26/5) | **+0.00 ± 0.12** (9/26/8), drift −4.1 | +0.05 ± 0.10 (7/33/3) | 11.1 | 1.08 s | 473/559 | 559/559 |
| adapt25g1hyb | −0.07 ± 0.10 (6/27/10) | +0.00 ± 0.09 (8/27/8) | <u>−0.05 ± 0.14</u> (8/27/8) | +0.00 ± 0.10 (6/33/4) | **15.6** | 0.92 s | 188/201 | 201/559 (36 %) |

Flips adapt25c1hyb_cloud vs teacher: wins easy_urban_rain 4→6, hydraulic_press_can_crush(_10) 7→8, syringe_hydraulic, tea_dilution, gas_pump_nozzle_trigger 7→8, lightbulb_and_switch_opposite, sprinkler_valve_control_opposite, easy_stonehenge_sunset 6→7 (9); losses railroad_crossing_gates 7→5, easy_greatwall 9→8, newtons_cradle, tire_inflate, inflating_balloon_09, string_pull_lamp_opposite 8→7 (6). Frames (easy_urban_rain: teacher drifts into dark wall close-ups, adapt25 and both hybrid rows keep the alley with graffiti containers; string_pull_lamp: all arms leave the lamp scene, hybrid 8→7 ends on a dark pulled cord): the hybrid rows are the adapt25 trajectory with the cloud's texture, cloud and device rows match.
Reading: (i) the first cloud stream above the teacher (+0.07, drift −8.7) = adapt25 draft + one cloud step + feedback, device shown at teacher parity (+0.00) at 11.1 fps; (ii) sending only the head-flagged 36 % of chunks keeps the device at 15.6 fps shown with adapt25 quality (−0.05) but the feedback on those chunks adds nothing (+0.00 vs adapt25, cloud −0.07 vs teacher) — the quality gain of feedback comes from the unflagged chunks too, i.e. the step-1 head ranks chunks for the 3rd step well but not for "needs the cloud". Recommendation at 704: realtime = adapt25 solo or adapt25g1hyb (15.3–15.6 fps, −0.05); best quality = adapt25c1hyb (11.1 fps shown, teacher parity on device, cloud stream +0.07).

## §50 Positioning vs Speculative Forcing (arXiv 2610.22347, posted 10/15): token-wise reimplementation on the 14B + their metrics on our clips (10/16, IN PROGRESS — numbers below are the ones in hand at 16:45 CDT; the 832 uniform4/uniform2/oracle arms, the quiet timing pass and the final table follow)
Context: paper/meeting_1012/positioning_vs_specforcing.md. Their method = token-wise early commitment inside one frozen few-step generator (learned head on the last block's hidden predicts rel_k(i) = gap to the generator's own full-step output; ε 0.24 for world models; 1.3B LingBot only; H100 DiT-only fps 18.1 → 24.5 = 1.35× at 2.55 steps/token; VBench 6-dim quality 82.65 vs 82.62, lower MUSIQ drift; their Table 5: chunk-wise acceptance 82.54 / drift 3.21 < token-wise; oracle verifier 82.61 = 4-step → the ceiling is the generator's own full-step output by construction).
Reimplementation (specwm/spec_early.py --mode early --eps 0.24, our 10/6 token-wise code = same design: LN + 3 conv head on the last block's hidden, 9×9 spatial + temporal smoothing, accepted tokens frozen with their KV, active-token-only forwards (bucket 128), clean t=0 commit; head = head14b_nob43 (trained without bench-43 scenes); 14B causal-fast FP8, 832×480, bench-43 × seeds 1234/4321 = 86 clips; out specwm_out/bench43_sf/<task>/sf24_s<seed>; judge data/vlm_bench43_sf.json):
- mean denoising steps per token 2.52 (median 2.56) — the same as their 2.55 on the 1.3B.
- Judge (paired by prompt+seed with the ARCHIVED bench-43 teacher clips, different samples): sf24 7.56 / drift 45.6 vs teacher 7.51 / 51.0 → **+0.05 ± 0.06 (13/64/9), drift −5.4 ± 3.1** = teacher quality kept (as they claim), the same as our chunk-wise cw07_sep (+0.03 ± 0.05, §12). ours832 2-step −0.30. (Same-run pairing vs the regenerated uniform4 pending.)
- Wall-clock: the bench run shared GPU 6 with the VBench/DA3 metric jobs → contaminated (gen 36 s median; first uncontaminated clip 23–26 s vs uniform4 31 s / uniform2 18 s on 10/6) → quiet timing pass queued (bench43_sf/quiet, 3 tasks × 2 seeds, arms sf24/uniform4/uniform2). Expected ≈ 1.3× over 4-step, slower than 2-step (10/6 numbers; their D.2 max-k* bound).
Their metrics on our clips (scripts/vbench6.py in tools/vbench_env: VBench 6 dims without temporal flickering + leaderboard normalization (their Table 3 4-step row reproduces 82.6) + MUSIQ Drift (std of 32-frame segment means); scripts/rpe_da3.py: Depth Anything 3 nested-giant-large poses, per-segment Sim(3) Umeyama (5 segments), consecutive-frame relative rotation error in degrees (Trans ratio is noise on our slow paths: 0.7–1.5 both for right and wrong trajectories; Rot discriminates: easy_lake teacher 0.83° vs 7.6° against a wrong trajectory); data/vbench6.json, data/rpe_da3.json; report scripts/metrics_report.py):

Readings: (1) VBench Q ranks the arms like the judge (rt2 < adapt25 < solo3 ≈ teacher < cloud feedback); adapt25c1hyb is above the teacher on VBench Q too (+0.39, 19 wins / 15 losses). (2) Imaging (MUSIQ) is 0.02–0.04 below the teacher for every 2/3-step arm → the gain is consistency/aesthetic, sharpness still favours 4 steps. (3) MUSIQ drift (sharpness variation over time) is lowest for the teacher and +0.4–0.7 for our arms — a different quantity from the judge drift (scene departure), report both with definitions. (4) RPE Rot: all our arms tie the teacher (|Δ| ≤ 0.05°); ABot +0.8° (14/14 worse) = the keyboard-conversion loss made visible. (5) 832 subset (12): rt2 +0.23 / adapt25 +0.05 / solo3 +0.33 vs a832_teacher, all within noise. (6) MUSIQ depends on resolution → pair only with the same-resolution teacher.

## §51 Stage 4 — 14B 2-step DMD self-forcing distillation (specwm/dmd14.py; design paper/meeting_1012/stage4_design.md; 10/16 10:18 CDT → ; runs R1 lr 2e-6 r64 GPUs 4+5, R2 lr 5e-6 r64 GPUs 0+1, R0 lr 1e-6 r32 GPUs 2+3; critic ratio 2, ~130 s/step; eval = solo rt2 --student on the 12-task subset at 704 every 100 steps, judge paired vs rt2 / teacher / adapt25 / solo3)
| run | step 100: Δq vs rt2 (w/t/l) | vs teacher | step 200: Δq vs rt2 (w/t/l) | vs teacher | Δ drift vs rt2 (200) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R0 (1e-6, r32) | **+0.00 ± 0.25** (3/7/2) | −0.25 ± 0.30 | **−0.25 ± 0.22** (2/5/5) | −0.50 ± 0.34 | +2.1 ± 7.0 |
| R1 (2e-6, r64) | −0.42 ± 0.23 (1/6/5) | −0.67 ± 0.40 | <u>−0.33 ± 0.22</u> (1/7/4) | −0.58 ± 0.40 | +0.8 ± 6.3 |
| R2 (5e-6, r64) | <u>−0.33 ± 0.31</u> (3/4/5) | −0.58 ± 0.34 | −0.83 ± 0.27 (1/3/8) | −1.08 ± 0.31 | +7.5 ± 6.1 |
Readings: (1) no run improves on rt2 at any checkpoint; the larger the lr the larger the loss (R2 −0.83 at 200), the smallest lr only delays it (R0 +0.00 → −0.25). (2) Not a §34-style collapse: frames stay sharp (R1 s100 grids: newtons_cradle / easy_lake — losses are content/trajectory: signage/glass-door textures invented at the end of the cradle orbit, the lake tree lost; rt2 wanders similarly but keeps the tree). (3) DMD loss 0.83 → 0.55–0.63 by step 240–260 with large step-to-step noise; |grad|/|x| 1.2–1.7; no sign of the generator moving toward the real score in judge terms. (4) Gates (design §4): step 100 R1/R2 fail (−0.42/−0.33 < −0.15), R0 passes trivially; step 200 all three fail (≥ rt2 ± 0.10). Actions: R1 closed 19:05 CDT and R2 closed 19:50 CDT 10/16 (GPUs 4,5,0,1 reassigned to the frozen-baseline generation); R0 kept to its step-300 eval (final gate: > rt2, wins > losses) — if it fails, the DMD line closes and R3 (feature-space adversarial, 1 GPU) is the next generator-side experiment. Eval fps numbers in eval/results.txt are contaminated (GPU 6 shared with metric jobs) — the LoRA student runs at rt2 speed (§48).
Sanity decodes at step 0 (sanity_{gen,real,fake}.mp4) were consistent (bid conditioning OK), so the failure is the objective/optimisation, not the conditioning.

Wall-clock: the bench run shared GPU 6 with metric jobs (sf24 2.70 vs uniform4 2.89 s/chunk = 1.07×, uniform2 1.65 = 1.75× under the same contamination; sparse steps run SDPA, dense steps Sage) → the quiet pass (bench43_sf/quiet) gives the paper numbers.

Next generator-side option per the design: R3 = feature-space adversarial (frozen 14B layer-20 features, hinge discriminator, no bid model, 1 GPU) — not started (GPUs reassigned to the frozen-baseline generation 10/16–17).
§50 addendum 2 (10/17 00:00 CDT) — quiet timing + oracle ceiling (832×480, 14B FP8):
| arm | mean steps ↓ | steady s/chunk ↓ (quiet, 3 tasks × 2 seeds) | fps ↑ | speed vs 4-step | judge Δq vs uniform4 (b/e/w), n=86 | VBench ΔQ (b/e/w) | MUSIQ drift Δ ↓ | RPE Rot Δ ↓ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| uniform 4-step | 4.00 | 2.883 | 4.16 | 1.00× | – | – | – | – |
| SF learned head ε 0.24 (sf24) | 2.46 | <u>1.893</u> | <u>6.34</u> | <u>1.52×</u> | **−0.05 ± 0.06** (9/65/12) | **−0.10 ± 0.17** | **+0.23** | **−0.02** |
| SF ORACLE verifier ε 0.24 (true gap to the same-noise 4-step output; reference pass not timed) | 2.31 | (4.03 incl. reference) | – | – | <u>−0.20 ± 0.06</u> (7/55/24), drift +6.1 | <u>−0.37 ± 0.18</u> | +0.35 | +0.02 |

Readings: (1) quiet wall-clock: token-wise SF gives 1.52× over 4-step on the 14B (their 1.3B: 1.35×), still slower than uniform 2-step (1.75×) and than our chunk-wise cw07_pc1 (1.74–1.77×, §12) at the same teacher quality. (2) The ORACLE verifier is WORSE than the learned head (−0.20 vs −0.05 judge; −0.37 vs −0.10 VBench): a perfect per-token convergence test accepts more tokens (2.31 steps) but frozen tokens keep step-k K/V while their neighbours keep denoising, and the gap is measured against the dense 4-step trajectory the sparse rollout no longer follows (same mechanism as 10/8: "the loss is stale K/V of frozen tokens, not the stop rule"). So the token-wise family is bounded by the generator's own 4-step output from above, and verifier accuracy is not what limits it — a quantitative form of the "cannot fix the video globally" argument (user, 10/16). (3) Their Table 5 oracle (= 4-step quality on the 1.3B) does not transfer to the 14B.

## §52 Divergence injection: does a draft follow an injected 14B context or return to its own path? (10/20, GPU 5, 832×480, stream tau2_s0, 5 scenes × 10 seeds; specwm/inject_probe.py + specwm/inject_analysis.py; fig figs/f21_inject.png, data data/f21_inject.json, outputs specwm_out/inject/)

Question (user): at the point where the 1.3B diverges from the 14B, inject the 14B's latents into the 1.3B's context — does the 1.3B then continue like the 14B or return to what it would have generated anyway? Control: the same with the 14B 2-step (= what the device/cloud hybrid does when it re-commits a cloud chunk).

Setup. T = archived 14B 4-step clips of f20 (paths2/K0_uniform4P_fx, act_spec2 code path; rsynced back from lamarr; used ONLY as injection source + reference envelope). G = 1.3B 4-step or 14B 2-step (first 2 of the 4 nodes), generated in ONE new solo code path (binding_v2 + ring KV window 18 = sink 6 + 4 chunks, dense clean commit, act_spec2's noise convention so the injected 14B-2step shares T's noise). Arms per (scene, seed): alone; once@k (KV reset, T's chunks 0..k re-committed through G's own clean pass, G continues k+1..12) for k ∈ {1, 3, 6, 9}; every (teacher forcing: every chunk denoised in T's context, T's chunk committed); self@3 (G's OWN chunks 0..3 re-committed = numerical floor). 13 chunks × 3 latent frames. Measures: d_T / d_G = same-seed latent distance of the arm's chunk to T's / to G-alone's chunk, in units of the mean T seed-to-seed distance at that chunk (1 = two independent 14B seeds); follow = mean over chunks after k of d_G/(d_T+d_G); envelope ratio = |arm seed-mean path − T seed-mean path| / 90th-pct T seed spread in the f20 PCA space (fit on T + G-alone), < 1 = inside the 14B seed envelope.

k* (first chunk whose G mean path leaves the T envelope): the 1.3B is outside from chunk 0 in ALL five scenes (latent-space ratio at c = 0: jar 1.35, castle 1.28, lake 2.5, stonehenge 2.1, pouring 2.0; PCA 3.4 / 2.3 / 4.6 / 8.5 / 7.4) → k* = 0 everywhere (with the new G-alone runs: jar 3 in PCA, 0 in latent space; the other four 0 / 0). There is no "point of divergence" to inject at — the 1.3B's first chunk is already a different distribution — so the grid k ∈ {1, 3, 6, 9} covers the clip (k = 1 = early anchor). The 14B 2-step (archived K0_uniform2P) never leaves the envelope (latent ratio 0.35–0.53 at every chunk; PCA < 1 except stonehenge chunks 3/5) → k* = none for the control, as expected for a same-model schedule change.

Floors / chaos: self@3 (re-commit of G's own context) is exact for chunks 0–3, then differs from G-alone by rel-L2 0.04 (1.3B) / 0.14 (14B FP8 + sage, non-deterministic) at k+1 and 0.4 / 0.5 by k+9: same-seed identity decays within ~4 chunks on its own, so d_T / d_G are read only for the first 4 chunks after the injection; the long range is read from the envelope ratio of the seed-mean paths. Follow floor (self@3) = 0.09–0.23 (1.3B), 0.31–0.33 (14B); teacher-forced ceiling (every) = 0.62–0.71 (1.3B) / 0.74–0.76 (14B) — even with the exact 14B context, the next 1.3B chunk is 0.5–0.65 of an independent-seed distance away from T's same-noise chunk (the 14B 2-step: 0.37–0.55).

### Frames (sandcastle s1234, 1 fps contact sheets specwm_out/inject/{1p3b_4step,14b_2step}/sheet_sandcastle_wave_impact_s1234/sheet.png + mp4 per arm; looked at both)
T (14B 4-step): wave hits the castle (0–2 s), pan to wet sand (3 s), a man in a green jacket crouches in the water (4–7 s), then a coast view with foam and a hill (8–10 s). 1.3B alone: wave, then a sandy mound with trees and hills — a different scene from 3 s on. 1.3B once@1 / once@3: T's chunks, then wet sand with debris, then a lake-and-mountains landscape and a bush (its own kind of content; the man never appears; the alone's sandy mound never appears either). 1.3B once@6 (man injected): the man persists for the rest of the clip (walking in the water, 6–10 s) while T itself cuts to the coast view at 8 s — the injected scene content is kept but T's later trajectory is not followed. once@9: coast with foam ≈ T. every: tracks T throughout (man, coast). 14B 2-step control: alone drifts to a boat-like sand structure in a lake; once@1/@3 keep "a person in the water" (a woman in a blue jacket with a backpack / a boy in green with a red bag — identity and action differ from T within 2–3 chunks) and then a wave; once@6 keeps T's man for ~2 s, then a man with a backpack walking; once@9 and every ≈ T.

### Reading
1. Neither generator returns to its own alone path: the distance to G-alone's mean path after an injection is 0.4–5.4 T-spreads (1.3B) / 0.2–0.6 (14B 2-step) and the same-seed d_G is ≈ 1 (as far from the alone chunk as an independent seed) from k+1 on. The alone content (sandy mound / sand boat) is never regenerated. "Own prior wins" is false in the sense of reverting to the alone clip.
2. But the 1.3B does not follow the 14B's trajectory either: right after the injection its next chunk is 0.76 / 0.66 / 0.82 / 0.90 / 0.81 (once@1, jar / castle / lake / stonehenge / pouring) of an independent-seed distance from T's same-seed chunk (14B 2-step: 0.43 / 0.44 / 0.41), i.e. it continues from the injected content with its own dynamics; the follow ratio sits at 0.44–0.64 against a ceiling of 0.62–0.71 (teacher-forced) and a floor of 0.09–0.23 (self re-commit). Even with the exact 14B context in every chunk the 1.3B's next chunk is 0.5–0.65 independent-seed distances off (14B 2-step: 0.37–0.55).
3. Long range (envelope of the seed-mean paths): an early injection (k = 1 / 3) brings the 1.3B from 1.4–7.5 T-spreads to 0.3–1.2 right after it, and it re-diverges within 2–6 chunks in castle / pouring (back above 1 by chunk 7–9; castle once@1 is never inside) while in jar / lake / stonehenge it stays near the 14B envelope (0.2–0.9) for the rest of the clip; later injections (k = 6 / 9) keep it inside (0.05–0.9) for the 3–6 remaining chunks. The 14B 2-step goes to 0.01–0.08 (deep inside) after k ≥ 3 and stays there — the same-model hybrid is a different regime from a cross-model draft.
4. Mechanism consistent with the KV window: the ring keeps the sink (chunks 0–1) + the last 4 chunks, so an injected context is attended for ≤ 4 chunks; after that only the content the draft itself generated from it carries the 14B's scene. The frames show exactly that: scene content (the man) is kept, scene evolution (T's cut to the coast) is not.
Answer to the user's question: the 1.3B follows the injected 14B context in CONTENT (it does not snap back to its own clip; distance to its alone path ≈ an independent seed from the first chunk on) but not in TRAJECTORY — it immediately continues with its own dynamics (same-seed distance to the 14B 0.66–0.90 of an independent seed at k+1 vs 0.41–0.44 for the 14B 2-step), and its seed-mean path leaves the 14B envelope again 2–6 chunks later in 2 of 5 scenes. Re-anchoring buys scene content for the window length, not a 14B-like rollout; for the hybrid (14B 2-step ← 4-step) the same injection puts the device stream deep inside the 14B envelope and keeps it there.

### Caveats
Compile / code path: all G arms (alone, self, once, every) of one generator ran in one process and one code path (ring_kv, dynamic compile, sage); T is the archived act_spec2 arm (ring_frame) used only as the injection source and the envelope reference. The 14B 2-step is FP8 + sage and non-deterministic: self@3 differs from alone by 0.14 rel-L2 at k+1 (1.3B 0.04), so its floor is higher (follow 0.25–0.33). Same-seed distances are chaotic (self@3 reaches 0.4–0.5 by k+9), hence the 4-chunk window for d_T / d_G and the mean-path envelope for the long range; the PCA envelope at chunks 0–2 is tiny (seeds have not spread), so ratios there are inflated. k* is degenerate (the 1.3B is outside from chunk 0), so the injection points are a fixed grid, not "the divergence point". Re-commit = KV reset + sequential clean commits (= the hybrid's recommit when every chunk is corrected in order; the hybrid's single-slot in-place recommit attends to newer slots once the ring has wrapped — not tested). Prompts for jar / castle are empty (as in f20). Control is partial (3 of 5 scenes, lake n = 5). GPU time: 1.3B 45 min, 14B 2-step 48 + 15 min (stopped), ≈ 1.8 GPU-h on GPU 5 shared with two scorer loops (timings in run.json are not quiet).
